S1.3 Standing conventions 

(C1) Determinism modulo coins. A single coin sequence ω jointly covers the sampling randomness of P and of RE ; given ω, both are deterministic functions of their inputs. All two-run comparisons (Thm. S3.25, Prop. S5.10, Prop. S8.20) couple ω, the user’s typed stream (A6), and (in the federated setting) the exogenous federation-log prefix (A8; local suffixes derived equal, Def. S4.2), identically across the compared runs. (C2) Probability space and adversary. All federated-protocol randomness lives on the filtration (Fs) gener-ated by logged protocol events and display events; the adversary’s strategy (presentation, submission, stopping, self-dispute) is predictable with respect to it. The construction, and the two structural constraints on adaptivity it implies, are given once in §S8.1 and used everywhere. (C3) Log order. The federation maintains a single linearizable, append-only log of protocol events: a TCB log service (a trusted sequencer or a consensus layer) supplies the total order, and its history is materialized as a Certificate-Transparency-style Merkle tree for tamper-evident audit — the tree

Figure S1: Dependency structure (edges denote “is used in the proof of”). (a) The single-user theorems rest on the trust axioms and harness rules alone (the channel-analysis lemmas use R1–R11; the structural lemmas use R12–R14); the coupling and Hoeffding lemmas are assumption-free. A7–A8 enter only Proposition S5.10, which supplies their delegated extension. (b) Dotted boxes import results proved in panel (a) and Appendix S8. The dashed edge is the only role of the accountability calibration: it grounds the constant cacc in the budget ledger and never enters any safety theorem. The pathwise floor, Thm. S6.2(i), depends on INV-3 alone, visible here as its sole incoming edge; the expected-cap clauses (ii)–(iii) additionally read the reference demand model’s masses, and the tool-spread comparison dtool ≤ ctool d is quarantined in Cor. S6.3 (A12), consumed by no other result. (TCB = trusted computing base; defined in §S1.2, inventoried in §S2.5.) 

42 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

authenticates the history; the sequencing service, assumed correct as TCB, provides the linearizability. It supplies the total order on decisions, demand events, and verdicts that Rules R12–R14 invoke. “The federation log,” “the log,” and “log order” all refer to this object and the order it induces. The hash-chained audit log that materializes the display transcript (Definition S3.8) is a distinct, per-deployment TCB component and is never called “the log.” (C4) Epochs, leases, rounds. Three multiplicities the development keeps distinct. (a) Epochs. An epoch is a fixed span of the federation log (a fixed number of sequence numbers or a fixed calendar period), common to all items and identities, readable off the log (TCB); every renewable per-epoch quantity (ρsub , Emax , notification deduplication, the accountability allowances of Rule R13(v), (vii)) is indexed to it. (b) Leases. A lease is the deployment-selected local scope lifetime of the requirements-level option O3 (O3; not part of the formal semantics); Elease = O(1) counts lease periods per (user, item) in the extension-level accounting of O3(e). (c) Rounds. A re-verification round re-presents a community-confirmed item to inspectors with no prior decision on its bytes; Erv counts rounds. 

(d) Permanence and boundaries. Decision freshness (Def. S4.5) and verdict capacity (Rule R13(iii)) are permanent and attach to none of the three: they never renew. Promotion weight and banked credits persist across epoch boundaries until the item retires. Finalisation windows are contained within epochs: a window that would cross a boundary closes at the boundary, banking its accrued endorsements to the item’s next entry exactly as a dormant-interval decision would (Def. S4.6); a boundary never finalises a promotion early and never discards honest effort. A boundary-spanning dispute counts toward Rule R14(i) in the epoch in which it opened. (C5) Byte identity. Items, freshness, disputes, and panels are keyed to exact byte strings. Paraphrase variants are distinct items (consequences: S1). (C6) Confirmations. A confirmation is a user approval—an accepting gate decision; it is durable (its fresh endorsed copy is written even when another review aborts the enclosing invocation, Rule R2) and is counted once per (value, tool) pair per gate transaction (Definition S2.5). Rejecting deci-sions, abandoned dialogs, batch-element page approvals, and repeated within-transaction reviews of an already-accepted pair are attention cost, counted separately (N18). Required , voluntary , and 

redundant confirmations are defined in §S6.1: the confirmation cap (Thm. S6.2) counts required pair-confirmations, the ledger (Lem. S6.7) required and voluntary; redundant confirmations are protocol-inert (Rule R13(i)) and excluded from both. Here, required means needed for the user’s intended use, not an obligation to accept. (C7) Store idealization. The store is idealized as unbounded: no operation’s defined behaviour depends on aggregate store volume. A deployment enforcing quotas must apply them to the untrusted tier by silent eviction, never by an error visible to P (otherwise store exhaustion reopens exactly the channel Rule R7 closes; S7). (C8) Constants vs. schedules. N, k, m, W, D pool , T win , T exp , Emax , ρ sub , r max , c ceil , c batch , c cand , c p

are fixed TCB configuration at any deployment; Appendix S6 additionally studies schedules 

N (M ), k (M ), . . . along a growing population, and every scheduled claim is confined to the cor-ridor stated there. All caps and multiplicity constants are positive integers where integral; in particular 

Emax ≥ 1 and ctool ≥ 1 (used by Lemma S8.18). (C9) Environment scheduling. An interpreter step has a fixed slot structure: first the channel slots deliver at most one typed event (W-T YPED ) and at most one paste event (W-P ASTE ), in that order; then P

emits exactly one operation, whose environment interaction (tool returns, reader outputs, retriever selection, gate decisions) occurs synchronously within that operation’s atomic transition. Two-run comparisons (Theorem S3.25, Proposition S5.10) couple only this slot skeleton and the typed stream (A6): paste presence and content are uncoupled and may differ arbitrarily across the compared attacks, as may every tool output and reader output. Channel-slot transitions acknowledge the channel, never 

P : nothing is appended to P ’s reply stream by W-T YPED or W-P ASTE (Figure S3, convention (vi)).  

> 43 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Two-run comparisons are always taken at a common horizon : the compared runs consist of the same number of interpreter slots, and transcript equality means equality of the two full transcripts at that horizon. 

S1.4 Named invariants and structural properties 

The following six named facts are cited constantly and are collected here; together they are the structural core of the design. They are heterogeneous in kind, and each entry declares its kind: rule property (a direct consequence of the cited rule and definition clauses); conditional inductive invariant (preserved by every transition, under the named first-party exceptions); protocol property (a fact of the counting and pacing rules); bound metaproperty (a property of the bounds of the cited theorems, holding under those theorems’ hypotheses). None is an axiom: each is established at the location cited, and nothing beyond the cited hypotheses is assumed. Invariants INV-1–INV-6 are stated in the vocabulary of the federated protocol, defined in Appendix S4. 

Invariant INV-1 (Byte-permanence of freshness) . An attested identity’s first logged decision on a byte string — its first decision taken while the bytes have a shared-tier presence (Definition S4.5) — is its only weight-bearing inspection of those bytes, ever: every later accepting decision by that identity (at any tool, in any pipeline entry, in any epoch, in any later shared-tier presence of the same bytes) carries no promotion weight and no verdict capacity. Freshness is decidable from the federation log (convention (C3)) and never renews; personal decisions outside any shared-tier presence are not logged and are outside this guarantee (Definition S4.5). Kind: rule property. Established by Definition S4.5 and the counting rule Definition S4.6. (Byte-keyed: paraphrase variants are distinct items, (C5), S1.) 

Invariant INV-2 (Rejection asymmetry) . A rejecting decision can freeze promotion, corroborate an open dispute, or trigger post-promotion review; it can never promote and never vindicate. A repeat acceptance carries no promotion weight and no verdict (INV-1). Hence re-exposure of bytes to a previously logged-decided inspector can only add rejection opportunities; exposure to a log-fresh inspector adds both a rejection opportunity and a possible weight-bearing acceptance, priced by the hypotheses of Theorem S5.13. Kind: rule property. Established by Rule R13(i)–(iii) with Definition S4.5. 

Invariant INV-3 (Single-approval cap, per (user, value, tool)) . No user is ever required to confirm a given 

(value, tool) pair more than once over the deployment’s lifetime: a user’s first accepting review of value v

at tool t — at any gate transaction, including one whose enclosing invocation aborts (Rule R2) — durably writes the local C copy with t ∈ scope , which covers all their later use of v at t and survives every third-party protocol event; and within one transaction, confirmations are counted once per pair (Definition S2.5). A first use of the same bytes at a different tool t′ is a distinct required confirmation, of the pair (v, t ′), itself once ever; the pairs per item are bounded by A12 in the budget model. The disclosed exception within the formal semantics is first-party: the owner’s own agent’s ungated evict (N13); a deployment adopting a conforming instantiation of the requirements-level Option O3 additionally admits lease expiry, an extension outside this document’s semantics (O3). Kind: conditional inductive invariant (exception: first-party evict, N13; extension-level: conforming-O3 lease expiry, O3). Established by Lemma S6.1 (from Rules R2, R4, A1–A2, and Lemma S5.8); reasoning pairs covered per the reasoning-coverage clause of Rule R13(i); consumed by 

Theorem S6.2 and Lemma S6.7(i). 

Invariant INV-4 (Banking safety) . A banked credit (a first decision logged during dormancy or during a freeze with no open entry) raises only the starting weight of the item’s next pipeline entry. Nothing finalises promotion except an open entry’s window end, so banking never bypasses a window’s rejection opportunity and never accelerates finalisation past a window boundary; and each banked decision remains its identity’s single fresh inspection of the bytes, so successive entries’ weight-bearing sets stay identity-disjoint (Lemma S5.3). Banked credits expire if the item retires first. Every banked decision is logged after its attempt’s anchor (Definition S5.1), so banking never lets a credited decision precede the conditioning point of Theorem S5.13. Kind: protocol property. Established by Definition S4.6 with Rule R13(iv).  

> 44 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Invariant INV-5 (Verdict non-renewal) . An identity casts at most one verdict on a given dispute instance, for the instance’s entire life, across any number of epoch boundaries. Verdict capacity is consumed only by cast verdicts (abandonment consumes nothing) and, like freshness, never renews. Kind: rule property. 

Established by Rule R13(iii). 

Invariant INV-6 (Reach-freeness) . The federated safety bounds depend only on protocol constants (N, k, m, D pool ) and the adversary’s resources ( f, f s, W adv ), never on the number of honest identities to whom a poisoned item is presented: the same stated upper bound applies at every reach. (No mono-tonicity in reach is claimed: presenting the bytes to log-fresh inspectors creates both rejection opportunities and possible weight-bearing acceptances, INV-2; what is proved is that the bound does not degrade with reach.) Reach-freeness is relative to the stated parameters: the bounds’ form is reach-independent, while the behavioural constants qpre , q pan are weakest-target infima over all cohorts the adversary can reach (theo-rem S5.19), so widening reach can lower the applicable constants without changing the bound’s form. Kind: bound metaproperty. The property is Definition S4.8; it is a property of the stated bounds of Theorems S5.11 and S5.13, holding under their hypotheses, not a property of reachable configurations; contrast with the reach-dependent no-dispute baseline, Theorem S8.9. 

S2 Formal Specification of the Memory Interface 

PerMIT places no restriction on which tools the Protected LLM may invoke or on how it reasons; the harness 

(the fixed interpreter code of Figures S2–S3 together with the display and logging paths) bounds two things only: what P can observe , and what may cross a tool boundary. The bounded surface is the memory interface: the P -facing operation surface 

ΣP = Σ ∪ { tool (¯ a) : tool ∈ Tools sens ∪ Tools data } ∪ { retrieve },

Σ = {write P , recall , fill , crystallize , display , confirm , evict },

(write , where it appears in prose, abbreviates the sole P -emitted write operation write P .) together with one interpreter-internal derived form ( map ). Every completeness claim below (Lemmas S3.17, S3.18) enumerates over ΣP , which coincides arm-for-arm with the mechanized operation type ( POp , §S9). Because this surface is finite, its interface transition schema is specified clause-complete at the interface alphabet : every operation of ΣP and channel event has its store effect, reply, transcript events, and sanitized-history events fixed by Figures S2–S3 with these rules, with totality per Rule R10(b); the full run state these transitions carry is fixed by Definition S2.6. What the figures deliberately do not formalise is P ’s internal computation (a strategy, §S2.1) and the UI-side rendering internals of Rule R8; the channel analysis of Appendix S3 needs only the interface schema, and is verified clause by clause against it, with the totality clause of Rule R10 ensuring that no invocation has undefined or distinguishing behaviour. (Model lineage and design rationale: N1.) 

S2.1 Configurations, labels, and roles Definition S2.1 (Trust lattice and labels) . The integrity lattice has two levels, U ⊏ E. Each block carries a label ℓ(b) ∈ { U, T, C, CC , PC }; the level map lvl sends lvl( U) = U and lvl( ℓ) = E for ℓ ∈ { T, C, CC , PC }.The four E-level labels are provenance-and-reuse tags, not a finer trust ordering: T (user-typed; reusable by its owner), C (single-user confirmed; admissible only to the tools in its scope, reusable without re-confirmation only by its owner), CC (community-confirmed, Appendix S4; admissible only to the exact targets endorsed (tools, or the reasoning pseudo-target, §S4.1), reusable by anyone), PC (P -composed; empty tool scope, hence never eligible for the sensitive fast path of Rule R4). Label combination is the meet on levels: an operation combining inputs yields level U if any input is at level U, else level E; no operation in Σ raises an untrusted value to level E by combination. 

Definition S2.2 (Store and configuration) . A block is b = (id , bytes( b), ℓ (b), π (b), scope (b)) with interpreter-only provenance π(b) ∈ { typed , pasted , ingress , cryst , pcomposed , confirmed , cache }, assigned only by the  

> 45 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

interpreter (provenance is interpreter-private and not part of obs P ), and, for endorsed blocks, a tool scope 

scope (b) ⊆ Tools sens ∪ Tools data (a set of individual tool identifiers). Every block-creating transition of Figures S2–S3 instantiates one of the total constructors 

newT( v) := (id E

for a tool target, σ(reasoning) = ∅; the cache confirmations of Options O1–O2 use newCache , all other accepting confirmations newC . Tool identifiers are canonical, fixed TCB configuration, so scope membership is a decidable syntactic test. Block identifiers are two-sorted and operation-determined (Rule R1(g)); the sorts separate the tiers: an endorsed-sort identifier resolves only to an E-level block, an untrusted-sort identifier only to a U-block. A configuration is c = ( M, σ P , σ Q, h ): the store, the Protected-LLM state, the Reader state, and the interpreter’s sanitized decision history h: a sequence of (target, decision) records, one appended at each reached gating review (one per reached gating position; for a batch, one per gating non-⋄ body position plus one aggregate element decision) — never bytes, never a block identifier of either sort, never a selection ordinal, never a candidate multiplicity, collection cardinality, or page count; selections, candidate lists, page renderings, and per-page decisions are recorded in the display transcript only (Definition S3.8). A null-handle abort appends the same (target, reject) record a user rejection appends (Rule R6). Hence h

contains no U-bytes, exposes no untrusted-sort identifier (Rule R1(g)), and carries no occupancy, cardinality, or pagination information (Rules R6, R7); this is the form machine-checked as HEvent (§S9). MU and ME

denote the restrictions of M to levels U and E.

Endorsement unit. The endorsement unit throughout is the (value, tool) pair : every confirmation names an exact byte string and an exact tool, and there is no endorsement transfer between tools, however similar. (Exact-target discipline governs the sensitive fast path and all endorsement, promotion, and counting; the 

baseline data-tool path of Rule R4(b) is scope-blind, Corollary S3.23(b), and Option O2 extends the exact-target discipline to data tools.) Combinations, orderings, and timings of separately endorsed values are ungated by design (rationale and residual: N2, S11; hardening: Option O1). 

Individuation of trusted blocks. T-blocks are individuated at write time: each field the user explicitly supplies (or, absent field structure, the command as one atomic unit) is one T-block. The individuating parser is fixed interpreter code inside the TCB; the fast-path test of Rule R4 is byte-equality against these stored blocks. 

Two scopes and one selector. The agent is a transition system over configurations whose steps are tool invocations, control flow, and the operations of Figures S2–S3, executed in one of two scopes: the privileged scope P may invoke any tool and reaches memory only through Σ. Throughout the formal development P is modelled as a strategy : a function from its interaction history, its own emitted operations, the reply stream of Rule R10, its view obs P (c), and the coins ω (convention (C1)), to its next operation. Its internal state 

σP is therefore an abbreviation for that history and carries no independent degrees of freedom: two runs agreeing on the history agree on σP by definition. This is not a restriction on P (the strategy is arbitrary, adversarial included) but the standard determinism-modulo-coins reading of (C1), and it is the form in which 

P is machine-checked (§S9). The quarantined scope Q has read-only access to the whole store and no tool access. A third model role, the trusted-store retriever RE (Rule R11), is a stateless selector over ME: it reads only the endorsed store and returns only block identifiers, which the interpreter dereferences. (The asymmetry between Q’s and RE ’s vision is forced by the theorems: N3.)  

> 46 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Definition S2.3 (Protected-LLM view) . For a configuration c, let H(c) denote the handle structure of Rule R1 and x(c) the trusted command input received so far; h is the decision history. The Protected-LLM view is 

obs P (c) =  ME, H (c), x (c), h .

By Figures S2–S3 and Rules R1–R11, obs P (c) together with the reply stream of Rule R10 is the entirety of what P can observe (Lemma S3.17). 

Remark S2.4 (Information interface vs. effect interface) . Σ is the interface of effects ; obs P (c) is the interface of information . Granting P direct sight of ME in its view is a deliberate, conservative strengthening: every theorem quantifies over all strategies of this better-informed P , so it a fortiori covers any P that must acquire the same data operationally, and the two-run theorem is harder under it (a more informed P has more ways to diverge). recall on endorsed identifiers is consequently informationally redundant and is retained as the operational access path; the sentence “ P reaches memory only through Σ” concerns effects, not information. The mechanization adopts exactly this reading (§S9: obs is computed through memEOf ). 

Definition S2.5 (Sensitive sinks, gate transactions, and confirmations) . A sensitive sink event is a call 

tool (a1, . . . , a n) with tool ∈ Tools sens .A gating position of a tool invocation is an argument position that does not pass the fast path of Rule R4. The invocation’s gating positions are processed left to right as one atomic gate transaction (Rule R2): at each reached position the interpreter resolves the argument, displays its bytes with zoned provenance (Rule R8), and reads the user’s selection (if among candidates) and decision z ∈ { accept , reject }; a null resolution renders the fixed no-value display of Rule R6 and takes the standard reject outcome, consuming no decision; a rejecting outcome aborts the review of the remaining positions. An accepting review of resolved bytes v at target t is a confirmation event confirm (v, t ). Confirmations are 

durable : at the close of the atomic transaction the interpreter writes, for every accepted gating position, one fresh endorsed copy of its displayed bytes scoped to the target (Figure S3, convention (vii)), whether or not the transaction as a whole succeeds . The tool itself executes iff every gating position was accepted and none was null; on execution the bytes that flow at each position are exactly the bytes displayed for it, and on abort no argument flows. 

Counting unit. For all accounting (Appendices S4, S6), confirmations are counted per (value, tool) pair per gate transaction : accepting reviews of the same exact pair at several positions of one transaction are one pair-confirmation; the repeated reviews themselves are attention cost (N18). A standalone confirm (v, t ) with t a tool or “reasoning” is the single-position special case. The “publish” target is not a local confirmation: it creates no local block and is defined by the P UBLISH transitions of Figure S3 and Rule R12. Batch-element page approvals under Rule R9 are call-local: they authorize the displayed elements for that map instance only and create no durable copy; gating non-⋄ arguments of a map body are ordinary durable confirmations (convention (vii ′)). 

S2.2 Operational semantics 

Figures S2–S3 give the interface transition schema of the memory interface: complete at the level of the interface alphabet (every operation’s store effect and reply is fixed here, with totality per Rule R10(b)), with the remaining components of the run state — fixed by Definition S2.6 — evolving by the deterministic bookkeeping stated in the rules and conventions. Every transition is fixed interpreter code; no model participates in any label assignment or any reply. Replies to P are drawn from the grammar at the top of Figure S2 and nothing else (Rule R10); the side conditions cite the Rules of §S2.3, which constrain naming, atomicity, display, and gating. Transitions triggered by the user or the environment (channel events, gate decisions, tool returns) are marked with their trigger; all others are P -emitted. 

Definition S2.6 (Extended configuration) . The full run state comprises: the store M; the two identifier allocators of Rule R1(g) (endorsed-sort and untrusted-sort); the interpreter-private handle-resolution table  

> 47 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(index = handle identifier; never read by any reply, Rule R7); P ’s reply stream (Rule R10); the decision history h; the trusted command input x(c); the executed-tool, confirmation, and reader-event counters that index the environment’s oracles; and three ghost logs used only by the theorems — the display transcript 

DT (Definition S3.8), the sensitive-sink log (Definition S3.7), and P ’s emitted-operation log. P -visible components are exactly obs P (c) and the reply stream (Lemma S3.17); all others are interpreter-private or ghost. A block is readable iff it is at level E, i.e. dereferenceable to bytes by recall (R ECALL -E); U-blocks are never readable to P . For the map-free fragment, the executable interpreter of §S9 realizes this extended configuration verbatim ( IState ), and the figures are its human-readable projection onto the store and reply components.

Figure S3: Interface transition schema of the memory interface (2/2): search, crystallization, confirmation, eviction, iteration, and failure, continued from Figure S2. Every transition is fixed interpreter code; no model assigns any label or composes any reply. Conventions for both figures follow below. 

50 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

Conventions (apply to Figures S2–S3). (i) capture( o) stores the outputs o as U-blocks newU( ·, ingress )

bound to a single fresh collection handle hj (Rules R1, R3); the identifier is operation-determined, computable by P , and never returned. A call whose captured output count is zero binds its collection handle with null 

resolution (Rule R6): a zero-cardinality capture and an empty search result are the same null-resolution case. The capture operation exposes no direct cardinality or resolution bit to P (Rule R7); any later distinction surfaces only through the logged null-collection display and its standard aggregated reject (Rule R9(b)). (ii) Element calls inside M AP are interpreter-iterated, not P -emitted: individually neither handle-producing nor acknowledged. If the argument collection has null resolution (an empty search result or zero-output capture, convention (i)), the tool-free form map (crystallize (⋄), H ) writes no summaries, binds the map’s operation-determined output collection handle with null resolution (Rules R1(a),(c), R6), returns the ordinary 

ack , and appends no display or decision event; the tool-bearing form takes the null-collection batch treatment of Rule R9(b). (iii) In T OOL -G ATE -A CC and T OOL -G ATE -R EJ , gating positions are reviewed left to right; one decision is recorded per reached gating position, and the displayed bytes of an executed call are exactly the bytes that flow; the entire step is atomic (Rule R2). Every accepted position’s fresh C

copy is written at the close of the step, on the accepting and the aborting branch alike; rejected and null positions never flow and write nothing. The reply stream carries one decision token per gated invocation, regardless of the number of gating positions; per-position decisions are recorded in the transcript and in 

h only (Rule R10(a)(i),(iv)). Whether an invocation is gated at all is a deterministic function of P ’s own emitted argument list and ME (Rule R4), hence already P -computable; the token-shape difference between TOOL -F AST , T OOL -G ATE -A CC , and T OOL -G ATE -R EJ therefore carries no information beyond trusted data and the logged decisions (Rule R7) (the CC branch’s live-entry bit is E-level log data, coupled under A8 in two-run comparisons (exogenous prefix coupled; local suffixes derived equal, Def. S4.2)), and within the reject class, user rejection and null-handle abort remain indistinguishable in the reply and in h (Rule R6). (iv) Totality (Rule R10(b)): every well-formed invocation with any argument is covered: unresolvable arguments take R ECALL -O-style opaque-handle replies, failed deterministic preconditions take F AIL ; no invocation is undefined and none distinguishes attacker-influenced states. (v) M ⊖ id denotes M \ { id }

when id resolves to a block of the owner’s local store, and M otherwise. The reply is the constant ack 

in every case; the realised outcome (removal of the owner’s block, at either level; no-op on an identifier not resolving to a block of the owner’s local store, including an unoccupied one) is recorded in the audit log interpreter-side and never returned to P . The effect on ME is P -visible trusted data (Rule R7); any effect on MU, and the absence of any effect, are indistinguishable to P . A handle argument to evict is a deterministic malformation of P ’s own emission and takes the generic failure outcome (Rule R10(a)(iii)). (vi) Channel-triggered transitions (W-T YPED , W-P ASTE ) acknowledge the channel; nothing is appended to 

P ’s reply stream, so the presence or absence of a paste event is not P -observable (Rule R7, convention (C9)). (vii) Fresh-copy endorsement; block immutability. An accepting confirmation never relabels a block in place: C ONF -A CC and T OOL -G ATE -A CC write, per accepted value, one fresh C-labelled block carrying exactly the displayed bytes, at the next endorsed-sort identifier (Rule R1(g)), with scope σ(t) = {t} for a tool target and σ(reasoning ) = ∅ (readable, but never eligible for the sensitive fast path of Rule R4); the resolved source block, where one exists, is unchanged and remains at its level, and the publish target (Rule R12) is a shared-tier act creating no local block. Endorsement is durable : the accepted positions’ fresh copies are written at the close of the atomic step whether or not the enclosing invocation executes (Rule R2). Consequently no transition of Figures S2–S3 alters an existing block’s bytes, label, or scope: blocks are immutable once written, and the store changes only by creation of fresh blocks and by E VICT . (vii ′) Batch acceptance is call-local for elements. An accepting batch confirmation (Rule R9) authorizes each ⋄-element’s bytes for its own element call of that map instance only: no persistent endorsed copy of any element is created, and the elements remain U-labelled in MU. Fresh C-copies are written for the accepted gating non-⋄ arguments , one each, scoped {tool }, exactly as at T OOL -G ATE -A CC , and durably so: they persist even when a later element page rejects and no element call executes (M AP -T OOL -R EJ ). This is forced by the identifier discipline of Rule R1(g): per-element copies would allocate |H| endorsed-sort identifiers with |H|

attacker-chosen, exposing an attacker-dependent quantity in ME; the non-⋄ count is fixed by P ’s emitted  

> 51 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

body. Convention (vii)’s per-accepted-value fresh copy, and the endorsed-copy clause of Definition S2.5, are read with this carve-out: they govern C ONF -A CC and T OOL -G ATE -A CC ; batch-accepted elements flow under Rule R2’s displayed-bytes binding without entering ME. Consequently re-processing the same elements in a later map is gated afresh — call-local authorization caches nothing — and the single-approval cap (INV-3) does not extend to map elements, whose confirmations are per-task cost (Appendix S6, Scope).          

> Commentary (non-normative; the MAP rules and the mechanization boundary). The split fixes, per body form, the decision token (none for the tool-free form), the store effect ( capture of element outputs vs. schema-bound
> U-summaries s1, . . . , s |H|written as in C RYST ), and the endorsement effect (fresh C-copies for gating non-⋄
> arguments only; elements are authorized call-locally, convention (vii ′)). In every case, including rejection, exactly one collection-handle index is consumed, on rejection with null resolution — so handle structure is independent of the outcome (Rule R1(a)), closing the identifier channel a skipped index would open. map is normative here; its batch-confirmation surface — pagination, per-page decisions, the aggregated decision, all-or-nothing displayed-bytes flow, and batch determinacy — is mechanized as a standalone layer ( BatchGate.lean ,§S9), while its store-level integration into the run-level interpreter remains outside the mechanized surface (delta (6)).

S2.3 Interface rules 

The rules below constrain the transitions of Figures S2–S3. Each clause is normative and implementable as stated; all rationale lives in Appendix S7 under the keys cited, or in the marked commentary blocks of §S1.1. 

Rule R1 (Operation-determined naming: handles and block identifiers) .

(a) Handle identifiers (including collection-handle identifiers) are drawn from the sequence of P -emitted operations alone: the j-th handle-producing operation of a run binds identifier hj . Identifiers never encode, count, or depend on store contents. (b) Handle-producing operations: ingress capture (the triggering tool invocation is P -emitted; P computes the collection handle’s identifier from its own operation count; nothing is returned); recall returning a handle; fill ; crystallize ; map .(c) A map invocation is exactly one handle-producing operation regardless of collection cardinality: its per-element outputs are captured as U-blocks bound to a single fresh collection handle determined by the map ’s position in P ’s operation sequence. Element calls are interpreter-iterated, not P -emitted, and are individually neither handle-producing nor acknowledged. (d) P reaches captured element outputs only through fill /crystallize searches. No handle identifier depends on collection size. (e) A collection handle admits exactly two uses: as the argument of map , or as a tool argument — the latter defined by desugaring: an invocation tool (. . . , h, . . . ) with a collection-handle argument h is the instance map (tool (. . . , ⋄, . . . ), h ), the desugaring applied before the invocation matches any transition of Figures S2–S3 (it never instantiates T OOL -F AST or T OOL -G ATE -*), batch-gated per Rule R9 and subject to the body grammar of clause (f) (in particular at most one collection handle per invocation and no other handle argument; violations take the generic failure outcome, deterministic in P ’s own emission, Rule R7). A collection handle exposes no length, no indexing, and no per-element access. (f) Map bodies. The body ˆo of map (ˆ o, H ) is a single operation template containing exactly one occurrence of the distinguished element placeholder ⋄, of one of exactly two forms: (i) one tool invocation 

tool (a1, . . . , a n) in which one argument is ⋄ and every other argument is a P -supplied non-handle value, subject per argument to the ordinary mediation of Rule R4 (fast-path values flow; a gating argument is confirmed once for the whole batch, at the batch confirmation of Rule R9, alongside the elements); or (ii) one tool-free crystallize (⋄) template. The body may not contain map , recall , fill , evict , confirm ,

display , the write operation write P , or a handle argument (the interpreter-side captures of forms (i)–(ii)  

> 52 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

are transitions of the interpreter, not body operations); nesting is not defined. Well-formedness is a syntactic test on P ’s emitted operation alone, and a body outside this grammar takes the generic failure outcome, deterministic in P ’s own emission (Rule R7). Multi-step per-element pipelines are expressed as successive map s: each map yields a fresh collection handle (clause (c)), which clause (e) admits as the argument of a further map , each step batch-gated independently; the grammar restricts the shape of one step, never the depth of a pipeline. (g) Two-sorted block identifiers. Block identifiers are drawn from two disjoint sorts, endorsed (id E 

> 0

, id E 

> 1

, . . . )and untrusted (id U 

> 0

, id U 

> 1

, . . . ), each allocated in sequence. Endorsed-sort identifiers are allocated by exactly the local endorsed-block-creating transitions: W-T YPED , W-P, C ONF -A CC , and, per accepted gating position , T OOL -G ATE -A CC , T OOL -G ATE -R EJ , M AP -T OOL -A CC , and M AP -T OOL -R EJ (for maps, gating non-⋄ body positions only — a count fixed by P ’s emitted operation, never by the collection cardinality, convention (vii ′)). A publish decision allocates no identifier of either sort (P UBLISH -A CC ). The j-th endorsed block of a run sits at id E 

> j

, a position determined by the coupled event sequence (the typed stream, P ’s emitted operations, and the logged decisions) alone, hence computable by P

and identical across runs whose coupled events agree. Untrusted-sort identifiers are allocated by the untrusted-writing transitions (W-P ASTE , ingress capture, C RYST ) and may depend on attacker-chosen multiplicities; no untrusted-sort identifier is ever exposed to P by any reply or view (Rules R6, R7, R10(b)). An endorsed-sort identifier never resolves to a U-block and an untrusted-sort identifier never resolves to an E-block, so the sorts separate the tiers syntactically. Like handle identifiers, block identifiers of either sort never encode, count, or depend on store contents beyond this allocation order.     

> Commentary (non-normative; role of the identifier discipline). Clause (g) closes the identifier channel that in-place endorsement would open: identifiers visible to P(those of ME) depend only on coupled events, while every attacker-dependent multiplicity can influence only untrusted-sort identifiers, which no reply and no view exposes. It is consumed by Theorem S3.25 (Step 4) and is machine-checked as the two-sorted id space of the mechanization (§S9).

Rule R2 (Atomic gating; durable per-value endorsement) . When confirmations gate a tool call, the reviews of all gating positions, the writing of the accepted positions’ fresh endorsed copies, and the invocation (or its abort) form one atomic interpreter step: no step may interleave between display, decision, endorsement, and flow. The bytes that flow at an executed call are the displayed bytes, bound to that call instance. If any gating position is rejected or null, the invocation aborts entire: no argument flows; the accepted positions’ endorsed copies are nevertheless written, their accepting decisions being recorded in the transcript like any confirmation. When a batch confirmation gates a map (Rule R9), the aggregated accepting decision and the batch of element invocations form one atomic step, and the bytes that flow at each element call are exactly the element bytes displayed for that call; accepted gating non-⋄ arguments are endorsed durably exactly as at a direct call, whatever the element pages’ outcome.    

> Commentary (non-normative; severability and durable endorsement). Soundness of durable endorsement on an aborting transaction is the severability clause of A4: intent is per displayed value, severally. A commit-on-success variant — discarding accepted positions’ copies on abort — would be safety-monotone but sacrifices the once-per-pair cap (INV-3): a transaction that accepts one pair and aborts on a later position leaves no durable copy, so re-presentations of the same pair re-force it without bound. Durability is adopted precisely because the cap is load-bearing (Theorem S6.2).

Rule R3 (Ingress: all tool outputs captured) . Tools partition into Tools sens (sensitive, effectful) and Tools data 

(data readers); the partition and the canonical tool identifiers are fixed deployment configuration, per capability, inside the TCB. The interpreter captures every tool output, including the status and error responses of sensitive calls, to MU with label U, and returns to P only a single constant acknowledgement token per invocation: never the bytes, never a success bit, never a count, size, or identifier derived from captured content.  

> 53 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Rule R4 (Whitelist mediation at every tool boundary) .

(a) A value a passed to a tool in Tools sens flows without confirmation iff a consists of readable E-level bytes and the interpreter verifies that a byte-equals a stored T-block, or byte-equals some C/CC -block b

with tool ∈ scope (b) (for CC , the matched block is the live shared-tier entry, checked valid at call time; §S4.1). ( PC blocks have empty scope and never pass this test.) (b) A value passed to a tool in Tools data flows without confirmation iff it consists of readable E-level bytes. (c) A handle argument never flows silently to any tool: it always triggers confirm (a, tool ), at data tools exactly as at sensitive ones, regardless of whether its resolved bytes coincide with stored endorsed content. There are no per-tool exceptions. (d) The fast-path check is membership in ME plus a scope test, not taint-tracking of P ’s code. 

Rule R5 (Readable local endorsement) . Every accepting confirmation with target t ∈ Tools sens ∪Tools data ∪{reasoning } writes a fresh block newC( v, t ) (Options O1–O2: newCache( v, t )) carrying exactly the displayed bytes, labelled C (or, under community promotion, the shared tier carries CC status; Appendix S4), at the next endorsed-sort identifier (Rule R1(g)); its bytes are readable by P via recall , the identifier being computable by P from its own coupled event history. The originating untrusted block, where one exists, is unchanged and remains untrusted. Before confirmation, a crystallized summary is U-labelled, lives reader/interpreter-side, and is not in σP . An accepting publish decision creates no local block and allocates no endorsed-sort identifier: it emits only the shared-tier submission event of Rule R12 (P UBLISH -A CC ). 

Rule R6 (Null handle) . A fill or crystallize search over MU that finds nothing returns a null handle : the handle’s existence and identifier are fixed by the requesting operation (Rule R1); only its resolution differs, and that difference is hidden from P . Likewise, recall on an identifier that does not resolve to a readable 

E-block returns a handle of the same kind: whether it resolves to U-bytes or to nothing (in particular, whether the identifier is occupied at all) is hidden from P . If a null handle is passed to any tool, the gate fires as usual (Rule R4), the decision pane renders a fixed “no value resolved” display, recorded in the display transcript like any confirmation event, the call aborts, and P receives the standard reject decision, indistinguishable in the reply stream and in the sanitized history h from a user rejection (Definition S2.2); the no-value display itself is recorded on the transcript like any confirmation event. 

Rule R7 (Failure indistinguishability) . The success/failure of a U-store search, and the resolution status of any recall , is consumed interpreter-side to choose the null-vs-real handle; no reply and no sanitized-history record exposes it. A handle’s private resolution may later be displayed at a gate: that display and the user’s logged decision are an explicit transcript release (Definition S3.8), not a failure channel. Any reader error, reader crash, or reader budget exhaustion arising during an operation over MU is likewise consumed interpreter-side: the operation returns the same operation-determined handle it would return on success (Rules R1, R6), whose resolution to bytes, to nothing, or to an aborted computation is hidden from P . The generic failure outcome ( fail , Figures S2–S3) is reserved for failures that are a deterministic function of P ’s own emitted operations, ME, and the coupled coins ω alone (malformed invocations, failed preconditions on σP , and the retrieval failure of R ETRIEVE -F). Search outcomes over ME are trusted data and may be exposed. (Rationale and the store-quota corollary: N4, S7, convention (C7).) 

Rule R8 (Faithful display on a split, zoned surface) .

(a) The display surface is split into a decision pane and auxiliary selection surfaces .(b) The decision pane shows exactly the resolved bytes that will flow (or be recorded as endorsed content), plus a provenance line in two visually separated zones: (i) attested facts (channel, label history, times-tamps, signature-verification status), rendered in an interpreter-controlled region: TCB-authenticated, not attacker-forgeable or -alterable in content (event timing may be attacker-influenced, e.g. a captured tool output’s timestamp reflects when the attacker acted; what is guaranteed is that the recorded facts are true and unmodified); (ii) source-identification strings originating in untrusted content (sender names,  

> 54 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

subjects, citations), rendered verbatim and visibly marked unverified . Where a transaction aborts after some accepting reviews, the pane states that the accepted values remain authorized for their targets. (c) The decision pane contains no reader-generated prose outside the confirmed payload region; where the payload itself is a crystallized summary, it is reader-generated by construction, rendered verbatim, and governed by Assumption A5. (d) Selection surfaces may show reader-generated previews, visibly marked unverified; previews are rendered UI-side only and never returned to P , and carry no endorsement authority. (e) Both surfaces are recorded in the display transcript and the audit log (Definition S3.8). (f) On a rejection, the displayed bytes are not returned to P ; P receives only the decision. (Preview rationale: N8, S9.) 

Rule R9 (Batch gate under untrusted-driven iteration) .

(a) A tool call reached inside map over a U-collection — including the desugared form of a collection-handle tool argument (Rule R1(e)) — takes no fast path of Rule R4. (b) The map is gated as a single batch confirmation whose decision pane displays the collection’s cardinality 

and the full resolved bytes of every element , together with the resolved bytes of any gating non-⋄

argument of the body (Rule R1(f)), displayed once for the batch, paginated as needed, the cardinality displayed and recorded on the first page; per-page cardinality is capped at cbatch ; the interpreter performs any split, presenting a larger collection as successive pages of one batch confirmation. A deployment may additionally fix a total-cardinality cap (TCB configuration): a map over a collection exceeding it renders a fixed oversize display — recorded in the transcript like the no-value display of Rule R6 — and takes the standard aggregated reject of clause (c), consuming no decision, indistinguishable in the reply stream and in h from a page rejection or a null collection. A null collection (a collection handle with null resolution: an empty search result, or a zero-output capture, convention (i); Rules R1(a), R6) is handled identically: the interpreter renders the fixed no-value display of Rule R6, recorded in the transcript as a batch event exactly like the oversize display, and the map takes the standard aggregated reject of clause (c), consuming no decision (M AP -T OOL -R EJ ; mechanized as batchGate_null , §S9). The no-value and oversize displays are distinct event kinds (Definition S3.8), so the first recorded batch event determines the size class (Lemma S3.20). (c) P receives a single aggregated decision for the whole map (accept iff every page was accepted, else the single standard reject), regardless of page count. The number of pages, like the cardinality itself, is never returned to P in any form; both are displayed to the user and recorded in the transcript only. (d) Each page’s rendering and decision is recorded in the transcript; the aggregated decision is recorded once. By Rule R2, the bytes that flow at each element call are exactly the element bytes displayed for it. The user may reject at any page, aborting the remainder. The oversize and null-collection displays are recorded under their respective fixed-display event kinds (Definition S3.8), each with its standard reject and the aggregated batch decision. (e) Per-element results are captured to MU and never returned to P (the map returns one ack token). (All-or-nothing rationale and cbatch sizing: N6, S12.) 

Rule R10 (Constant-alphabet interface, with totality) .

(a) Every value the interpreter returns to P is of one of two kinds. The first kind is drawn from a fixed finite alphabet independent of the attack and the environment: (i) one constant acknowledgement per 

executed tool invocation or map , independent of outcome and of the number, size, or content of captured blocks; a gate-rejected invocation does not execute and returns only the reject decision of item (iv) (T OOL -G ATE -R EJ ); (ii) opaque handles named by Rule R1; (iii) the generic failure outcome of Rule R7, deterministic in P ’s emitted operations, ME, and the coupled coins ω; (iv) the user’s decision at a  

> 55 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

confirmation, with the null-gate abort of Rule R6 rendered as the reject decision. The second kind is (v): verbatim E-bytes dereferenced from ME.(b) Totality. Every syntactically well-formed invocation of every operation of Figures S2–S3, with any argument, has its behaviour defined by the figures and these rules; failed preconditions and unresolvable arguments are rendered, uniformly, as the generic failure outcome or as an operation-determined opaque handle, never as a distinguishing error. In particular, whether a given identifier is occupied by a U-block is never observable to P through any operation: recall and evict alike return operation-determined replies (an opaque handle; the constant ack ) uniformly across resolving, non-resolving, U-occupied, and 

E-occupied arguments, resolution consumed interpreter-side (Rules R6, R7; Figure S3, convention (v)). 

Rule R11 (Trusted-store retrieval) . RE (i) reads only ME, never any U-bytes; (ii) has no tool access; (iii) returns only block identifiers, which the interpreter dereferences against ME verbatim; non-dereferencing output is discarded. RE ’s sampling randomness is coupled alongside P ’s (convention (C1)). (Why neither reader’s vision can be relaxed: N3.) 

Deployment-selectable hardening. Options O1–O2 are formally instantiated: each strictly gates more flows, and their monotonicity is proved as Proposition S3.28. Option O3 is a requirements-level extension, outside the formal semantics and outside Proposition S3.28’s quantifier (O3). 

Option O1 (Sink-scoped trusted values) . T-blocks require a first-use confirmation per sensitive sink, after which scope-caching applies. Blocks created by these confirmations carry the interpreter-only provenance mark cache and are ineligible for submission (Rule R12). 

Option O2 (Exact-target mediation at data tools) . The data-tool clause of Rule R4(b) is replaced by the sensitive-tool test of clause (a): a value passed to a tool in Tools data flows without confirmation iff it byte-equals a stored T-block, or byte-equals some C/CC -block b with tool ∈ scope (b) (for CC , the live shared-tier entry, checked valid at call time, §S4.1). Any other data-tool argument — a freshly composed value, or endorsed bytes whose scope does not admit this data tool (in particular every reasoning-target copy, whose scope is empty, convention (vii)) — is confirmed once; the fresh copy is scoped {tool } per convention (vii), so it scope-caches and covers all the user’s later use of the pair (INV-3). Blocks created by these confirmations carry the interpreter-only cache mark and are ineligible for submission (Rule R12); their gate events are cache confirmations, accept-inert per Definition S4.6. 

Option O3 (Local scope leases (requirements level; not formally instantiated)) . C-scopes carry a deployment-set lease (convention (C4)): expiry withdraws the expired copy’s tool authorization, so the pair’s next scope-checked use with no other currently authorizing witness re-confirms once. Unlike Options O1–O2, O3 is specified at the requirements level only: it is not part of the formal semantics of this document, no theorem quantifies over it, and Proposition S3.28 does not cover it. A conforming instantiation is a future extension obligated to satisfy all of the following, and to prove analogues of Lemma S3.20, Theorem S3.25, Lemma S6.1, Theorem S6.2, and the ledger accounting of Lemma S6.7 for the extended semantics. (a) Immutability. Blocks stay immutable (A2): a lease is a validity predicate computed interpreter-side from creation-time metadata (creation epoch or slot index, never wall-clock time) and the current index; expiry is never a block mutation or removal. (b) Determinism on matched data. Validity inputs are functions of the coupled event sequence, and lease state is added to the matched components of Definition S3.6 and carried through the unwinding, so matched runs gate identically. (c) Witness-level expiry at tool targets. For tool targets, expiry invalidates only the expired local authorization witness (an ordinary or cache-marked C copy), and the two authorization tests it touches are kept distinct. Literal authorization , on a scope-checked literal path, is existential over whichever witnesses the deployment’s selected formal options recognize there — byte-equal T-blocks (per O1’s per-sink treatment where selected), live CC entries, and lease-valid in-scope C copies; the baseline data-tool path of Rule R4(b) is scope-blind, so lease enforcement at data tools requires O2 — and a literal use gates iff  

> 56 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

no such witness currently authorizes the pair. Handle coverage (Rule R13(i)) is narrower: a handle use gates in every case (Rule R4(c)) and is covered iff the identity holds a lease-valid, in-scope local C copy; a T or live-CC witness never makes a handle use covered, exactly as in the base semantics. The reasoning-coverage branch of Rule R13(i) remains readability-based and lease-insensitive: reasoning-target reuse never requires another approval. (d) Classification and renewal. An expiry-induced gate whose pair lacks any currently authorizing witness is required — whether a literal gate fired by expiry or a handle gate reclassified from covered to required because no lease-valid in-scope C copy remains. Any required approval so caused writes the fresh copy prescribed by the triggering rule — an ordinary C copy at an ordinary confirmation, a cache-marked, federation-inert copy at an O1/O2 cache gate (Rule R12, Definition S4.6(iii)) — and starts a new lease, so subsequent literal or handle use within the new period is fast-pathed or covered: at most one required confirmation per pair per lease period. The class is determined by the gate that fired, never by any expired duplicate witness; rejection and abandonment renew nothing; duplicate matching copies are resolved by the existential tests of clause (c), never by a selection among witnesses. (e) Accounting. With each (user, item) seeing Elease = O(1) lease periods, the extension’s pathwise floor is the once-per-pair cap per lease period, an O(1) multiplicative factor on Theorem S6.2’s constants — an extension-level claim, to be proved with the extension. No saturation-vanishing conclusion follows from Elease = O(1) alone: expiry re-fires of promoted pairs are residual required confirmations, so the extension additionally requires an O3-augmented analogue of the complete residual bound of A11 and Theorem S6.9(iii) — the per-pair D0 + ε n form with expiry re-fires included, together with its product and residual-fraction rate conditions — not merely a substitute for ε(M ) → 0.(O3 is the single-user twin of the federated re-verification queue: N16.) 

S2.4 Trust axioms Assumption A1 (Deterministic source labelling) . ℓ(b) is a function of the channel/source of b alone, never of bytes( b). Hence the attacker cannot change any label. 

Assumption A2 (Label persistence and block immutability) . Blocks are immutable once written: no party and no transition alters an existing block’s bytes, label, or scope; the store changes only by the deterministic block-creating transitions of Figures S2–S3 (convention (vii)) and by evict . Trust is raised only by the creation of a fresh endorsed block at an accepting confirm , or by deterministic community promotion of a shared-tier entry (Appendix S4); the attacker can do neither. 

Assumption A3 (Faithful, zoned display) . At a confirmation, the decision-pane bytes are exactly the bytes that will flow or be recorded; the attested-fact zone is TCB-authenticated and not attacker-forgeable (Rule R8(b)); untrusted source-identification strings appear only in the marked zone; previews are confined to selection surfaces, visibly marked (Rule R8). 

Assumption A4 (Sound atomic endorsement) . The user returns accept on a displayed value v at tool tool 

only if the user intends exactly v to be authorized for tool ; the intent is per displayed value, severally, and does not depend on the outcomes of the transaction’s other reviews — which is what makes the durable endorsement of Rule R2 sound when a later review aborts the invocation. At a batch confirmation (Rule R9), the user returns accept on a page only if this intent holds for each displayed element and each displayed gating non-⋄ argument, severally, at the batch’s tool: batch acceptance is the conjunction of the per-value intents, never an aggregate judgment of the collection. 

Assumption A5 (Sound narrative endorsement) . The user returns accept on a displayed crystallized summary 

S only if, having checked S against its displayed source citations, the user intends S’s content to inform reasoning. 

Assumption A6 (Exogenous command stream) . The user’s typed command-channel input is an exogenous input stream, coupled identically across the runs compared in Theorem S3.25.   

> 57 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT
> Commentary (non-normative; epistemic status of A4–A6). A4 and A5 are trust axioms in the sense of CaMeL Debenedetti et al. (2025); A6 is the human-channel idealization that any displayed-content carve-out requires. Their epistemic status, the regime that makes them defensible, and the discharge of A6’s practical content by logging both display surfaces are discussed at N5 (see also S9, S10, S12); A5’s realistic weakness at scale is what the community mechanism of Appendix S4 addresses.

S2.5 Attacker model and trusted computing base Attacker. The attacker controls the content of every U-labelled block, all tool outputs (including sensitive-tool responses and error strings) and paste-channel input, including the byte-length and the number of 

U-blocks captured per call. It does not control the user’s typed command input, the labelling, the interpreter, or the user’s decisions. P , Q, and RE are isolated from the attacker, not assumed benign : the single-user theorems hold under adversarial behaviour of either reader, and misdirection among the user’s own typed or endorsed values is a utility failure outside the threat model (Option O1 offers defence in depth; scope discussion: §S7.4). 

Trusted computing base (TCB), single-user. The TCB comprises: an authenticated command channel distinguishing typed from pasted from tool input; the canonical tool identifiers and the Tools sens /Tools data 

partition; the interpreter’s implementation (including byte-equality and dereference checks) and the individu-ating parser; the zoned rendering path of Rule R8; and the hash-chained audit log materializing the display transcript. The store idealization and quota policy are convention (C7). Federated TCB additions are listed in §S4.3. 

S3 Single-User Guarantees 

This appendix proves two conditional interface properties. Action Integrity is an exact-byte, exact-tool authorization property: no untrusted-origin value reaches a sensitive action unless the user endorsed exactly that value for exactly that action; it does not authorize the choice to invoke a tool (§S7.4). Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript is a fixed-horizon, termination-insensitive trace-equivalence property: holding fixed everything the user ever saw and decided on the display surface, together with the coupled exogenous inputs, untrusted memory has no further effect on behaviour; it is not a claim of reasoning correctness or of resistance to manipulation through displayed content (Remark S3.9, N7). The statements are in the noninterference-modulo-declassification tradition Goguen & Meseguer (1982; 1984); Sabelfeld & Sands (2009); Sabelfeld & Myers (2004); Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007); the first is the PerMIT instance of qualified robustness Zdancewic & Myers (2005); Myers et al. (2006). Both theorems, the supporting lemmas, the baseline separation, the flow half of the hardening monotonicity, and the batch-confirmation surface of Rule R9 are machine-checked (§S9: scope, axiom audit, artifact, correspondence Table S5, and the disclosed modelling deltas). Lineage and positioning: N1. 

S3.1 Definitions Definition S3.1 (U-equivalence) . c1 ≈U c2 iff obs P (c1) = obs P (c2): agreement on ME (content and scopes), H(c), x(c), and h, while MU may differ arbitrarily Volpano et al. (1996); Sabelfeld & Myers (2003). 

Definition S3.2 (Sink-event safety) . Fix a finite sequence of display events — decision-pane confirmation events, each carrying its displayed bytes, target, and decision (Definition S2.5, Rule R8), and renderings — and a trusted typed-input stream x. A recorded sensitive sink event is safe for that history if every argument value of the event is: (i) the exact bytes of a typed input unit of x (a T-block’s bytes, individuation per §S2.1); or (ii) endorsed at a prior accepting decision-pane confirmation event of the history displaying exactly those bytes at exactly the event’s tool (for element calls of a batch-gated map , at the batch confirmation  

> 58 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

displaying those exact element bytes, Rule R9); or (iii) gated at the event’s own call, the flowed bytes being the displayed bytes of that call’s accepting confirmation (Rule R2). The delegated form extends case (ii) by one alternative, relative to a designated federation-log prefix (in a run, the prefix delivered at the event’s step, Definition S4.2): the bytes byte-equal a live community entry of that prefix’s replay naming the event’s exact tool, backed by a currently valid promotion certificate (Definition S4.1, A7); the personal form is the single-user predicate, machine-checked as SinkSafe (§S9). It is consumed by Definition S3.3(v) and Definition S3.10. 

Definition S3.3 (Well-formed configuration) . A configuration c is well-formed , written WF (c), if: (i) every 

T-block’s bytes occur in the typed stream x(c); (ii) every C-block b with t ∈ scope (b) is backed by an accepting decision-pane confirmation event displaying exactly bytes( b) at target t in the audit transcript, with the matching sanitized (target, accept) record in h; (iii) no block carries label CC (single-user; the federated counterpart replaces (iii) by a valid promotion certificate on the federation log, Appendix S4); (iv) the handle structure H(c) and the endorsed-sort identifier allocation are the operation-determined structures of Rule R1 for the configuration’s interaction history, σP is the strategy state of that history (§S2.1), and the recorded reply stream is the interface’s reply to that history (Figures S2–S3); (v) every recorded sink event is safe (Definition S3.2; federated form: the delegated predicate). The empty configuration is well-formed. A seeded 

configuration (nonempty ME, empty interaction history) is well-formed only together with a seed record :a designated initial-authorization prefix of the audit transcript (with its sanitized projection in h) backing every seeded T-block per (i) and every seeded scoped C-block per (ii) — operationally, the authorizations imported with a migrated store; formally, the record is read as part of x(c), h, and DT , whereupon (i)–(v) hold verbatim. A seeded configuration without such a record is in general not well-formed; it is sorted 

(Definition S3.5), which is what the two-run results require. Well-formedness is preserved by every transition of Figures S2–S3 (this is the induction of Lemmas S3.12–S3.14, machine-checked as the invariant Inv of §S9). Action Integrity and its corollaries quantify over runs from well-formed configurations; the two-run results quantify over matched pairs of sorted configurations (Definitions S3.5, S3.6). The history against which clause (v) reads Definition S3.2 is the configuration’s full audit history: any seed-record prefix followed by the run transcript (Definition S3.8). 

Remark S3.4 . Clause (v) is the standard inductive strengthening required to prove Theorem S3.21 by induction on run length (mirrored verbatim as the sinkSound field of Inv in the mechanization, §S9): it tracks, through the induction, exactly the per-event predicate of Definition S3.2, whose universal form over reachable configurations is the theorem’s conclusion — not an independent assumption, and not circular: the predicate is defined without reference to the theorem. The from-arbitrary-well-formed forms are preservation 

statements; the empty-initial-configuration forms, where the invariant is established rather than assumed (inv_init , §S9), are the creation forms. 

Definition S3.5 (Sorted configuration) . A configuration c is sorted if clauses (iii) and (iv) of Definition S3.3 hold of it: no CC label (federated form as there), operation-determined handle and endorsed-sort identifier structure with σP the strategy state, and an interface-conformant reply stream. Every well-formed configura-tion is sorted. Every endorsed-seed configuration — an arbitrary E-level ME at endorsed-sort identifiers (Rule R1(g)), an arbitrary MU, and an empty interaction history — is sorted, with no seed record required. Sortedness is preserved by every transition (machine-checked as WFs , with the endorsed-seed condition 

EStore ; §S9).                 

> Commentary (non-normative; why two classes). The split is forced by the theorems, in opposite directions. Action Integrity is an endorsement-soundness statement, so its quantifier must carry endorsement soundness: admitting a bare seed as well-formed would falsify Theorem S3.21 (Remark S3.15) — a seeded C-block with
> t∈scope flows to tungated, with no endorsing confirmation anywhere in the run. Reasoning Integrity, by contrast, never consumes endorsement soundness: its unwinding needs only the matched view, the operation-determined naming of Rule R1, and the strategy reading — precisely the sorted clauses — so its quantifier is deliberately the weaker class, admitting arbitrary shared endorsed seeds. The mechanization enforces exactly 59 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT
> this split: action_integrity_from requires the full invariant Inv , while reasoning_integrity_from
> requires only EStore /WFs (§S9).

Definition S3.6 (Matched configurations) . Two sorted configurations (Definition S3.5) are matched , c1 ≈ c2,if obs P (c1) = obs P (c2); P ’s interaction histories coincide (the same emitted-operation sequence, the same reply stream, and the same consumed coin prefix — hence σP, 1 = σP, 2, by the strategy reading of §S2.1); and the ghost display transcripts and sink logs coincide. Matched views and histories also fix the endorsed-sort allocation state (Rule R1(g)), so subsequent endorsed blocks are created at equal identifiers in the two runs. 

MU and the interpreter’s private handle resolutions may differ arbitrarily; the handle count is matched through H(c) (Rule R7). Matched implies ≈U; the converse fails on σP and the ghost logs, which is why the two-run results are stated over matched pairs. The canonical matched pair is two runs launched from a shared endorsed-seed configuration (Definition S3.5) with empty histories and empty ghost logs under two different attacks (the form mechanized as initE , §S9); matched pairs need not be well-formed, and Theorem S3.25 holds without any endorsement-soundness hypothesis on the seed. This relation is mechanized, field for field, as MatchW (§S9). 

Definition S3.7 (Ungated sink projection) . For a finite run ρ, Sink (ρ) is the sequence of sensitive sink events, each with its arguments, except that arguments displayed and accepted in the immediately preceding atomic gating confirmation (Rule R2, including, for element calls of a map , the batch confirmation of Rule R9) are stripped from the annotation. Stripping makes Sink (ρ) well-defined for baseline comparisons in which transcripts may be empty (Proposition S3.27); under DT -equality the stripped arguments are equal in any case, so the projection hides nothing in Theorem S3.25. 

Definition S3.8 (Display transcript) . For a run ρ from a designated starting configuration, DT (ρ) is the ordered sequence of all display events appended during ρ (the run suffix; an audit prefix carried by a seeded starting configuration belongs to its seed record, Definition S3.3, not to DT (ρ)): (i) selection-surface renderings, each with its candidate identifiers, rendered preview bytes (marked unverified), and the user’s selection, if any; (ii) decision-pane confirmation events, one per reached gating review, each with the resolved bytes (or the fixed no-value display of Rule R6), both provenance zones, the target, and the decision; (iii) for a batch, either the fixed oversize display of Rule R9(b) with its standard aggregated reject (where the deployment cap applies), or the fixed no-value display of a null collection with its standard aggregated reject (Rules R6, R9(b)), or every page’s rendering and per-page decision, the first page recording the collection’s cardinality — which, with cbatch , determines the deterministic pagination and hence the page count (Rule R9(b)) — and the aggregated decision, recorded once. Transcript entries carry interpreter-assigned event kinds (selection-surface rendering; decision-pane confirmation; batch page; the fixed no-value display; the fixed oversize display; aggregated batch decision), fixed constructors of the TCB logging path: the fixed displays are distinguished from ordinary pages by kind, never by comparing rendered byte strings, so page content colliding textually with a fixed message cannot confuse the classification (consumed by Lemma S3.20). Rejections are recorded as well as acceptances, and so are the accepting reviews of transactions that subsequently abort (Rule R2). The harness materializes the full audit history — any seed-record prefix followed by DT (ρ) — in a hash-chained audit log (operational reading: N7; previews: N8, S9). 

Remark S3.9 (Coverage of the display transcript) . By Rule R8(e), DT (ρ) comprises the totality of attacker-influenceable content ever rendered to the user, together with everything the user decided. Recording rejections is necessary: two runs in which the user rejects and later accepts the same content differ in behaviour during the interval, and a projection that forgets rejections cannot distinguish them. Previews carry no endorsement authority; their inclusion records them as part of the release history. 

Definition S3.10 (Action Integrity, personal and delegated) . PerMIT has personal Action Integrity (equiv-alently: argument-value integrity ) if in every run from a well-formed configuration, under every attack, every sensitive sink event is safe in the personal form of Definition S3.2: every value reaching a sensitive sink is a user-typed T-value, or was endorsed by this user at its exact bytes for that exact sink (now, or at a prior in-scope confirmation; for element calls of a batch-gated map , at the batch confirmation that  

> 60 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

displayed those exact element bytes, Rule R9). It has delegated Action Integrity if the same holds with the endorsement authority extended to a qualifying community confirmation of the exact bytes at the exact tool (CC , Appendix S4) (the delegated predicate of Definition S3.2); delegation is an assumption (A7), never a theorem of the single-user model. 

Definition S3.11 (Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript) . PerMIT has Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript if, for every pair of matched configurations c1 ≈ c2 (Definition S3.6), every pair of attacks (coupled per convention (C9): slot skeleton and typed stream only; paste presence and content, tool outputs, and reader outputs uncoupled), and every coupled coin sequence ω (convention (C1)), any two runs of a common horizon (convention (C9)) with DT (ρ1) = DT (ρ2) satisfy Sink (ρ1) = Sink (ρ2) and emit the same sequence of Protected-LLM operations and tool invocations. Informal reading (a conditional statement, not an unconditional inertness claim): any influence of untrusted memory on the listed behaviours must pass through a display event or an endorsed release — conditional on the transcript, hidden untrusted state has no further effect. An unrendered untrusted byte is not claimed inert outright: it may steer which candidates or previews the Reader surfaces, and thereby the transcript itself; what is excluded is influence that bypasses the transcript. The statement is trace-wise per coin sequence; the knowledge-based formulation in the style of gradual release Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007) that it induces is recorded in §S8.4. 

S3.2 Structural lemmas Lemma S3.12 (Protected-LLM isolation / provenance preservation) . In every configuration reachable from a sorted configuration (Definition S3.5; a fortiori from a well-formed one), no byte value enters σP by delivery from a U-labelled source : every bytes-carrying reply P receives is, at the step of its emission, the verbatim content of a non-U block of the store, and U-derived data appears in σP only as opaque handles. This is a provenance statement, not a disjointness of byte strings across tiers: a typed or endorsed byte string in σP

may be extensionally equal to the bytes of an unrelated U-block, and the flow it licenses is the trusted copy’s. 

(Machine-checked, run-level: bytes_reply_isolation , §S9. )

Proof. Induction on run length with invariant I(c): every bytes-carrying value ever delivered to P was delivered as the content of a non-U block (reply grammar case (v)) or as trusted command input; everything else in P ’s input stream is a handle or constant token. At a sorted start, σP is the strategy state of the initial interaction history (§S2.1, Definition S3.5), whose reply stream is interface-conformant: every bytes-carrying reply in it is E-level by the reply grammar, and its typed input is trusted command input at level T; an endorsed-seed configuration has empty history and satisfies I trivially. For the inductive step, case-analyse the transitions of Figures S2–S3. T OOL -F AST , T OOL -G ATE -A CC /R EJ , M AP : by Rules R3 and R9 all outputs land in MU and only the constant acknowledgement (and, for gated calls, the decision) returns; the collection handle’s identifier is operation-determined and computed by P , not returned (Rule R1(b)). W-T YPED stores T; W-P ASTE is captured to MU. W-P has precondition b ∈ σP , hence E-level by the hypothesis, and stores at PC with empty scope. R ECALL -E returns E-bytes; R ECALL -O returns an opaque handle: uniformly for U-blocks, unknown, and nonexistent identifiers (Rules R1, R6, and the totality clause Rule R10(b)); RE -retrieval returns verbatim E-bytes (Rule R11). F ILL and C RYST return one handle and, by Rule R7, no branchable bit. D ISPLAY and E VICT inject nothing (E VICT replies the constant ack uniformly, Figure S3, convention (v)); previews are UI-side only (Rule R8(d)). C ONF -A CC /R EJ return only the decision (the null-gate abort returning the reject decision, Rule R6) and on accept produce an E-block. F AIL returns the constant fail token. P -generated content is a function of σP and the coins, hence E-level by the meet rule (Definition S2.1). By A1–A2 and convention (vii), no transition raises the level of any existing block; an accepting confirm writes a fresh E-block and leaves its untrusted source, if any, at U. Thus every delivery to P carries non-U provenance; the existence of extensionally equal untrusted copies is irrelevant. I is preserved.  

> 61 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Lemma S3.13 (Whitelist mediation, by tool class) . Every value a passed to any tool satisfies the following, according to the tool’s class. (Sensitive tools.) If tool ∈ Tools sens , at least one of: (i) a consists of readable 

E-bytes byte-equal to a stored T-block; (ii) a consists of readable E-bytes byte-equal to some C/CC -block b

with tool ∈ scope (b); (iii) the call is gated by an atomic confirm (a, tool ) displaying a’s resolved bytes (or, for a batch, every element’s bytes; or, for a null handle, the fixed no-value display, whereupon the call aborts). For a literal-expressed argument, in the baseline semantics of Rule R4(a) — before the formally instantiated Options O1–O2 are applied — case (iii) is the complement of the union of (i) and (ii): the fast path passes iff (i) or (ii) holds, and the gate fires otherwise. Under Option O1, a sensitive-tool argument satisfying case (i) may nevertheless gate (first use of the T-value per sensitive sink); Option O2 leaves this sensitive case analysis unchanged and modifies only the data-tool case (D) below; the option-modified analyses are Proposition S3.28’s. Option O3 is requirements-level, outside this lemma and outside Proposition S3.28 (O3). Cases (i) and (ii) themselves may hold simultaneously — the store may contain a T-block and an in-scope C/CC -block with identical bytes, a co-occurrence Option O1 manufactures by design — and either suffices for the baseline fast path; no consumer of this lemma uses exclusivity of (i) against (ii), each case being separately sufficient in Theorem S3.21. Handle-expressed arguments take case (iii) unconditionally (Handles clause below). (Data tools.) If tool ∈ Tools data , then either (D) a consists of readable E-level bytes held by P — that every byte value P holds carries non-U provenance, is Lemma S3.12’s contribution, not Rule R4(b) alone — which flow without confirmation (Rule R4(b)) — in particular PC -labelled and freshly P -composed E-bytes flow ungated at data tools; this is precisely the clause Option O2 hardens — or the call is gated per case (iii). (Handles.) Every handle argument, at every tool, falls under (iii): an ordinary handle via Rule R4(c); a collection handle via the batch gate of Rule R9, by the desugaring of Rule R1(e). (Under Option O2, case (D) is replaced by the clause-(a) case analysis applied at the data tool; Proposition S3.28.) (Machine-checked core: processArgs_spec and bytes_reply_isolation , §S9: an ungated flowed argument is a verbatim P -supplied literal, and at a sensitive tool it passes the whitelist test over ME; that P holds only E-level bytes in case (D) is bytes_reply_isolation ’s contribution; witness for case (D): Scenario 7. )

Proof.
