The owner’s creating confirmation of v, and any publish decision (itself federation-inert as an inspection, Definition S4.6), is a demand event, so every item has, in each epoch of its publication or re-entry, nlog  

> v

≥ 1 with its owner among its realized demanders; in the reference model the owner-coupling convention (§S6.1) mirrors this latently, so nv ≥ 1 holds in those epochs as well. A retired item is no longer surfaced (Rule R14), so no further confirmations accrue to its shared-tier ledger; the same bytes encountered later through a user’s own channels are ordinary personal content, counted in the single-user stream, not in v’s ledger (this ledger-residual demand is bounded pathwise by Theorem S6.2(i), persistence exceptions per INV-3; the aggregate expected per-user contribution of its benign, A11-conforming component vanishes under the vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii), while poisoned or assumption-violating demand remains in r(e)bad , Corollary S6.10). 

Definition S4.5 (Shared-presence freshness) . An attested identity’s inspection of a shared candidate v is 

fresh if the identity has no prior logged decision (a protocol-bearing decision: accepting endorsement, qualifying rejection, or panel verdict; Definition S4.6) on v’s exact bytes; otherwise it is repeat . Decisions are logged exactly when protocol-bearing (Definition S4.6), so freshness is shared-presence-first , not life-first: a personal decision on the same bytes taken outside any shared-tier presence (before first publication, or after retirement) is not logged and does not consume freshness — a disclosed boundary of the byte-permanence guarantee (INV-1); the behavioural parameters qpre , q pan , p b are accordingly read over the log-defined fresh class (theorem S5.19). Freshness is decidable from the federation log, is permanent while so defined, and never renews: across entries, dormancy, freezes, suspensions, and epochs within and across the bytes’ shared-tier presences, one logged decision per identity is final. All promotion weight and all panel verdicts are carried by fresh inspections (Definition S4.6, Rule R13(iii)); a repeat inspection can contribute only a rejection. This is INV-1; the safety of the asymmetry is INV-2 (rationale: theorem S4.7).  

> 73 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Definition S4.6 (Endorsement counting and banking) . A published item enters the promotion pipeline by submission (Rule R12); each pipeline entry opens a fresh finalisation window of length Twin at (re-)submission. A gate decision is protocol-bearing for a shared candidate iff all of the following hold: (i) its exact bytes have, at decision time, a shared-tier presence (a candidate or CC entry that is not retired; frozen and suspended presences included, since freeze- and suspension-time decisions are precisely the verdicts, corroborations, and bankable credits the counting rules consume); (ii) its target is a canonical tool in Tools sens ∪ Tools data or the canonical pseudo-target reasoning (§S4.1); (iii) the gate is not a covered gate (Rule R13(i)); and, if it is a cache confirmation of Options O1–O2, its outcome is rejecting — cache gates are accept-inert (their accepting branch is never protocol-bearing), while their rejecting branch on presence-coinciding bytes is protocol-bearing like any rejection (INV-2); (iv) its outcome is a durable accepting pair-confirmation or a rejecting review (Definition S2.5). Only protocol-bearing decisions are appended to the federation log as decisions on the candidate: only they consume shared-presence freshness, carry promotion weight, initiate or corroborate disputes, or become panel verdicts. A publish decision is logged as a submission event (P UBLISH -A CC , Rule R12), not as an inspection: it carries no promotion weight and consumes no freshness, while remaining a demand event for its item (Definition S4.4). All other confirmations — personal content — are purely local events with no federated effect and no freshness consequence for shared-tier counting. Cache confirmations are accept-inert regardless of whether their bytes coincide with a shared-tier presence: their accepting branch is never logged as a shared-candidate decision (no promotion weight, no credit, no notification cohort, no freshness consumption); their rejecting branch on presence-coinciding bytes remains a qualifying rejection like any other (INV-2), consuming freshness as any logged rejection does. (Their accepting branch, being unlogged, retains the identity’s freshness on the bytes; the acceptance itself carries no weight, and any later non-cache decision by the same identity is one more fresh decision inside the log-defined class the weakest-target parameters qpre , q pan , p b are already required to cover, theorem S5.19.) Inertness and coverage are decided by interpreter-only, attacker-immutable attributes (provenance, Definition S2.2; the owner-side coverage test, Rule R13(i)). (a) Once per identity per item, keyed to bytes, log-scoped, authority-gated. An identity’s first logged 

accepting decision on v’s bytes (Definition S4.5) is its one weight-bearing endorsement provided the identity holds dispute-initiation authority at the moment of the decision (not rejection-suspended, below its ceiling cceil , with a free rmax slot, and within its per-item initiation allowance, Rule R13(i),(v)); it is counted at the single tool that decision names and toward the pipeline entry open at the time of the decision, subject to the credit caps of clause (f). An accepting decision by an identity lacking that authority is logged, consumes freshness, and creates the decider’s local C copy (Lemma S6.1), but is authority-inert : it carries no promotion weight, is never credited (in-entry, banked, or vindication-credited), and joins no notification cohort. Authority is a deterministic predicate of the prior log (Rule R13(v)), evaluated interpreter-side at the decision’s header atom and recorded with it. Every later acceptance by the same identity (at any tool, in any entry, in any epoch, in any shared-tier presence) carries no weight (INV-1). (b) Banking. A first decision recorded during dormancy (window closed, not yet resubmitted or retired) or during a freeze with no open entry is banked : credited as endorsement weight at its tool when the owner’s next entry opens (subject to clause (f)), a deterministic relabel of the already-logged event, not a new confirmation and not a finalisation. Banked credits expire if the item retires first (INV-4). (c) Finalisation only at window end. Within an entry, v is promoted to CC at a specific tool when the weight of distinct, un-revoked accepting endorsers at that tool reaches W in credited endorsements (unweighted: 

N credited endorsers; crediting per clause (f)), drawn from at least Dpool diverse pools, with finalisation only at the end of the window; any qualifying rejection logged within the window intervenes. (d) Dormancy. Between entries the item remains surfaced and personally usable; a gate confirmation during dormancy is logged as a decision, consuming freshness, counted in the item’s ledger (Lemma S6.7),  

> 74 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

banked per (b), and creating the user’s local C copy, after which their gate never re-fires at that tool 

(INV-3). (e) Consequence. The weight-bearing endorsement sets of successive entries of the same bytes are identity-disjoint across the entire deployment history, and every weight-bearing endorsement is its endorser’s fresh inspection of the bytes (Lemma S5.3). (f) Credit caps (per entry, per tool). Crediting is greedy in log order and capped: a qualifying accepting decision (in-entry, or banked and credited at the entry’s opening in log order of the original decisions) is 

credited to the entry at its tool iff, at the moment of crediting, the entry holds fewer than ⌈N/D pool ⌉

credits from the decider’s pool at that tool (Definition S4.3) and fewer than N + := Dpool ⌈N/D pool ⌉

credits at that tool in total. Crediting requires authority at decision time (clause (a)); the caps bind over authority-active credits only. An uncredited accepting decision retains every personal effect (the decider’s local C copy, Lemma S6.1; freshness consumed, Definition S4.5) but carries no promotion weight, joins no notification cohort, and is never re-credited. The caps are promotion-neutral: a pool-capped decision never counted toward promotion under Definition S4.3 in any case, and the total cap binds only once the entry holds N + ≥ N credits which, under the per-pool cap, necessarily span at least 

Dpool pools — i.e. only when finalisation at window end is already enabled. Weighted deployments cap credited weight analogously (per pool and in total), bounding credited endorsers by O(W/w min ):constants change, never exponents (Remark S5.30). An endorsement is not a separate protocol act: it is the endorser’s own accepting confirmation of (v, tool ) at their gate (Theorem S3.21), signed and appended to the federation log. On re-entry, the interpreter notifies exactly the weight-bearing endorsers credited to the immediately preceding entry; no earlier cohort is ever re-notified, so the notified set per re-entry is bounded by that single entry’s credited endorsements, at most 

N + per demanded tool (clause (f)), independently of the item’s history. Notifications are deduplicated jointly with the suspension prompts of Rule R13(vi), at most one invitation per identity per item per epoch. An invitation is valid only in the epoch of its issuance: an unopened invitation expires at the epoch boundary and is not reissued for the same re-entry; acting on an invitation (accepting, rejecting, or declining) is an event of the issuance epoch. Narrowing the cohort weakens nothing: no safety bound relies on repeat inspections (INV-1, INV-2), and notifications carry no protocol authority. A notified endorser’s re-inspection is repeat: its accepting branch carries no weight, its rejecting branch is a qualifying rejection like any other (Rule R13(i)). Declining costs nothing (Theorem S6.2; theorem S4.7).                   

> Commentary (non-normative; role of the credit caps). Clause (f) supplies the counting the budget ledger consumes. Without it, finalisation only at window end would let a popular entry accrue credited endorsers without bound inside one window, and the weight-bearing row of Table S2, the notified re-entry cohort, and the reconsideration term of Lemma S6.7 would all grow with the item’s popularity. With it, credited endorsers per (entry, tool) number at most N+< N +Dpool ≤2N(using Dpool ≤N, Definition S4.3), deterministically; the overflow — in-window accepting demanders beyond the caps — is within-window demand, counted by the window-bucket row of Table S2 under A10’s demand clause. Safety is unaffected a fortiori: promotion still requires Ncredited fresh endorsements, Theorem S5.13 lower-bounds honest decisions (of which credits are a subset), rejections are never capped (INV-2), and promotion-neutrality (clause (f)) leaves Assumption A11’s sufficiency reading unchanged.

Remark S4.7 (Why the freshness asymmetry is safe) . The asymmetry of Definition S4.5 (repeat acceptances inert, repeat rejections still qualifying) is deliberate: a rejection can only freeze or corroborate, never promote (INV-2), so re-exposure of bytes to a previously fooled inspector can help the defence but never the attack, and the defence never rests on a person catching on a second logged look what they missed on the first: no logged repeat inspection carries weight. (Unlogged personal inspections outside a shared-tier presence are outside this discharge, Definition S4.5; their calibration consequence is theorem S5.19.) Banking is safe in both directions per INV-4 (as restated there: starting weight only, window’s rejection opportunity never bypassed, identity-disjointness preserved). Declining a re-entry notification costs the decliner nothing: a  

> 75 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

user who has personally confirmed holds a local in-scope C copy and reuses it at that tool without any gate (INV-3); the floor distinguishes such voluntary repeat acts from required confirmations (Theorem S6.2(ii), Remark S6.4). 

Definition S4.8 (Reach-free bound) . A safety bound is reach-free if it does not depend on the number of honest identities to whom the item is presented (its reach ), only on protocol constants and the adversary’s resources (INV-6), and the stated behavioural parameters qpre , q pan , read as weakest-target infima (INV-6). 

S4.2 Protocol rules 

The rules below are normative; all rationale lives in Appendix S7 under the keys cited, or in the marked commentary blocks of §S1.1. Clause numbering of Rule R13 preserves the roman numerals (i)–(vii) cited throughout. 

Rule R12 (Sharing eligibility and the publish gate) . Only C-labelled blocks with provenance confirmed 

(never cache , Options O1–O2) are eligible for submission, by a deterministic label-and-provenance member-ship test (provenance is interpreter-only, Definition S2.2, hence attacker-immutable); a model may nominate 

but cannot submit; submission requires the owner’s accepting confirm (v, publish) on the decision pane (P UBLISH -A CC ), recorded in the owner’s transcript, which emits one signed submit (v) event to the federa-tion log and creates the candidate’s shared-tier presence; it creates no local block and consumes no freshness (Definition S4.6). Each identity emits at most ρsub submission events per epoch; re-entry of an item into the promotion pipeline counts as a fresh submission, and a submission of bytes whose per-epoch entry cap is exhausted (Rule R14(i)) is ineligible. 

Required deployment parameters. A deployment instantiating Rule R13 fixes, as TCB configuration: the measurement window and minimum sample size of the charged-rejection rate trigger (v)(c); the suspension and cool-down durations of (v)(c) and (vii); the escalation schedule and the attestation-weight reduction function of (vii); the derivation-cascade relation of (iv); and the lifetime-account trigger of (v)(f) where selected. The theorems below consume only the explicit caps and threshold properties stated in their hypotheses ( rmax , cceil , θacc > 1 − pb, k, m, Emax , cp); no proof depends on the unspecified schedule shapes. 

Rule R13 (Dispute and demand-paced panel resolution) .

(i) Qualifying rejection = protocol-bearing gate rejection. Every attested identity’s rejecting decision on a shared candidate at its own protocol-bearing confirmation gate (Definition S4.6(iii): tool- or reasoning-target, non-covered; cache gates included on their rejecting branch) is automatically a signed, logged qualifying rejection. There is no separate filing step and no rejection weight floor: inspection and report are one act. A user who merely abandons the dialog records nothing (abstention is counted as a miss inside 1 − q; theorem S5.19). Each identity holds at most rmax open (unresolved) rejections. 

Covered gates are inert. A gate event fired by a handle-expressed use (Rule R4(c)) of a pair for which the identity already holds an in-scope local C copy — a covered gate ; a literal-expressed reuse takes the fast path (Rule R4(a)) and never gates — is protocol-inert for the shared tier, on both branches: its accepting decision carries no promotion weight, consumes no freshness, and joins no notification cohort (a redundant confirmation, §S6.1); its rejecting decision is logged as corroboration if the item is frozen and as an advisory flag otherwise — no dispute initiation, no verdict, no accountability exposure. Coverage is decided owner-side on the identity’s own local store, a deterministic test independent of any shared-tier or attacker-influenced state. Reasoning coverage. A gate event with target reasoning on bytes of which the identity’s local store holds any readable E-level block ( T, PC , or C of any scope; Rule R5, Definition S2.6) is likewise a covered gate, protocol-inert on both branches exactly as a tool-target covered gate: its accepting decision is a redundant confirmation (§S6.1) — no promotion weight, no freshness consumption, no notification cohort — and its rejecting decision is logged as corroboration if the bytes are frozen and as an advisory flag otherwise. The coverage test is owner-side  

> 76 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

and deterministic: it reads only the identity’s own local store’s readability facts, never any shared-tier or attacker-influenced state. Within an epoch, only an identity’s first rejecting decision on a given byte string can take effect as a dispute-initiating rejection; every later rejecting decision by the same identity on the same bytes within the epoch is logged as corroboration if the item is frozen, and otherwise as an advisory flag (the zero-authority class above): no protocol effect, no accountability exposure. The allowance renews at the epoch boundary. A rejecting decision that is verdict-eligible under clause (iii) is cast as a panel verdict regardless of this cap, exactly as under clause (v)(e); the cap restricts dispute 

initiation only, and capped rejections remain visible as flags. An identity whose rejection rights are under suspension (clause (v)) files advisory flags instead: no protocol effect, no penalty; flags alert other users. (ii) Disputed state. A qualifying rejection freezes promotion of v at every tool, immediately, and visibly flags the item. (A qualifying rejection on bytes holding CC status at any tool is instead a post-promotion rejection, routed to clause (vi).) A frozen item remains retrievable (surfaced flagged, Rule R14(ii)) and personally usable through each user’s own gate confirmation; the freeze suspends community promotion only. The item’s pre-dispute endorsers are notified. Pre-dispute endorsements are retained on the log for audit and reputation but carry no weight in resolution. (iii) Panel formation (demand-paced, fresh-juror). The panel is not a body convened in advance but a capped tally of decisions: it comprises the first k eligible verdicts logged on the disputed item, with no global deadline. Because a disputed item is unpromoted, every demander must personally inspect-and-confirm it to use it (Theorem S3.21); the interpreter displays the exact bytes at the demander’s own gate, visibly carrying the dispute flag and the rejecter’s stated ground. At the moment a demander records a decision, and only then, the interpreter checks eligibility on log facts: (a) attestation weight ≥ wseat ;(b) fresh (Definition S4.5); (c) no verdict already cast by this identity on this dispute instance; verdict eligibility is scoped to the dispute instance for its entire life: an identity casts at most one verdict on a given dispute, ever (INV-5); (d) at most ⌈k/D pool ⌉ verdicts per identity pool, likewise scoped per dispute instance; (e) fewer than k verdicts so far. A cache gate (Options O1–O2) is never a verdict surface: its rejecting decision may initiate or corrobo-rate a dispute per clause (i), but is not verdict-eligible and occupies no panel seat. (Only a cache gate’s rejecting branch is logged, Definition S4.6(iii); admitting such gates as verdict surfaces would make ver-dict membership outcome-selected there, against the benign-panel hypothesis of Proposition S5.20. The exclusion is theorem-compatible and resolves an otherwise outcome-selected surface: Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 bound tallies over verdicts actually cast, and the eligible class is simply defined without cache gates; no pathwise monotonicity is claimed.) A dispute instance opens at its qualifying rejection and closes at resolution or expiry; its verdict tally and pool caps persist unchanged across epoch boundaries (convention (C4)), and a boundary-spanning dispute counts toward Rule R14(i) in the epoch in which it opened. If eligible, that gate decision is the verdict , recorded once, verdicts ordered by log order of decisions; otherwise the decision is an ordinary personal confirmation (a bystander rejection still logs as corroboration). A user who opens the dialog and abandons it records nothing and occupies nothing: verdict capacity is consumed only by cast verdicts, so abandonment cannot exhaust or stall the panel, and no seat allocation, timer, or release mechanism exists. The dispute, like freshness, is byte-level: the panel adjudicates the bytes, each juror inspecting them at the juror’s own attempted tool, with the dispute flag and the rejecter’s stated ground displayed. Pre-dispute endorsers cast no verdicts: a notified endorser’s re-inspection is repeat (Definition S4.5); its accepting branch has no protocol effect, and its rejecting branch is a further qualifying rejection with no additional protocol effect while the  

> 77 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

item is frozen (logged as corroboration). Consequently every verdict-bearing inspection is the juror’s first logged decision on the bytes (Definition S4.5; INV-1, INV-2). (iv) Resolution (early and decisive). Verdicts are tallied as cast. The moment rejecting verdicts reach mrej :

evicted : demoted to U in the shared tier, cool-down, cascade to shared derivations. The moment accept-ing verdicts reach m: vindicated : the byte-level freeze lifts and the interrupted pipeline entry resumes, its window clock excluding the freeze; vindication consumes neither the owner’s rate limit nor an entry of Rule R14(i), and it never directly promotes. Each juror’s accepting verdict is simultaneously that juror’s gate confirmation at the juror’s own tool (its one fresh decision on the bytes), credited toward the entry’s per-tool endorsement sets subject to the authority gate and credit caps of Definition S4.6(a),(f); it joins those sets together with the retained pre-dispute endorsements, which carry no weight in resolution but keep their promotion weight, being fresh inspections the panel’s vindication never contradicted. Freeze-time bystander confirmations, fresh accepting gate confirmations by demanders whose decisions are not verdicts (under-weight, pool-capped, or post-capacity), are credited identically on vindication at their own tools (per Definition S4.6(a),(f)); crediting adds no confirmation event (Lemma S6.7). If no entry was open at the freeze, vindication lifts the freeze and the item returns to dormant status, its verdict-confirmations credited on the next entry’s opening subject to Definition S4.6(a),(f), or expiring with it (Definition S4.6(b), INV-4). Every promotion, after any number of disputes, therefore still requires N fresh-inspection endorsements at the promoted tool itself (Lemma S3.14). 

Default bars. The default bars are mrej = k + 1 − m with m > f s and mrej > f s (jointly forcing 

k ≥ 2fs + 1 ); their consequences (mutually exclusive outcomes and decisive, tally-deadlock-free resolution) are Lemma S5.7. Deployments choosing looser bars ( m + mrej ≥ k + 2 ) retain a neither-bar outcome, in which the item returns to unpromoted candidate status and re-entry consumes the owner’s rate limit (Rule R12) and gathers a fresh endorsement set. A disputed item that attracts no new demand for Texp expires (Rule R14(i)): retired unpromoted, all open rejection slots on it freed, no penalty to any party. (v) Rejecter accountability. 

(a) Panel verdicts are never penalised and never charged. (b) A dispute-initiating rejection is charged when its dispute ends in vindication, at the moment the vindication is logged, to the epoch in which that vindication is logged. Rejections whose disputes end in eviction or expiry charge nothing. (c) The first charge per identity per epoch carries no penalty. Repeated charges escalate to suspension of rejection rights only (endorsement weight and ordinary use untouched), restored after cool-down. Escalation is rate-based, never count-based: the trigger is the identity’s charged-rejection rate per fresh inspection , over a minimum sample size, at a threshold θacc calibrated strictly above the honest baseline 1 − pb (theorem S5.5). (d) Hard ceiling. Independently of (c): once cceil charges have accrued to an identity within an epoch, the identity’s further dispute-initiating rejections for the remainder of the epoch are filed as advisory flags (the zero-authority class of clause (i)); the allowance renews at the epoch boundary. (e) Suspension and the ceiling restrict dispute-initiating rejections only: a rejecting decision that is verdict-eligible under clause (iii) is cast as a panel verdict regardless of the identity’s account-ability state, so the panel arithmetic of Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 is independent of accountability state; freeze-time corroborating rejections, having no protocol effect, are logged either way. (f) Deployments may additionally maintain a lifetime charged-rejection account per identity, with the same per-inspection normalisation and a comparative (population-outlier) trigger, suspending rejection rights directly for extreme outliers. (g) Both layers touch rejection rights only, never endorsement weight or any local store (Lemma S5.8).  

> 78 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(vi) Post-promotion rejection. A qualifying rejection against a promoted item moves it to Suspended .Suspension invalidates the shared entry for the fast-path test of Rule R4(a) (§S4.1): community authority is withdrawn for the duration of the review, so every user’s use of the pair gates — except a user holding a personally confirmed in-scope local C copy, who continues through that copy (Lemma S6.1), unaffected. Suspension additionally imposes the byte-level freeze of clause (ii) on promotion at every tool. A fast-track panel (same parameters) attempts completion within Tpan ; failure to complete by Tpan leaves the item Suspended with verdict collection continuing demand-paced, exactly as an ordinary dispute. Its verdicts arise at the gates suspension re-arms: (a) Demanders during suspension: any user demanding the pair while the entry is suspended (new adopter and prior ungated adopter alike) gates, and verdict eligibility is decided by the log-fact check of clause (iii) exactly as in an ordinary dispute. In particular a prior ungated adopter with no logged decision on the bytes is fresh, while one with any prior logged decision (a rejection, or an acceptance at another tool) is repeat and ineligible, its rejecting branch a qualifying rejection like any other (logged as corroboration while frozen). (b) Prompted re-inspection: the suspension notification carries a voluntary re-inspection prompt to current adopters not presently demanding the pair; accepting it re-displays the exact bytes at the adopter’s own gate under the dispute flag, eligibility again per clause (iii); an accepting inspection earns the adopter a local C copy, covering all later use of the pair. Declining costs nothing beyond the suspension itself: the adopter’s next actual use of the pair gates once, at the floor (Corollary S6.5). Prompts are issued to at most cpk adopters per suspension; re-inspection invitations of either kind, re-entry notifications (Definition S4.6) and suspension prompts, are deduplicated jointly, at most one invitation per identity per item per epoch. Prompts expire at the end of their issuance epoch; a review left unresolved at the boundary continues demand-paced but issues no new prompts merely for having crossed it. At most Emax fast-track reviews are opened per item per epoch; once the cap is reached, the item remains Suspended for the remainder of the epoch, verdict collection continues demand-paced exactly as in an ordinary dispute, and further post-promotion rejections are logged as corroboration of the open review (the zero-authority class of clause (i): no protocol effect, no accountability exposure). The panel decides demotion-with-cascade or reinstatement. If k fresh verdicts do not arrive within 

Tpan , the item remains Suspended and verdict collection continues demand-paced, exactly as an ordinary dispute; a Suspended item attracting no fresh demand for Texp is retired from the shared tier (Rule R14(i)): no reinstatement, no eviction, no accountability consequence for either side, and every personally confirmed local copy untouched (Lemma S5.8). (vii) Publisher accountability. When a panel evicts an item, the publishing identity incurs an escalating penalty: the first eviction per identity per epoch triggers a submission cool-down; repeated evictions within an epoch escalate to suspension of submission rights and reduction of attestation weight. The penalty touches shared-tier rights and attestation weight only, never any local store, label, or scope (Lemma S5.8), and is triggered only by a resolved panel’s eviction verdict, never by rejections alone and never by expiry (Proposition S5.22).       

> Commentary (non-normative; role of clause (v)). Clause (v)’s induced deterministic bound cacc =cceil +rmax
> is Lemma S5.4; these clauses enter only the budget constants of Appendix S6, never any safety theorem (Figure S1). Calibration guidance and the honest cost analysis: theorem S5.5, theorem S5.19, S15.

Remark S4.9 (Per-pair dispute scoping (deployment variant)) . A deployment may scope disputes and panels per (value, tool) pair: a qualifying rejection then freezes promotion at the rejected pair only, and jurors are drawn from that pair’s demanders, adjudicating the rejected tool’s context. This removes the cross-tool-jury residual of S4: the applicable qpan becomes the targeted tool’s. The costs are per-pair panel formation and freeze scope, panels and prompts multiplying by up to ctool per item, and a re-keying decision — whether  

> 79 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

freshness and verdict capacity remain byte-level or become pair-level — that this document does not make; an analogous per-attempt bound is plausible but not formally instantiated here, the freshness and verdict-capacity re-keying being deliberately undecided. No theorem of this document consumes the variant. 

Rule R14 (Candidate hygiene) . The shared candidate store is curated by three deterministic, interpreter-applied policies. (i) Entry cap and expiry. At most Emax pipeline entries open per exact byte string per epoch — promoted, evicted, and unpromoted entries alike, republications and re-entries included; a submission of bytes whose per-epoch entry cap is exhausted is ineligible (Rule R12) and takes the uniform reject of P UBLISH -REJ , eligibility returning at the epoch boundary. The cap is availability-only: banked credits and any live promotion status persist across the boundary (convention (C4); N14). Additionally, a published candidate is retired from the shared tier when any of the following holds: (a) it completes Emax pipeline entries without promotion within the epoch (the count is cumulative across republications within the epoch and resets only at the epoch boundary); (b) it accumulates Emax non-vindicated disputes within the epoch (disputes ending in vindication are exempt; under the default bars this trigger fires only through the optional neither-bar outcome of Rule R13(iv); rationale: N15); (c) it remains dormant (window closed, not resubmitted) for Texp ;(d) it attracts no demand for Texp , including a disputed candidate attracting no new demand for Texp .Entries and dormant intervals alternate: each pipeline entry is followed by at most one dormant interval, ending at the next entry or at retirement; by the entry cap, entries per item per epoch number at most 

Emax and dormant intervals begun within the epoch at most Emax , deterministically (one further dormant interval may be carried across the boundary; the ledger’s conservative common bound is 

E⋆ = Emax + 1 , Lemma S6.7). Because the cap and the count of clause (a) are per epoch, a persistently demanded benign item may re-enter in later epochs; safety is unaffected, every federated safety bound being per-attempt (Definition S5.1) with lifetime exposure counted by Corollary S5.27. The owner’s local C copy is untouched (Lemma S5.8); the owner may republish, subject to Rule R12 and the entry cap. Retirement of a disputed item frees all open rejection slots on it and is neither an eviction nor a vindication: no accountability consequence for any party. (ii) Surfacing order. The ≤ ccand candidates a retrieval surfaces are ordered by trust status ( CC , then undisputed candidates by endorsement weight, then disputed items, flagged), computed on log facts, never by a model. (iii) Demotion hygiene. Items evicted by panel verdict are removed from retrieval entirely for the cool-down period. These policies only refuse entry openings, remove items, or reorder items in the shared tier; their safety- and budget-preservation is Lemma S5.9. 

S4.3 Federated assumptions, adversary, and TCB additions Assumption A7 (Federated endorsement transfer) . A Confirmed item endorsed at a specific tool by a qualifying weighted set of distinct attested identities is authoritative for that exact tool for any user (and likewise at the pseudo-target: a qualifying quorum at reasoning is authoritative for informing any user’s reasoning), provided the item is context-independent , a semantic property judged by the publishing owner and the inspecting community, not decided by the type system. Tool identifiers are canonical across the federation (TCB), so “the same tool” is well-defined; there is no transfer between tools. (Scope: as at §S2.1, endorsement unit; the baseline data-tool path is governed by Rule R4(b) and Corollary S3.23(b).) The type-level half is discharged by Lemma S5.2 and is not assumed. (All formal guarantees are integrity  

> 80 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

guarantees; semantic confidentiality of published content rests on the publish gate and is not separately proved: §S7.4.) 

Assumption A8 (Exogenous federation log) . In the two-run comparison of Theorem S3.25 extended to the federated setting (Proposition S5.10), the exogenous log prefix delivered at each step’s log slot (Definition S4.2) — every federation-log event not generated by the compared local run itself: other parties’ endorsements, rejections, promotions, suspensions, panel verdicts, and accountability events — is an exogenous input stream, coupled identically across the compared runs, exactly as A6 couples the user’s typed stream. The compared run’s own locally generated federation events (Definition S4.2) are not assumed coupled: their equality across the compared runs is derived in Proposition S5.10(ii) from the matched components, so the full delivered logs coincide without constraining either attack. The federated Reasoning Integrity statement therefore isolates the effect of unendorsed, undisplayed untrusted bytes; attacker influence exercised through legitimate log activity is governed not by that theorem but by the safety bounds of Appendix S5 (Theorems S5.11, S5.13). 

Assumption A9 (Verdict rate) . There is ρseat ∈ (0 , 1] that lower-bounds, uniformly over disputed items, times, and histories, the conditional probability, given all prior protocol events, that the next arriving demander of a disputed item casts an eligible verdict (Rule R13(iii)): weight ≥ wseat , fresh, no prior verdict on the dispute instance, within the pool caps, and recording a decision rather than abandoning. The bound is asserted jointly over behaviour and protocol state : eligibility is consumed endogenously (freshness spent, Definition S4.5; pool caps filled, Rule R13(iii)(d); capacity exhausted, (iii)(e)), so A9 entails in particular that the item’s remaining fresh, seat-weighted, pool-diverse demand does not run out while the dispute is open — a demand-composition property, not a psychological one. The bound is read per the conditioning convention of §S8.1: conditional on all logged atoms prior to the arrival, and uniformly over demand realisations.                        

> Commentary (non-normative; epistemic status and failure mode of A9). A9 has two components with different characters. The behavioural component (a demander who could cast an eligible verdict does so at rate
> ≥ρseat ) has the same epistemic status as qpre , q pan , p b. The structural component is stronger and can fail
> deterministically , with no human variation at all: freshness consumption and the pool caps are protocol mechanics, so an item pre-exposed to most of a pool’s identities, or whose demand is concentrated in few pools, can reach a state where no arriving demander is eligible — the conditional probability is then 0, and no behavioural assumption restores it. The consequences of failure are confined by design: every result resting on A9 is availability-side (Lemma S5.6, Lemma S5.24(ii), the ledger’s bucket rows, and through them the budget asymptotics); no safety theorem consumes it (Figure S1), the unresolved dispute stalls frozen (Rule R13(ii)), and each affected demander still pays at most the floor (Theorem S6.2(i), Lemma S5.24(ii)’s unconditional clause). Validation: verdict-eligibility rates on disputed items, stratified by pool and by the item’s prior exposure (N17). Quantitative consequences: Lemma S5.6. The eligible-verdict class excludes cache-gate decisions (Rule R13(iii)); ρseat is read over the class as so defined. qpre and ρseat condition on the same human act consistently: an abstaining demander is a miss inside 1−qpre and a non-verdict inside ρseat ; high abstention degrades resolution speed, never the validity of the safety parameter (theorem S5.19).

Adversary (federated). The adversary controls, over the deployment’s lifetime, f qualifying identities of total weight ≤ Wadv , of which at most fs are ever seat-eligible (static corruption, §S4.1). It does not control honest users’ decisions, the labelling, the interpreter, or the log. It may present a poisoned item to whom it likes, in what order it likes, and may stop or continue as a function of past outcomes, but never as a function of an inspection not yet performed: formally, its strategy is a predictable process with respect to the protocol filtration (§S8.1, convention (C2)), against which Theorem S5.13 is stated. Two structural constraints on its adaptivity (within-entry byte immutability, and the reading of q as a uniform bound over the honest identities the adversary can target) are recorded in §S8.1 and theorem S5.19. 

Definition S4.10 (Exogenous item classification) . Fix a classification Bad ⊆ V × (Tools sens ∪ Tools data ∪{reasoning }), exogenous to the protocol: no rule reads it, no transition depends on it, and no model computes it. An item v is poisoned if Bad (v, t ) holds for some tool t, and benign otherwise; “harmful at t” means  

> 81 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Bad (v, t ); harm at reasoning means content that misleads reasoning if adopted (A5). Every behavioural parameter ( qpre , q pan , p b, q c) and every safety and budget statement of Appendices S5–S6 is read relative to this classification; the classification is a modelling device for stating the bounds, not a protocol object, and the protocol’s behaviour is identical under any choice of it. 

TCB additions (federated). Beyond §S2.5: the attestation layer and its weight assignment on crypto-graphic facts; the pool assignment of Definition S4.3; canonical tool identifiers shared and attested across the federation; and the log service of convention (C3) (sequencing and authenticated history). The Sybil conditions (finiteness of Wadv and the seat margin m > f s) require an attestation authority or identity cost Douceur (2002); Dwork & Naor (2007). Because f , Wadv , and fs are lifetime totals (§S4.1), they are enforceable only to the extent that the attestation or identity-cost mechanism supplies certified lifetime bounds: the issuance policy must bound the identities and weight any party can ever acquire, and that bound is what the constants denote. Where issuance is open or uncosted, no finite f exists and the federated safety results are compatibility statements — valid for whatever lifetime totals the deployment can certify — while the single-user layer and the cap (Theorems S3.21, S3.25, S6.2) are unaffected, consuming no Sybil bound. 

S5 Federated Guarantees 

This appendix proves the guarantees of the protocol of Appendix S4: the structural lemmas (§S5.1), then the integrity lifting, the deterministic safety theorem, panel safety, wrongful eviction, publisher safety, denial-of-service, and submission-budget results (§S5.2). Dependencies are as in Figure S1. 

S5.1 Structural lemmas Definition S5.1 (Promotion attempt) . Fix a byte string v. A promotion attempt is a maximal segment of one shared-tier presence of v beginning at an anchor and ending at the first finalisation thereafter — the close of a pipeline entry’s window at its full length, where promotion is decided (Definition S4.6(c)) — or, where no finalisation occurs, at the end of the presence (retirement, or demotion by panel eviction) or at a loose-bar neither-bar resolution returning the item to candidate status (Rule R13(iv)). Anchors are: the presence-creating submission event (first attempt), and each attempt-ending event just listed other than the end of the presence (each later attempt). A window closed early at an epoch boundary is not a finalisation (convention (C4)): it banks its accrued endorsements to the item’s next entry, which belongs to the same 

attempt. Anchors are log events, and every decision credited to an attempt’s entries — in-window, banked in a dormant or frozen interval, boundary-banked, or vindication-credited (Definition S4.6(b),(f), Rule R13(iv)) — is logged strictly after the attempt’s anchor: in-window and interval decisions follow the preceding anchor by construction, and boundary-banked decisions were logged in an earlier entry of the same attempt, itself opened after the anchor. Likewise no ordinary dispute and no fast-track review is open at any anchor: either state imposes the byte-level freeze (Rule R13(ii),(vi)), which interrupts a window rather than letting it finalise (Rule R13(iv)), and retirement frees all open rejection slots (Rule R14(i)); so every dispute or review bearing on an attempt opens, and casts all its verdicts, after the attempt’s anchor. Attempts partition each presence; every entry belongs to exactly one attempt (the attempt whose anchor precedes its opening); at most one entry of an attempt lies in any single epoch (a second entry of the same attempt arises only across an epoch boundary, via a boundary-closed window); and only an attempt’s final entry can finalise. An attempt succeeds if its finalisation is a promotion; an attempt with no finalisation — ending at retirement, eviction, or a neither-bar resolution, or with no entry ever opened — succeeds with probability zero. Attempts whose finalisation lies in a given epoch number at most the epoch’s entries of the item, hence at most Emax per item (Rule R14(i)), each such finalising entry opened by a rate-limited (re-)submission of the same epoch (Rule R12; windows are epoch-contained, convention (C4)). Dually, the attempts anchored in a given epoch are begun at that epoch’s presence-creating submissions and finalisation  

> 82 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

events (under the default bars; under loose bars also its neither-bar resolutions), which is the counting Corollary S5.27 consumes.     

> Commentary (non-normative; role of the attempt unit). The attempt is the analysis unit at which the per-submission safety bound is conditioned. Banked decisions credited to an entry precede the entry’s opening, and the owner — or an adversary — may decide whether to open the entry as a function of the banked outcomes; a bound conditioned at the entry’s opening would therefore condition on an adversarially selected event (an optional-selection error). The attempt’s anchor precedes every decision creditable to its entries by construction, so conditioning at the anchor is sound; and an attempt whose intervals bank decisions is an attempt whether or not a further entry opens, so the choice not to open is a choice among attempts already counted, at success probability zero. No protocol rule reads attempts; like the ledger’s charging convention (Lemma S6.7), they are a hindsight bookkeeping device over the same window/interval alternation the ledger’s buckets partition.

Lemma S5.2 (Type-level sharing exclusion) . No T-block, no U-block, no cache-marked block, and no block lacking an accepting publish confirmation by its owner ever enters the shared candidate store. Proof. Label and provenance membership are deterministic and attacker-immutable (A1–A2; provenance is interpreter-only, Definition S2.2); the publish gate of Rule R12 is a confirmation event under Rule R8 and A3; nomination has no authority. 

Lemma S5.3 (Entry disjointness) . For a fixed byte string, the weight-bearing endorsement sets of distinct pipeline entries are disjoint in identities over the deployment’s entire history, and every weight-bearing endorsement (in-entry or banked-and-credited) is its endorser’s fresh, authority-active inspection of the bytes (Definition S4.6(a)). Proof. By Definition S4.6(a), an identity’s weight-bearing endorsement of the bytes is its first logged decision on them (Definition S4.5), taken while authority-active (Definition S4.6(a)), (when credited, Definition S4.6(f)), counted toward exactly one entry (the entry open at decision time, or, via banking, Definition S4.6(b), the next entry to open). Every later acceptance by the same identity is repeat and carries no weight (INV-1). Hence no identity contributes weight to two entries, and each contribution is by construction a fresh inspection. 

Lemma S5.4 (Deterministic accountability bound) . Fix an identity and an epoch. The identity’s dispute-initiating rejections filed within the epoch that both take protocol effect and are eventually charged (contra-dicted by vindication), in this epoch or any later one, number at most cacc := cceil + rmax .Proof. By Rule R13(v)(b), a rejection is charged at the moment its vindication is logged. Suppose first that the epoch’s cceil -th charge to this identity occurs, at some moment T . From T onward, the identity’s further dispute-initiating rejections in the epoch are filed as advisory flags with no protocol effect (Rule R13(v)(d)), hence are never counted. Every counted rejection is therefore either charged no later than T (and the epoch’s charges up to and including T number exactly cceil , by the choice of T ) or still open at T and charged afterward; open rejections number at most rmax at any moment (Rule R13(i)). The two classes are disjoint, a rejection being charged exactly once; summing gives cceil + rmax . If instead fewer than cceil charges accrue within the epoch, at most cceil − 1 counted rejections are charged within it, and every one charged in a later epoch is open at the epoch boundary, at most rmax again. 

Remark S5.5 (Calibration of rejecter accountability) . The rate trigger of Rule R13(v)(c) is normalised per fresh inspection , never per rejection. The normalisation matters: when poison is rare, almost all of an honest inspector’s rejections are wrongful rejections of benign items and almost all are vindicated, so charges 

per rejection approach one for honest and adversarial identities alike and cannot separate them; charges 

per inspection sit near 1 − pb for an honest identity and near its full rejection rate for a campaign. The ceiling cceil is calibrated, like the rate trigger, strictly above the honest baseline: an honest identity accrues charges at rate at most 1 − pb per benign fresh inspection, and its fresh inspections per epoch are bounded  

> 83 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

by its own required-confirmation stream (small, and shrinking with population, Appendix S6), so cceil set at a high quantile of (1 − pb)×(honest per-epoch fresh-inspection count), with margin, is reached by an honest identity only rarely; an honest identity that does reach it retains advisory flags and ordinary use — though its acceptances are authority-inert for the duration (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18) — loses nothing permanent, is restored at the epoch boundary, and its inspections during that state are excluded trials, not misses (theorem S5.19), so no safety statement is affected. Renewal semantics: convention (C4) (the accountability allowances are the only freshness-like quantities that renew). (This calibration concerns the per-fresh-inspection rejection-rate trigger only; suspension-induced required confirmations are priced separately, once per demander, per Note N14.) Mis-calibration risk: S15. 

Lemma S5.6 (Verdict pacing under A9) . Under A9, for any disputed item and any adversary strategy (convention (C2)): the number of demanders who arrive while the dispute is unresolved is stochastically dominated by the index of the (k+1) -st success in an i.i.d. Bernoulli( ρseat ) sequence, i.e. by a sum of k+1 

independent Geometric( ρseat ) variables. In particular its expectation is at most κ := ( k+1) /ρ seat , and for any c0 > 1 the probability that more than c0 κ demanders arrive while the dispute is unresolved is at most 

e−λc0 k for a constant λc0 > 0 depending only on c0 and ρseat .Proof. Index trials by arrivals: let Oi be the event that an i-th demander of the item arrives while the dispute is unresolved, measurable at that arrival’s rendering atom, and let Zi indicate that this arrival’s disposition is an eligible verdict on the dispute instance. Under the atomic reading of §S8.1, each arrival’s disposition is realized at its own log position, so the σ-algebra of atoms prior to the i-th rendering atom contains the dispositions and protocol effects of all earlier arrivals; A9 gives P[Zi = 1 | G i] ≥ ρseat on Oi at exactly these σ-algebras, and Lemma S8.2(ii), in the form of Remark S8.4, supplies i.i.d. Bernoulli( ρseat )

variables Yi with Yi ≤ Zi on Oi. A dispute instance accepts at most k eligible verdicts in its entire life (Rule R13(iii)(e)), and every verdict is cast at an arrival; hence on On, P 

> i≤n−1

Zi ≤ k, so P 

> i≤n−1

Yi ≤ k,i.e. n ≤ G := min {j : P 

> i≤j

Yi = k + 1 }. The arrival count is therefore pathwise at most G, a sum of k+1 independent Geometric( ρseat ) variables. Linearity gives the expectation; the tail is the Chernoff bound for sums of independent geometrics. (Because dispositions resolve at arrival, the sharper count P 

> i≤n−1

Zi ≤ k − 1 in fact holds while the dispute is unresolved, improving κ to k/ρ seat ; we retain the conservative κ = ( k+1) /ρ seat , which every downstream constant consumes, the slack being monotone.) 

Lemma S5.7 (Decisive resolution under the default bars) . Under the default bars mrej = k + 1 − m with 

m > f s and mrej > f s: (i) eviction and vindication are mutually exclusive; (ii) every panel that gathers k

verdicts reaches exactly one bar, and the first bar reached in log order is the bar reached by the full tally of k

verdicts: early resolution changes timing, never the outcome; (iii) both bars are reachable by honest verdicts alone, and the adversary’s at most fs verdicts (INV-5) alone reach neither; hence no tally deadlock exists once k verdicts are gathered, and neither bar can be reached by the adversary’s verdicts alone; whether k

eligible verdicts arrive is the separate, availability-side matter of A9 (Remark S5.21). Proof. (i)–(ii) m + mrej = k + 1 , so k verdicts cannot meet both bars and must meet at least one; verdict counts are non-decreasing along the verdict sequence, so a bar is reached at some prefix iff it is reached by the full tally. (iii) From k = m + mrej − 1 with m, m rej > f s: k − fs ≥ m and k − fs ≥ mrej , so honest verdicts alone suffice for either bar; and fs < m means the adversary’s at most fs verdicts alone cannot reach the m-bar, and symmetrically fs < m rej for the other bar. 

Lemma S5.8 (Third-party immutability of local stores) . For every user u and every block b in u’s local store (the user-owned tier: T-, C-, PC -, and U-blocks; community status lives in the shared tier and is referenced, never stored, locally, §S4.1), no protocol event initiated by any identity other than u alters bytes( b), ℓ(b),or scope (b). Dispute, suspension, vindication, demotion, eviction, expiry, and every accountability penalty of Rule R13(v),(vii) act on the shared tier alone: they invalidate or flag shared entries, restrict shared-tier rights, or reduce attestation weight. Demotion of a CC item withdraws community authority from users who have not personally confirmed it, whose gates simply fire again, within the single-user cap of Theorem S6.2,  

> 84 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

and leaves every personally confirmed C copy intact (with a notification). Suspension likewise withdraws community authority for the review’s duration (Rule R13(vi)), equally without touching any local block. Consequently no third-party protocol transition ever directly alters or removes any block of any user’s local store; first-party removal by the owner’s own agent ( evict , including evictions the agent performs in reaction to notifications or other legitimate log events) is the disclosed residual N13. The induced cost bound, that no campaign pushes any user above the single-user cap, is Theorem S6.2, which rests on the present lemma. Proof. By Figures S2–S3, local stores mutate only via owner-invoked Σ-operations and owner confirmations (A1–A2); evict is owner-only (first-party eviction by the owner’s own agent is a disclosed residual: N13, §S7.4). Community status is shared-tier state (§S4.1), so demotion and suspension update the shared entry and never touch a local block; promotion, demotion, and expiry are interpreter relabels or retirements of the shared log entry (Rules R13, R14); the accountability penalties of clauses (v) and (vii) act on rejection rights, submission rights, and attestation weight, none of which is a local-store attribute; the cascade quarantines shared derivations, not local stores; notifications are advisory. 

Lemma S5.9 (Hygiene preserves safety through bound uniformity) . The policies of Rule R14 preserve every theorem of this document. The entry cap only refuses entry openings, and retirement and demotion hygiene only remove shared-tier items; none of these can enable a promotion. Surfacing reordering is not monotone by event-set inclusion — reordering, and removal of one candidate, can increase another item’s reach within the ccand -capped surface — but the safety bounds are unaffected because Theorems S5.11 and S5.13 are uniform over adaptive presentation schedules and reach-free (INV-6): any demand redistribution hygiene induces is one more presentation schedule inside the quantified class. Lemma S5.8 is unaffected because no local store is touched; the ledger of Appendix S6 is unaffected at the moment of any hygiene action, which adds no confirmation event. Retirement does redirect later demand: a user who encounters the retired bytes afterwards through their own channels confirms them as ordinary personal content, in the single-user stream (Definition S4.4), bounded by the cap (Theorem S6.2), its benign, A11-conforming component vanishing in expectation under the vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii) and the remainder entering 

r(e)bad (Corollary S6.10); the shared-tier ledger rows are never increased by removal or reordering. Proof. Each policy is a deterministic interpreter action on the shared tier: the entry cap refuses an entry opening, retirement removes a candidate, surfacing order permutes at most ccand surfaced candidates, demotion hygiene removes an evicted item from retrieval. None relabels any local block, forces any confirmation, or adds any promotion path. For the safety theorems, the relevant quantification is over adaptive presentation and demand schedules (convention (C2)); the schedules realizable under any hygiene policy are a subset of that class, and the bounds of Theorems S5.11 and S5.13 are uniform over it and independent of reach, so they apply verbatim to the hygiene-modified deployment. The post-retirement reclassification is Definition S4.4’s retirement clause. 

S5.2 Main theorems Proposition S5.10 (Delegated Action Integrity and federated Reasoning Integrity) . Assume A7 and A8, and read the interpreter over federated product configurations (Definition S4.1) under the federated step of Definition S4.2, with Definition S3.3(iii) in its federated form (every virtual CC authority in force backed by a currently valid promotion certificate on L). Theorem S5.11 (Deterministic safety under perfect honest detection) . Under A1–A5 and Rules R12–R13, with perfect honest detection ( q = 1 : every honest inspection of the poisoned item rejects): if W > W adv 

and m > f s, then no poisoned item (Definition S4.10) is ever promoted to CC at any tool, at any population size and under any attack, through either the ordinary or the panel path. Proof. With q = 1 , every accepting endorsement and every accepting panel verdict on a poisoned item is adversarial. The ordinary path requires accepting weight at least W at the tool in question, while the adversary’s weight is at most Wadv and W > W adv : impossible. The panel path requires at least m accepting verdicts, while the adversary casts at most fs verdicts (INV-5), m > f s, and honest accepting verdicts are zero: impossible. Vindication-credited and dormancy-banked endorsements are covered identically: with 

q = 1 every honest decision on the poison rejects, so every credited acceptance is adversarial and already counted inside Wadv (respectively inside the fs verdict cap). Deterministic and population-free. 

Remark S5.12 (Scope of Theorem S5.11) . The hypothesis q = 1 is a behavioural idealization, the strongest in this document, not the absence of one: the Sybil conditions W > W adv , m > f s exclude adversarial 

weight, while honest acceptances of the poison are excluded only by q = 1 itself. The theorem is therefore 

not a fallback for the unrecognizable-poison regime q → 0, where honest inspectors themselves supply the threshold and no community-truthfulness guarantee is available within a human-judgment design. What survives that regime is the local layer — personal Action Integrity, the single-user cap, and no-deletion (Theorem S3.21, Theorem S6.2, Lemma S5.8) — together with the one-sided detection clause (a single honest effective qualifying rejection , whenever one occurs, freezes promotion immediately, Rule R13(i),(v)) and re-verification against never-decided inspectors (N16). See S14.  

> 86 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Theorem S5.13 (Panel safety under adaptive presentation) . Adopt Rules R12–R13 with the default bars of Rule R13(iv) ( mrej = k+1 − m, m > f s, mrej > f s; Lemma S5.7) and the probabilistic framework of §S8.1. Suppose: (a) ordinary promotion at a tool requires N distinct accepting endorsements counted toward one pipeline entry (Definition S4.6), with N > f ;(b) at most fs of the adversary’s identities are seat-eligible, with m > f s;(c) there are qpre , q pan ∈ (0 , 1] such that: each honest fresh, authority-active (Definition S4.6(a)) inspection of the poisoned item at a non-covered, non-cache confirmation gate ends in an effective qualifying rejection — a recorded rejecting decision that takes protocol effect under Rule R13(i) — with conditional probability at least qpre ; and each honest eligible verdict on the item rejects with conditional probability at least qpan , conditionally on the verdict being cast , i.e. at the verdict’s header-inclusive σ-algebra (§S8.1) (Rule R13(iii)). Both bounds are read per the conditioning convention of §S8.1 (all prior logged atoms; the demand realisation), and both are uniform lower bounds over the honest identities and presentation contexts the adversary can target — the catch rate of the most fallible targetable honest cohort, not a population average (theorem S5.19) — qpre ’s contexts are the unfrozen candidate contexts — in-window and candidate-dormancy inspections, any advisory-flag state — where an effective qualifying rejection is possible (Rule R13(i),(ii)); freeze- and suspension-time fresh inspections carry no initiation and belong to qpan ’s province where verdict-eligible, their non-verdict rejections being corroborations, consumed by no trial of this theorem. qpan ’s contexts include the freeze itself: verdicts are cast while the item is frozen. Both bounds are read over the target contexts (tools and reasoning ) in which the trials actually occur: qpan is a uniform bound over the tool contexts of eligible jurors , which under byte-level panels may include tools at which the item is innocuous — for tool-targeted poison the applicable qpan may therefore be small (S4). Authority-inert inspections, covered gates, and cache gates (Options O1–O2) belong to no trial class of this theorem: an authority-inert acceptance carries no promotion weight and an authority-inert rejection no initiation (Definition S4.6(a)), covered gates are protocol-inert on both branches (Rule R13(i), theorem S5.19), and cache gates are accept-inert and verdict-ineligible (Definition S4.6(iii), Rule R13(iii)) — their rejecting branch is one more effective qualifying rejection, which can only deny the case-(a) path — so promotion still requires N − f honest authority-active fresh non-cache credits, and qpre is quantified over exactly the class whose members can both accept with weight and reject with effect. Abstention by an authority-active fresh inspector records nothing and is counted as a miss inside 1 − qpre ; the two parameters deliberately live at different conditioning levels — qpre at the disposition of a fresh inspection, where abstention must count as a miss; qpan at a cast verdict, where abstention is already excluded by eligibility — and qpre ≤ qpan is typical but not assumed; (d) the panel margin is positive: δ := m−fs 

> k−fs

− (1 − qpan ) > 0.Then for any poisoned (Definition S4.10) submission, every promotion attempt (Definition S5.1) succeeds with probability at most the display below, conditionally on the σ-algebra generated by all atoms up to and including the attempt’s anchor atom , uniformly over adaptive presentation, stopping, and re-entry strategies and regardless of how many users the item is shown to (INV-6), 

P[promotion ] ≤ (1 − qpre )N −f + PBin( k − fs, 1 − qpan ) ≥ m − fs



≤ (1 − qpre )N −f + e−2δ2(k−fs).

Remark S5.14 (Scope of Theorem S5.13) . Theorem S5.11 is the special case q = 1 . The bound covers a single promotion attempt (Definition S5.1); for attempts anchored while the bytes hold live CC status at some tool (additional-tool promotion of already-promoted bytes, possible only after a prior success), hypothesis (c) is read with qpre ’s contexts extended per the closing paragraph of the proof; first-promotion attempts need no extension; entries embed injectively into attempts, so per-entry counting statements read a fortiori; attempts  

> 87 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

are counted at their anchors, and the per-anchor-epoch union bound taken, in Corollary S5.27. No hypothesis is placed on repeat inspections: none carries promotion weight or a verdict (INV-1, INV-5; Remark S4.7). The panel arithmetic rests on INV-5 alone, which is stated independently of the freshness rule precisely so that it never depends on INV-1. The default bars are a hypothesis of the theorem: case (b) uses decisive resolution (Lemma S5.7) to cap honest verdicts at k − a. Under the looser bars permitted by Rule R13(iv) (m + mrej ≥ k + 2 ), the same argument bounds the vindication event verbatim — vindication still requires 

m accepting verdicts among at most k, so the case-(b) term stands with k − fs trials — and the additional neither-bar outcome returns the item to unpromoted candidate status, whose re-entry opens a new attempt with an identity-disjoint credited set, counted separately and marginally by Corollary S5.27. The bound therefore extends to loose-bar deployments per entry; only the decisiveness clauses (mutual exclusivity, tally-deadlock-freeness) are specific to the defaults. 

Remark S5.15 (Single-parameter reading) . Every appearance of a single q elsewhere in this document (Theorem S5.11’s hypothesis q = 1 ; the corridor row q + pb > 1; Corollary S5.27; the composed summary of §S7.5; the correlated rate qc of S13) is read as q := min( qpre , q pan ): the bounds of Theorem S5.13 are monotone in each parameter, so the single-q forms are the special case qpre = qpan = q and remain valid verbatim. q = 1 means both acts are perfect; q → 0 (S14) is the regime where the smaller of the two vanishes. 

Remark S5.16 (Itemwise reading; heterogeneous item difficulty) . Theorem S5.13 is stated and proved per promotion attempt (Definition S5.1): nothing in its trials couples distinct items, so its hypotheses and bound are itemwise — for each poisoned item v, the bound holds with v’s own weakest-target rates qpre (v), q pan (v),and it is monotone in each. Heterogeneous item difficulty is therefore inside the model, transparently: easily recognized poison contributes a negligible term; near-unrecognizable poison ( qpre (v) → 0) degrades its own term toward the disclosed residual S14, leaving every other item’s bound untouched. The per-anchor-epoch union bound of Corollary S5.27 holds in the same itemwise form, P

> e

(1 − qpre (ve)) N −f + e−2δ(ve)2(k−fs)

(terms with δ(ve) ≤ 0 read as the exact binomial tail), the single-q display being the uniform special case; a deployment-level constant is thus dominated by the hardest items the adversary can mint — exactly the calibration duty of S1. No parametric ability-or-difficulty model (jury, psychometric, or crowdsourcing) is assumed: the weakest-target conditional floor subsumes any such structure, at the price that the floor, not the average, is the deployed constant (theorem S5.19, N17). 

Remark S5.17 (The certified per-attempt ceiling Batt ). Write 

Batt := (1 − qpre )N −f + PBin( k − fs, 1 − qpan ) ≥ m − fs



for the right-hand side of Theorem S5.13’s bound in its exact binomial form, and call its two summands the ordinary term and the panel term . The word certified names this analytic bound and is unrelated to the promotion certificates of Definition S4.1, which are log objects. Three properties are used throughout the empirical evaluation: the first two are immediate from the display, the third from the theorem’s statement. The panel term is a floor : at fixed panel geometry (k, m, f s) and fixed qpan it does not contain N , so raising the endorsement quorum shrinks only the ordinary term and Batt can never fall below it — panel geometry, not quorum, is the binding deployment parameter, which is the frontier Proposition S5.20 reads against and which the corridor of §S6.5 moves only by scheduling k itself. The two terms fall in different protocol constants and must be pushed down independently: the ordinary term geometrically in N − f , the panel term as a binomial tail in k − fs whose Hoeffding relaxation e−2δ2(k−fs) is exponential in that constant. And Batt is per promotion attempt (Definition S5.1) and reach-free (INV-6); deployment-wide exposure is obtained by summing over anchored attempts and byte-distinct variants, through the union, expectation and deterministic-cap forms of Corollary S5.27 and the lifetime ceiling of Remark S5.29, never by reading the per-attempt figure as a per-epoch one. 

Reporting discipline. Any numerical value quoted for Batt is the exact binomial tail above, never the Hoeffding relaxation that Theorem S5.13 displays alongside it. The two differ materially, and by different factors on the two sides of the design: at k = 31 , m = 20 , fs = 1 with qpan = 0 .6 the relaxation is  

> 88 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

3.81 × 10 −2 against an exact panel term of 8.30 × 10 −3, a factor of about 4.6; on the benign side of Proposition S5.20, at pb = 0 .9 and the same δ, the numerically identical relaxation stands against an exact wrongful-eviction tail of 8.91 × 10 −5, a factor of about 430 . A reader recomputing either relaxation from a quoted exact value will therefore not reproduce it, and — because the two relaxations coincide numerically while their exact counterparts differ by two orders of magnitude — the two slacks must never be interchanged. 

Proof of Theorem S5.13. Fix the attempt and condition on the σ-algebra through and including its anchor atom (Definition S5.1). The behavioural bounds of hypothesis (c) are asserted uniformly over histories, so the argument restarts after any fixed anchor-inclusive history; every trial charged below occurs strictly after the anchor, and each trial’s own conditioning σ-algebra (§S8.1) refines the anchor-inclusive one. Partition by whether an effective qualifying rejection was logged after the anchor and before the attempt’s finalisation. Throughout, the bytes’ promotion status at every tool is constant within the attempt: promotion occurs only at a finalisation, which ends the attempt, and demotion by eviction ends the presence (Definition S5.1, Definition S4.6(c)), so whether the bytes hold live CC status at any tool is decided at the anchor. Suppose first that they do not — the case of every attempt preceding the bytes’ first promotion, hence of every attempt the ever-promoted events of Corollary S5.27 require; the complementary case is scoped at the close of the proof. Then no suspension can arise within the attempt (suspension presupposes promoted bytes, Rule R13(vi)), every qualifying rejection on the bytes within it is dispute-initiating (Rule R13(i),(ii)), and every non-freeze piece of the attempt is an entry window or a candidate-dormancy interval (presence taxonomy, Lemma S6.7): every unfrozen header within the attempt is an unfrozen candidate context in the sense of hypothesis (c). 

(a) Promotion with no qualifying rejection. By Rule R13(i),(v), an honest authority-active identity’s rejecting gate decision on an unfrozen candidate takes protocol effect, so the case-(a) event entails that no honest authority-active inspection of the item ended in an effective qualifying rejection before the attempt’s finalisation. Promotion at the finalisation requires N credited accepting endorsements at the finalising entry (in-window, interval-banked, boundary-banked, or vindication-credited, Definition S4.6), of which at least 

N − f come from honest identities; each is a distinct honest identity’s fresh, authority-active, non-covered decision on the bytes (Lemma S5.3, Definition S4.6(a)), and each is logged strictly after the attempt’s anchor and before its finalisation (Definition S5.1). Index the trials by log order within the attempt: let Oi be the event that an i-th protocol-bearing decision at a non-covered, non-cache gate on the item, by an honest, fresh, authority-active identity, is logged after the attempt’s anchor and before the attempt ends, at an unfrozen header — by the opening reduction, an unfrozen candidate context in the sense of hypothesis (c). Oi is measurable at that record’s header atom (§S8.1): attempt membership is decided by the anchor, a past log event, and freshness, coverage, authority, and frozen status are deterministic predicates of the prior log (Definitions S4.5, S4.6(a), Rule R13(i),(ii)) — no trial’s occurrence depends on the attempt’s future. Because no event interleaves between a position’s rendering and header atoms (§S8.1), the status at the header is the status under which hypothesis (c)’s disposition-level bound is read. Let Zi indicate that the trial’s content is an effective qualifying rejection. Hypothesis (c) lower-bounds by qpre the disposition-level probability of such a rejection at the trial’s rendering atom, on all of Oi

and irrespective of whether case (a) occurs — the class is confined to exactly the unfrozen candidate contexts over which qpre is quantified; conditioning on a recorded decision only raises the bound (Lemma S8.1(iii)). Hence P[Zi = 1 | G i] ≥ qpre on Oi at the header-inclusive σ-algebras of the conditioning convention (§S8.1), each of which refines the anchor σ-algebra, so Remark S8.4 applies under the anchor-conditional measure directly. Only now is the case-(a) event used. On it, no effective qualifying rejection is logged after the anchor and before the attempt’s finalisation; since no dispute or review is open at the anchor (Definition S5.1) and every freeze arising within the attempt presupposes such a rejection, every trial of the class preceding the finalisation occurs at an unfrozen header, and the N − f honest credited endorsements — in-window, interval-banked in a candidate-dormancy interval, or boundary-banked, by the opening reduction; a vindication-credited endorsement presupposes a panel, hence a case-(b) run — are all logged at trials of the class, each necessarily  

> 89 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

non-cache (cache acceptances are never logged, hence never credited, Definition S4.6(iii)). The case-(a) success event is therefore contained in ON −f ∩ { Z1 = · · · = ZN −f = 0 }: at least N − f trials of the class are logged before the finalisation, so the first N − f trials in log order all precede it, and each has 

Zi = 0 , an effective qualifying rejection before finalisation being a case-(b) run. The joint all-zeros bound of Lemma S8.2(ii), in the σ-algebra form of Remark S8.4, gives P[case (a) | anchor ] ≤ (1 − qpre )N −f .Additional reach creates both rejection opportunities and possible weight-bearing acceptances (INV-2); no pathwise monotonicity in reach is claimed. What the bound uses is only the N − f honest fresh authority-active trials promotion itself requires, so the stated bound is unchanged at every reach (INV-6). 

(b) Promotion after a qualifying rejection. The byte-level freeze then blocks every tool’s pipeline until an accepting panel resolution (Rule R13(ii)–(iv),(vi)), so the case-(b) success event is contained in the accepting outcome (vindication, or reinstatement under Rule R13(vi)) of the first panel instance opened within the attempt — an ordinary dispute panel or a fast-track suspension review, with the same k, m, m rej parameters and bars; that panel instance opens, and casts all its verdicts, after the attempt’s anchor (Definition S5.1), so the argument below runs under the anchor-conditional measure verbatim: the accepting-verdict count must reach m. Suppose the adversary casts a ≤ fs accepting verdicts (at most one per identity for the entire life of the panel instance (INV-5, read over the instance; Rule R13(iii),(vi)), across any number of epoch boundaries, by INV-5; seat floor and pool caps per Rule R13(iii); abandonment casts none). An accepting resolution then requires m − a honest accepting verdicts; with the default bars, at most k verdicts are cast before a bar is reached (Lemma S5.7), so at most k − a are honest. Each honest verdict is a recorded decision at a fresh inspection. By the atomic decomposition of §S8.1, the event that an i-th honest verdict is cast is measurable at that verdict’s header atom, strictly before its content atom, and honest verdicts are log-ordered with every earlier verdict’s content preceding every later verdict’s header. By hypothesis (c), each honest verdict rejects with conditional probability at least qpan at its header-inclusive σ-algebra (§S8.1); equivalently, its conditional accept probability there is at most 1 − qpan (a cast verdict’s content is binary). Apply Lemma S8.2(i), in the σ-algebra form of Remark S8.4 at those σ-algebras, to the accept indicators Zi of the honest verdicts: there are i.i.d. Bernoulli(1 −qpan ) majorants Y1, . . . , Y k with Zi ≤ Yi pointwise, unrealised verdicts contributing zero, so the early decisive truncation of Rule R13(iv) removes only nonnegative terms (Lemma S8.2(i)).
