Reach-freeness. The bound of case (a) is independent of reach: it counts only the N −f honest fresh decisions promotion itself requires, however many further inspections occur; in case (b), k and m are protocol constants, viewers whose decisions are not verdicts do not vote, and logged-repeat viewers cannot vote at all (INV-1). An expired dispute retires the item unpromoted (Rule R14); republication is a fresh submission whose endorsement set is identity-disjoint from all prior sets (Lemma S5.3), counted separately and marginally (Corollary S5.27). The case-(b) bound holds regardless of who filed the rejection, so self-dispute gains nothing (S2). Finally, the adversary’s choice whether to open an entry after favourable banked outcomes is a choice among attempts already counted: an attempt whose intervals bank decisions is an attempt whether or not the entry opens, and an unopened attempt succeeds with probability zero (Definition S5.1). 

Attempts anchored with live CC status. For an attempt at whose anchor the bytes hold live CC status at some tool — possible only after a prior successful attempt of the same bytes, itself bounded marginally at its own anchor — the inter-window spans are stable promoted intervals, first decisions banked there are creditable (Definition S4.6(b), Lemma S6.7), and a fresh inspector’s rejection there is a post-promotion rejection routed to Rule R13(vi) rather than a dispute initiation. The two-case argument applies to such attempts under a correspondingly extended reading of hypothesis (c): qpre quantified over the attempt’s unfrozen contexts 

including its stable promoted intervals, with the effective qualifying rejection there being the review-opening post-promotion rejection of Rule R13(vi) within its per-epoch review cap — an extension a deployment must calibrate separately, disclosed here (cf. S4). The event that any byte string is ever promoted at all is a union of first-promotion events, whose attempts are anchored CC -free and need no extension.  

> 90 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Remark S5.18 (Authority-gated crediting: soundness and cost) . The authority gate of Definition S4.6(a) restores the quantifier compatibility that deterministic silencing would otherwise break: without it, an adversary steering presentations toward identities whose rejection rights are suspended, ceiling-capped, or slot-exhausted would face inspectors whose effective rejection probability is zero while their acceptances still carried full promotion weight, and no qpre > 0 could satisfy hypothesis (c) uniformly over targetable cohorts. With the gate, the two capacities are coupled at one deterministic log predicate: a silenced identity can neither reject with effect nor accept with weight, so targeting it gains the adversary nothing. The split is exact: verdict capacity is never authority-gated (Rule R13(v)(a),(e)) — an authority-inactive juror’s accepting verdict counts toward the m-bar like any other, keeping the panel arithmetic of Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 calibration-independent — while promotion crediting always is (Definition S4.6(a)): the same verdict contributes weight only if its caster held authority at decision time, in-entry, banked, and vindication-credited cases alike. Honest cost is negligible: an honest identity is authority-inactive only in the rare calibrated states of theorem S5.5, and its acceptance in that state still buys its personal C copy — INV-3 is unaffected, the cap counting required confirmations, never credits. Safety is monotone: authority-gating removes acceptances from the promotion count and removes nothing from the rejection side (rejections by inactive identities remain advisory flags or corroboration exactly as before, and verdict-eligible rejections are cast regardless of accountability state, Rule R13(v)(e)); Theorem S5.11 is unaffected (its counting is adversarial-weight only), qpan needs no gating (verdicts are never silenced, Rule R13(v)(a),(e)), and verdict eligibility, hence A9, is untouched. One availability-side consequence is disclosed: an adversary who engineers charges against honest identities (S15) now also delays their crediting capacity, slowing benign promotion — an exposure of the same character, availability-only, bounded by the same calibration margins (theorem S5.5) and priced inside ε(M ) where it matters (A11, heuristic sufficiency). 

Remark S5.19 (Interpretation of the parameter q). qpre is the probability that an honest inspector, presented the item at their own confirmation gate, detects the poison and records a rejecting decision that takes protocol effect (quantified over unfrozen candidate contexts, Theorem S5.13(c)); qpan is the probability that an honest 

eligible juror’s cast verdict rejects it. The split matters for calibration: the two acts have different filters (seat weight, pool caps, and verdict capacity apply only to jurors) and different honest baselines, so their log validation is stratified separately (N17); a deployment estimating one number reads it as q := min( qpre , q pan ),under which every bound of Theorem S5.13 holds with q in both roles (theorem S5.15). Both parameters are 

uniform, weakest-target, conditional lower bounds (§S8.1: conditional on all prior logged atoms and the demand factor): because the adversary chooses whom to present the item to, each is the catch rate of the most fallible honest cohort the adversary can reach, never a population average, and aggregate log rates do not directly estimate them (N17). Because a gate rejection is automatically qualifying (Rule R13(i)), detection and reporting are a single act: there is no separate filing step whose friction could decouple them. Abstention (a user who suspects the item and abandons the dialog) records nothing and is counted as a miss inside 1 − q.An honest identity whose rejection rights are suspended, whose rmax slots are transiently full, whose per-epoch ceiling cceil is reached, or whose single per-item initiation is spent is authority-inactive : its inspections are excluded trials, not silenced misses — its acceptance carries no promotion weight and its rejection no initiation (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18) — and such states are rare for honest identities (honest charges accrue at rate at most 1 − pb per benign fresh inspection, both the rate trigger and the ceiling calibrated strictly above that baseline, theorem S5.5; slots free on resolution or expiry, Lemma S5.24; the ceiling and the per-item allowance renew each epoch). Covered gates (Rule R13(i)) are not silenced misses but excluded trials : both branches are protocol-inert, so they belong to no trial class of Theorem S5.13, and the identity’s inspection of the bytes, where one is counted at all, is the earlier decision that created its coverage; so, symmetrically, are authority-inactive inspections (Definition S4.6(a)), and so are cache gates on the accepting side (accept-inert, Definition S4.6(iii)), whose rejections take effect as dispute initiations or corroborations but never as verdicts (Rule R13(iii)). Freshness being shared-presence-scoped (Definition S4.5), the fresh class can include identities with a prior unlogged personal decision on the bytes (before first publication, or across a retirement gap); qpre and qpan are lower bounds over this log-defined class, so a previously fooled but log-fresh inspector is inside the class the parameters must cover — one more reason the bounds are  

> 91 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

weakest-target infima, never averages, and one the validation strata of N17 must include (prior-exposure stratification). Case (a) of Theorem S5.13 needs only qpre > 0: one honest effective qualifying rejection —a rejecting decision at a protocol-bearing gate by an identity currently holding dispute-initiation authority (Rule R13(i),(v)) — denies the ordinary path, a one-sided disjunction, not a vote; coverage-silenced and authority-inactive detections are excluded trials rather than misses (Definition S4.6(a)), the corresponding acceptances carrying no weight; post-promotion rejections beyond the review cap of Rule R13(vi) concern the suspended state only and touch no case-(a) trial. The panel is a bounded jury: joint safety and a low wrongful-eviction bound need the better-than-chance condition qpan + pb > 1; safety alone can be forced for any qpan > 0 by raising m toward k, at the price of a weaker wrongful-eviction bound. The regime q → 0 is the disclosed limitation S14. The exponents N − f and k − fs are protocol constants; the bases 1 − qpre ,

1 − qpan are behavioural parameters of the targeted population. Because panel verdicts are never penalised and first charges are free each epoch (Rule R13(v)), the protocol attaches no penalty to a first good-faith rejection, so it does not disincentivise the gate behaviour that generates qpre . Finally, qpre and ρseat condition on the same human act consistently: an abstaining demander is a miss inside 1 − qpre and a non-verdict inside ρseat ; high abstention degrades resolution speed, never the validity of the safety parameter.

Remark S5.21 (Resolution as a demand-side event) . Resolution is a demand-side property: the demand-paced panel has no deadline, gk(M ) may grow arbitrarily slowly, and an item without demand expires with no protocol consequence, for which liveness (in the classical, eventual-success sense) is vacuous (theorem S5.26). Proposition S5.20 and Theorem S6.13(ii) bound wrongful eviction unconditionally, asserting no probability for resolution itself. Three consequences, in decreasing strength of hypothesis. (Fixed instances.) For any r dispute instances on benign items of the same publisher, predictably specified : membership of an instance in the family is determined no later than that instance’s opening atom, measurably in the opening-inclusive log history and before any verdict content of that instance (any advance deterministic choice included); a family selected retrospectively from panel outcomes is not a fixed-instance family — it is covered by the capped tier where the deterministic cap L ≤ ¯ℓ is available, and otherwise only by the linear random-count tier. For such a family, the probability that all r instances end in wrongful eviction is at most (p∗) r,uniformly over adversary strategies and over any dependence among the panels. (Random count, linear.) Remark S5.23 (Reading of Proposition S5.22(ii)) . The escalating penalties of Rule R13(vii), requiring 

r ≥ 2 wrongful evictions within one epoch, are exponentially rare per specified pair of instances, bounded in expectation in general, and carry the r-fold exponent exactly where a deterministic instance cap holds (capped tier). For repeated panels on the same byte string the bounds sharpen: every verdict is a logged decision consuming shared-presence freshness (Definition S4.5, Rule R13(iii)(b)–(c)), so each identity casts at most one verdict across all of the bytes’ panels ever; in particular the adversary’s fs seat-eligible identities are spent once across those panels, not once per panel. 

Lemma S5.24 (Bounds on rejection-based denial of service) .

(i) At any time, at most f · rmax items are held in Disputed by adversarial rejections. Moreover, adversarial dispute-initiations against any single item number at most f per epoch (Rule R13(i), single-initiation clause), independently of rmax .(ii) (Demand-paced resolution.) Unconditionally, with no behavioural assumption (persistence exceptions per INV-3), a freeze costs each affected demander at most one accepting confirmation per demanded pair, and only if the demander holds no local in-scope copy: exactly the required confirmation any demander of an unpromoted pair owes regardless (Theorem S6.2(i)). A demander who rejects or abandons incurs inspection effort, a distinct resource disclosed in N18, not bounded by the cap. A dispute therefore suspends only the community amortisation for its demanders and never pushes anyone above the single-user cap, however long the freeze lasts. Under A9, the number so affected is additionally bounded: the demanders affected while the dispute is unresolved number at most κ in expectation, with exponential tail (Lemma S5.6), independent of the item’s popularity and of the freeze’s wall-clock duration. A dispute on an undemanded item affects no user and expires within Texp of its last demand (Rule R14). (Wide pre-exposure: Remark S5.25; S6.) (iii) No panel-lapse exploitation; slot economics. There is no panel lapse, hence no forced re-entry: an unresolved dispute never drains the owner’s submission budget. A rejection slot stays occupied until  

> 94 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

resolution or expiry: against demanded items, disputes resolve at demand rate into the accountability schedule; against undemanded items, the slot pins only an undemanded item and frees at expiry. (Remark S5.26; S5.) (iv) A sustained wrongful-rejection campaign alters no local store (Lemma S5.8) and costs each user at most the single-user floor (Theorem S6.2); beyond the f free first charges each epoch, it expends the adversary’s rejection rights at an escalating rate (Rule R13(v)). Its throughput is bounded by three deterministic caps, each in its own currency: charged dispute-initiations (those eventually contradicted by vindication) number at most cacc f per epoch (Lemma S5.4); concurrent open rejections number at most f r max at any moment, and freezes against any single item at most f per epoch (clause (i), single initiation); and uncharged terminal disputes (ending in eviction or expiry) number at most Emax per shared-tier presence per item per epoch (Rule R14(i)), each retiring that presence. No single constant bounds all wrongful rejections across all items — a slot freed by expiry may be re-used against another item — and what each re-use buys is one freeze, resolved or expired at that item’s own demand clock, within the single-user cap. The allowances renew at epoch boundaries (N14). Proof. (i) Each adversarial identity holds at most rmax open rejections (Rule R13(i)) and the adversary controls at most f identities. The per-item clause caps each identity at one initiation per item per epoch. (ii) The harm of a freeze is exactly the amortisation it suspends: the demanders who must confirm personally while the item is frozen; by Rule R13(iii)–(iv) the eligible deciding demanders cast the verdicts and, with the default bars, resolution occurs by the k-th verdict; each affected demander’s accepting confirmation, where one occurs, is the one required confirmation they would owe on any unpromoted pair, so the per-user floor is untouched (INV-3); the in-expectation and tail bounds are Lemma S5.6; an item with no new demand meets the expiry clause of Rule R14(i). (iii) By Rule R13(iv) the only re-entry paths are the optional neither-bar outcome and post-expiry republication, both owner-initiated; slots free on resolution or expiry (Rules R13(iv), R14(i)). (iv) Every dispute-initiating rejection contradicted by vindication charges the rejecter’s schedule and ceiling (Rule R13(v)); by Lemma S5.4 the adversary’s effective charged dispute-initiations are deterministically at most cacc per identity per epoch, bounding per-epoch charged-initiation 

throughput at cacc f as a protocol fact; the concurrency, single-initiation, and terminal-dispute caps are Rule R13(i) and Rule R14(i) verbatim. By Lemma S5.8 no local store is altered, and each user’s required cost remains at most the single-user cap (Theorem S6.2). 

Remark S5.25 (Wide pre-exposure) . An item pre-exposed so widely that few verdict-eligible demanders remain simply stalls frozen : the freeze blocks promotion at every tool for as long as the panel is unresolved, and the demand-paced panel carries no deadline an adversary could exploit (Rule R13(ii)–(iii)); this is the deterministic failure mode of A9, and its cost is availability, never safety. For a poisoned item the stall is borne by the adversary’s own submission. A benign item can also be stalled this way — by pre-exposure or pool-skewed demand, with or without adversarial help — and then the availability cost falls on its publisher and demanders; the harm is bounded exactly as for any freeze: each demander pays at most one required confirmation per pair, within the single-user cap (Lemma S5.24(ii), unconditional clause), the item remains personally usable through each user’s own gate (Rule R13(ii)), local stores are untouched (Lemma S5.8), and an item whose demand lapses expires harmlessly (Rule R14(i)). (S6.) 

Remark S5.26 (Roles of Twin , Tpan , Texp ; the demand-paced design) . The window Twin forbids the pro-motion race: finalisation occurs only at window end, so any qualifying rejection intervenes even if N

acceptances arrive first: this is what makes the partition of Theorem S5.13 well-defined; and each attempt’s identity-disjoint credited endorsement set (Lemma S5.3, Definition S5.1) is what makes the per-attempt marginal bounds and their union valid (Corollary S5.27). Tpan survives only as the clock of fast-track suspension panels, whose log-fresh jurors are drawn from demanders during suspension and prior adopters without a logged decision on the bytes (Rule R13(vi)); their supply is not guaranteed by construction — prior adopters with logged decisions are ineligible — but is availability-side, exactly A9’s province; the ordinary panel carries no global deadline; it is demand-paced: verdicts accumulate at the item’s own demand rate,  

> 95 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

so resolution time scales inversely with demand while the harm of the freeze scales directly with it, and their product is bounded (Lemma S5.24(ii)). A disputed item with too little fresh demand simply waits, exactly as it would have waited for ordinary promotion; an item nobody demands expires harmlessly within 

Texp . Zero-tolerance designs lack an accountability clock entirely; the demand-paced panel provides one wherever fresh demand exists. An evict-and-restart rule would instead multiply per-item cost by the number of rejections; the panel’s decisive, demand-paced resolution is what keeps the dispute load an additive 

O(Emax N + ctool ¯Dvκ) per demanded item, with ¯Dv the expected dispute count of Lemma S6.7, absorbed by the cross-term and shrinking-front-loading conditions of Theorem S6.13 (S5). 

Corollary S5.27 (Submission budget and publisher accountability) . Promotion occurs only at an attempt’s finalisation (Definition S5.1), and every attempt is counted at its anchor — a logged event preceding every decision creditable to the attempt, hence a sound conditioning point (Definition S5.1, §S8.1). Under the default bars, the anchors lying in a given epoch are of exactly two kinds, each bounded by the submission rate limit: presence-creating submissions, and finalisation events, each of which closes an entry opened by a same-epoch rate-limited (re-)submission (windows are epoch-contained, convention (C4); Rule R12). Hence the promotion attempts of adversary-published poisoned items anchored in a given epoch number at most 2f ρ sub (paraphrase variants and re-entries included). Each such attempt gathers an identity-disjoint credited endorsement set (Lemma S5.3) and is bounded marginally by Theorem S5.13 at its own anchor (under its hypotheses); the union bound, valid under arbitrary dependence across attempts, gives

> 96 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(A5) and publication co-occur among honest identities — though the per-identity rate limit yields the crude default-bar cap Sanc ≤ 2M ρ sub over M eligible identities, and each such item, on eviction, penalises its publisher (Rule R13(vii)), economically suppressing repeat deception. Under permanent freshness, the credited endorsement sets of all attempts of a given byte string are identity-disjoint over the deployment’s 

entire history , not merely within an epoch (Lemma S5.3); consequently the number of attempts of the same bytes that can ever gather N credited endorsements is at most ⌊Hacc /(N − f )⌋, where Hacc is the total number of honest identities that ever accept those bytes at a gate. Moreover, every eviction of a poisoned attempt triggers the publisher-accountability consequences of Rule R13(vii) (cool-down; on repetition within an epoch, suspension of submission rights and attestation-weight reduction); the quantitative effect on future submission capacity depends on the deployment’s escalation schedule (§S4.2, required parameters) and is consumed by no proof, while a benign publisher’s exposure to the same penalty is bounded by Proposition S5.22(ii). Proof. An attempt is counted at its anchor. Under the default bars every anchor is a presence-creating submis-sion or a finalisation (Definition S5.1); distinct attempts have distinct anchors; presence-creating submissions of adversary-published items in the epoch number at most f ρ sub (Rule R12); and each finalisation in the epoch closes an entry whose opening (re-)submission lies in the same epoch (windows are epoch-contained, convention (C4)), so finalisation anchors also number at most f ρ sub : at most 2f ρ sub anchored attempts. Union bound over these attempts, each covered marginally by Theorem S5.13 at its anchor on its own credited set (Lemma S5.3); a union bound requires no independence across the events it covers. The horizon form sums the per-epoch display over the anchor epochs of the horizon, every promotion belonging to the attempt anchored in some such epoch (from deployment inception; a general interval is covered by summing from inception through its end, no carry-in-conditional bound being asserted). The system-wide form is the delayed-marks lemma (Lemma S8.5) applied to anchor-indexed success indicators. Its occurrence events lie in the conditioning σ-algebras: take Gi generated by all atoms up to and including the i-th anchor atom, so that Oi is Gi-measurable; the per-attempt bound of Theorem S5.13 is stated conditionally on exactly this anchor-inclusive σ-algebra, so it bounds each mark conditionally on Gi, the mark resolving only at the attempt’s finalisation, which Lemma S8.5 permits; the deterministic-cap form substitutes ¯S. The lifetime clause is immediate from identity-disjointness of credited decisions across all attempts. The accountability clauses are Rule R13(vii) and Proposition S5.22(ii)–(iii). 

Remark S5.28 (Why attempts are keyed to anchor epochs, not finalisation epochs) . An earlier version of Corollary S5.27 counted the attempts whose finalisation lies in the epoch. That attribution is unsound, for the same optional-selection reason the attempt anchor exists (Definition S5.1, commentary): banked decisions logged during dormancy are public log events preceding the owner’s resubmission decision, so the set of attempts finalising in a given epoch is outcome-selected — an adversary maintaining many anchored dormant attempts on distinct byte strings and resubmitting only those with favourable banked outcomes makes the per-finalisation-epoch attempt count small while the epoch’s promotion probability is governed by the whole maintained pool. Anchors, by contrast, precede every decision creditable to their attempts, so the anchored-in-epoch family is determined before any of the outcomes it is charged with, and the delayed-marks lemma applies. The per-attempt bound of Theorem S5.13 and the lifetime ceiling of Remark S5.29 are unaffected: only the calendar attribution of attempts to epochs changes. 

Remark S5.29 (Lifetime exposure under permanent freshness) . The horizon union over anchor epochs in Corollary S5.27, and the lifetime ceiling, count anchored attempts — the lifetime ceiling, threshold-reaching attempts — whose honest endorsers are never re-used: every pipeline entry of a given byte string that reaches its threshold consumes N − f honest identities’ first-and-only logged decisions on those bytes, so the adversary cannot re-use a previously logged-deciding population, and ⌊Hacc /(N − f )⌋ is a hard ceiling on the number of threshold-reaching attempts of the same bytes over the deployment’s lifetime, independent of the number of epochs. Unsuccessful attempts — each gathering fewer than N − f new honest credited acceptances — and presentations yielding no logged acceptance are not bounded by this ceiling; they are  

> 97 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

governed by the per-epoch rate limit ρsub and the per-attempt marginal bound of Theorem S5.13, each attempt consuming its own union-bound term. The ceiling is per exact byte string (convention (C5)): an adversary minting byte-distinct paraphrase variants holds a fresh ceiling per variant, each consuming its own rate-limited submission and union-bound term (S1, Corollary S5.27); the lifetime clause therefore hard-bounds successful re-entry of fixed bytes, while variant families are governed by the per-epoch rate and calibration clauses, not by this ceiling. 

Remark S5.30 (Weighted form) . With endorsement weights, replace the exponent N − f by (W −

Wadv )/w max . With seat weights in [wseat , w max ] and a vindication weight bar Wm, honest accepting weight at least Wm − fswmax is required; seat weights are attestation-layer constants fixed at verdict time, hence predictable in the sense of Lemma S8.8’s second clause, and Hoeffding for weighted sums (Lemma S8.8) gives exp( −2t2/(( k − fs)w2max )) with t the weight margin: constants change, exponents do not, and the bound remains reach-free. 

Remark S5.31 (Per-source-class thresholds) . Promotion thresholds may be set per source class of the underlying content (authentication is not trust: a signed phishing message is still an attack; so source identity only tunes the threshold, never bypasses the quorum, and never widens a tool scope). All bounds hold with 

W , N , m, k read as the minimum over source classes; Theorem S5.11 requires min class W > W adv and 

min class m > f s.

S6 The Memory-Verification Budget 

Scope. All results in this appendix count confirmations attached to reusable memory content , per (value, tool) pair: a one-time personal cap of private values, and the stream of shareable Confirmed items. Per-task confirmations (for freshly composed sensitive arguments and for crystallizing ephemeral task content) are common to every gated design, scale with usage rather than population, and are orthogonal to the amortisation analysed here. PerMIT’s distinction is layered. Pathwise — independent of every federated and demand assumption, persistence exceptions per INV-3 — no user’s required cost exceeds once per (value, tool) pair, ever, and the epoch’s expected shared required load never exceeds the standalone single-user mass 

dtool — the cost of having no community at all (Theorem S6.2(i)–(ii)); this cap is the appendix’s firmest guarantee: pathwise, consuming no federated or demand assumption (its expected clauses read the reference model’s masses), subject only to the stated first-party persistence exceptions. On top of it, the ledger-charged 

shared stream is at most the front-loading multiple Bfl ¯γ of the standalone mass, independently of item popularity (Theorem S6.9, Remark S6.11); and the ledger residual — demand arriving outside every charged piece, in particular handle-expressed arrivals of already-certified bytes, which gate by design (Rule R4(c), S18) — has its benign, assumption-conforming component vanish under the full vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii): the coverage-and-representation assumption A11 together with the product-rate condition 

D0(M )V (M ) = o(M ), ε(M ) dgood (M ) → 0 (under A13, from ε(M ) → 0; dgood per Lemma S6.8), and bounded tool spread (A12); these conditions are sufficient, not necessary, and demand on poisoned or assumption-violating pairs remains in r(e)bad (Corollary S6.10). Under the measured corridor of §S6.5 — whose front-loading rows describe deployments whose time geometry changes with scale, not consequences of population growth alone (Remark S6.12) — both shared components vanish; where the corridor or A11 fails, the universal fallback applies to the required component: the epoch’s expected shared required load remains at most the standalone mass dtool (Theorem S6.2(ii)), the once-per-pair cap binding throughout. Voluntary invitation confirmations remain governed by the ledger rows of Lemma S6.7; rejection and attention effort is accounted separately in N18. No standalone-mass ceiling is claimed for those additional resources. Headline readings of this appendix elsewhere in the paper must carry these conditions: “shared verification load vanishes” is asserted only under measured saturation, smoothness, coverage, juror-supply, and bounded-mass hypotheses (A10–A13), never from population growth alone (Remark S6.12); the firmest statements are the pathwise once-per-pair cap and the reference-model standalone-mass ceiling (Theorem S6.2).

Scope of the exogenous-schedule reading. Assumption A10 (Temporal smoothness, fractional form) . There are a constant s0 ≥ 1 and a front-loading bound ¯γ = ¯ γ(M ) ∈ (0 , 1] such that, for every pair (v, t ) demanded in the epoch: the number of distinct demanders of (v, t ) whose first demand of the pair falls within any single finalisation window (length Twin )or any single candidate-dormancy interval (an inter-window span carrying no live community entry, of length 

≤ Texp by the retirement clock; stable promoted intervals — a live, unsuspended CC presence with no open window — are exempt from both clauses: demand there is fast-pathed or ledger-residual, never bucketed, Lemma S6.7) is at most ¯γ n v + s0; and likewise the number of distinct identities whose first audit-recorded decision on the pair (v, t ) in the epoch (§S8.1: display-transcript decisions at non-covered gates, cache gates on both branches included — the accepting branch is audit-recorded though never federation-logged, Definition S4.6(iii)) falls within any single such window or interval is at most ¯γ n v + s0 (the first-pair-decision clause ). Demander counts are realized shared-tier demand events ( nlog , §S8.1); decider counts are audit-recorded first pair-decisions (a class containing every federation-logged first pair-decision), whose deciders are latent demanders (required-approval clause, §S6.1) but need not be realized log-demanders. Both are capped against the item’s latent demand nv — the weaker denominator, since nlog  

> v

≤ nv and every decider is a latent demander.               

> Commentary (non-normative; reading and validation of A10). The cap is relative to the item’s own demand, never an absolute count: it forbids front-loading (demand concentrating inside one window), not popularity. For demand arriving steadily over an epoch of length L,¯γ≈Twin /L for every item, independently of nv
> and of M; the additive s0makes the cap satisfiable for items of tiny demand. A10 is a hindsight property of completed epochs, used only in the analysis: no protocol rule reads, estimates, or depends on ¯γ. Its violation is 100

PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

item-local and graceful: an item whose demand spikes into one window ( γv ≈ 1) simply fails to amortise: each of its demanders pays the one required confirmation the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) already bounds, while every other item’s accounting is untouched (S17). Demand-paced panels need no such cap: panels accumulate verdicts across windows, so A10 constrains promotion finalisation windows and dormant intervals only; the dormant-interval clause is the stronger half ( Texp ≫ Twin typically), kept plausible by the dormancy-retirement clock of Rule R14(i) and by its restriction to candidate dormancy: a promoted item’s demand, however concentrated, is exempt from both clauses (its cost is fast-pathed or ledger-residual, Lemma S6.7). Validation: per-item realised fractions γv , checked specifically on the most-demanded items (N17). The first-pair-decision clause has the same character and the same validation path: for demand arriving steadily, first pair-decisions track first pair-demands; both realised fractions are logged per item (N17). The audit-recorded class is validated the same way, directly from display-transcript logs: both branches of every gate, cache gates included, are recorded there (Rule R8(e)), so realised first-pair-decision fractions are observable without reference to the federation log. Its pair-level indexing is load-bearing: it is what makes the dispute-initiation classification of Lemma S6.7 exhaustive, since a cross-tool repeat decision (a prior acceptance at another pair) and a decision postponed past an abandoned dialog are each the identity’s first decision at their own pair, counted where they fall. The pair-level form is the item-level cap read tool by tool, still stated against the item’s total demand nv ,the weaker and more plausible denominator; steady demand gives ¯γ ≈ Twin /L per pair exactly as per item, and the same per-item logging of realised fractions γv validates it (N17). 

Assumption A11 (Coverage sufficiency, operational form) . Adopt the presence taxonomy and charging convention of Lemma S6.7: within an epoch, an item’s shared-tier presence partitions into entry windows, candidate-dormancy intervals, and freeze/suspension intervals (the ledger-charged pieces), and stable promoted intervals (a live, unsuspended community entry with no open entry window). For a pair (v, t ) and an epoch, let Uv,t count the epoch’s residual required pair-approvals of (v, t ): those charged to no ledger row (option-induced cache confirmations excluded: floor-charged, Corollary S6.6) — a subclass of the required confirmations by demanders whose epoch-first demand of the item falls in a stable promoted interval or outside every shared-tier presence (a credited such decision is weight-row-charged instead, Lemma S6.7, only shrinking the residual) (before first publication, after retirement, or after demotion; gates fired during a suspension are freeze-bucket events, ledger-charged, never residual). Each belongs to a distinct demander of the pair (INV-3), so Uv,t ≤ nv,t pathwise. The residual class is operational , keyed to gate behaviour, never to certificate existence: a live CC entry naming (v, t ) fast-paths only literal -expressed use (Rule R4(a)); a handle-expressed use of the same pair gates at every tool (Rule R4(c)) and, absent a local in-scope copy, is required — and, in a stable promoted interval, residual. There are D0(M ) ≥ 0 and ε(M ) → 0 such that, for every benign (Definition S4.10) shareable item v ∈ V , every target t, and every n ≥ 1, conditional on 

nv,t = n:

EUv,t | nv,t = n ≤ D0(M ) + ε(M ) n. 

Like A10, this is a hindsight property of completed epochs, read against latent demand (§S8.1); no protocol rule reads, estimates, or depends on it, and it is trivially satisfied whenever n ≤ D0(M ) (since Uv,t ≤

nv,t ). The growth of D0 is consumed only through the product condition D0(M ) V (M ) = o(M ) of Theorem S6.9(iii), a joint property of the coverage threshold and the saturation law, never of either alone; under the Heaps form V (M ) = Θ( M β ) (§S6.1), the sufficient rate is D0(M ) = o(M 1−β ), the form used in the corridor (§S6.5) and in the sufficiency sketch below. It bounds jointly: (i) publication coverage —a demanded benign item its owner never publishes has every gate-firing accepting demand residual, and satisfies the bound only if that demand is small; (ii) promotion coverage — demand at not-yet-promoted pairs arriving outside every ledger-charged piece; and (iii) representation coverage — demand for live-CC 

pairs arriving handle-expressed (untrusted-channel arrival of already-certified bytes), which gates and pays despite the certificate (S18). An item or arrival pattern violating it never amortises: each residual demander pays the one required confirmation the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) already bounds, item-locally and gracefully, entering the residual r(e)bad of Corollary S6.10. 

Heuristic sufficiency and compatibility. The following sufficiency sketch is heuristic — a plausibility argument, not a derivation: it does not formally compose intervening freezes, wrongful evictions, the entry 

101 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

cap, the pool-composition requirements, or the arrival-representation mix into a proof of A11, which remains an assumption; the compatibility calculation closing the paragraph is exact. Heuristically sufficient, for a benign item and any fixed η > 0: the owner publishes the item before more than an ε-fraction of each demanded pair’s demand arrives and resubmits promptly after each unpromoted window (dormant intervals short of the retirement clock, within the per-epoch entry cap of Rule R14(i)); some entry, counting the banked credits of its preceding interval (Definition S4.6(b)), accrues at least (1 + η)N/p b fresh byte-first demanders (a positive byte-first fraction per demanded tool — enough demanders whose first decision on the bytes occurs at that tool — is one empirical mechanism supporting this cohort’s existence; it is a heuristic diagnostic for A11, not a hypothesis of any theorem) whose demand spans at least Dpool pools with at least 

⌈N/D pool ⌉ accepting demanders available per pool (so the per-pool credit caps of Definition S4.6(f) do not bind below N ), almost all of them authority-active (Definition S4.6(a): honest identities are authority-inactive only in the rare calibrated states of theorem S5.5, folded into the same ε) — a demand-composition property of the same character as A9’s structural half, validated by the same pool-stratified logging (N17); and demotion of promoted benign entries is exponentially rare (Proposition S5.20), so promoted status, once reached, persists; and, for the representation clause, demand for promoted pairs predominantly arrives fast-path-expressed — through trusted retrieval of the live entry (Definition S4.1, Rule R14(ii)) or reuse of local readable copies — with the handle-arrival fraction (S18) folded into the same ε: an arrival-channel property of the demand stream, logged directly (N17), never derived from the protocol. For schedules with N (M ) → ∞ — every corridor schedule, §S6.5 — the accepting count then crosses N except with probability e−2η′2N (M )/(1+ η)2

→ 0 by Hoeffding’s lower tail, for a margin η′ depending on η and pb, giving 

ε(M ) → 0; at fixed N the same cohort yields only a fixed failure probability, so ε(M ) → 0 under fixed protocol constants requires instead that available cohorts grow beyond a fixed multiple of N , which A11 then asserts rather than derives; intervening disputes are absorbed by the vindication exemption of Rule R14(i)(b) (N15) and resolve at the item’s own demand rate, freezes excluding themselves from the window clock (Rule R13(iv)). Consistency with A10 : under the fractional cap the two assumptions do not constrain one quantity from opposite sides. A crossing window is permitted to contain (1 + η)N/p b demanders whenever 

¯γ n v + s0 ≥ (1 + η)N/p b, i.e. whenever nv ≥  (1 + η)N/p b − s0

/¯γ; D0(M ) may therefore be taken of order N (M )/¯γ(M ), and the product condition’s Heaps form D0(M ) = o(M 1−β ) (Theorem S6.9(iii)) becomes N (M )/¯γ(M ) = o(M 1−β ), satisfied by every corridor schedule (§S6.5). 

Commentary (non-normative; scoping, representation dependence, and validation of A11). Benign scoping is deliberate : poisoned items are excluded; their promotion probability is bounded above by Theorem S5.13, and their residual demand is charged at the single-user cap by design, each such confirmation being a personal gate inspection of an unpromoted or flagged item, exactly the inspection the design intends. The operational (no-row) form is load-bearing : an earlier draft keyed coverage to certificate existence, under which a handle-expressed use of a live-CC pair counted as covered while its gate — required under Rule R4(c) — was charged to no row and to no residual; the operational form charges every required confirmation somewhere by construction (Lemma S6.7, charging convention), and names exactly the demand the amortisation story must cover. Representation dependence is the disclosed price : community certification amortises the flows that can take the fast path — literal-expressed reuse of promoted bytes, in particular bytes obtained through trusted retrieval (R ETRIEVE returns live entries as readable E-bytes, Definition S4.1, Rule R14(ii), which then byte-match the live entry, Rule R4(a)) — and never the handle-expressed arrivals, which gate by design (S18); ε(M ) must absorb the handle-arrival fraction of certified demand, so the vanishing of the residual is a joint property of publication timing, promotion speed, and arrival representation. Failure is graceful and item-local: residual demand is priced at the cap (Theorem S6.2(i)), and the epoch’s shared required load never exceeds the standalone mass dtool in any case (Theorem S6.2(ii)). Incentive sensitivity is the disclosed residual S16. Under permanent freshness the condition weakens in one direction and is explicitly priced in the other: banked credits accumulate across epoch boundaries toward the next entry, so qualifying demand need not concentrate within one epoch; conversely, endorsers consumed by a failed entry never renew, which the identity-disjoint counting already prices (Lemma S5.3). Validation: log each item’s realized residual fraction Uv,t /n v,t and the fast-path-vs-gate split of live-CC demand (the handle-arrival fraction), stratified 102 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

on the most-demanded items, together with per-item time-to-promotion against accumulated demand and the per-epoch demanded-but-unpublished mass (N17).

Commentary (non-normative; reading and validation of A12). The bound formalises the observation that a published item typically addresses the scenario its owner used it in: endorsement, promotion, and scope are all per (value, tool); it licenses the per-tool split in Theorem S6.9(iii). Validation: per-item distinct-tool counts (N17). The byte-first density of a demanded tool’s stream — the fraction of its demanders whose first decision on the bytes occurs at that tool — is deliberately not part of this assumption: no proof consumes it, and its role is heuristic support for the promotion-coverage clause of A11 (see the heuristic-sufficiency paragraph there).

Commentary (non-normative; reading and validation of A13). The assumption is load-bearing and deliberately explicit: without it, the vanishing conclusions would fail — a population-indexed demand law with d(M ) → ∞ 

(each user demanding ever more distinct shared items as the community grows) satisfies every remaining hypothesis while the front-loading term Bfl ¯γ d grows — and pointwise finiteness of d at each M does not supply the uniform bound the limits consume. It is an individual-usage property, not a population property: under any fixed per-user demand law the masses are constants in M and A13 is automatic (the Zipf law of §S8.5 has d = P 

> r

pr = 1 identically), and the Heaps form is standardly read over a corpus accumulated from bounded individual streams Lü et al. (2010), so the saturation hypothesis already carries this reading. The owner-coupling convention cannot violate it: coupled indicators number at most ρsub per user per epoch (Rule R12). The disclosed failure mode is demand growth induced by the shared tier itself — a richer library drawing each user to more shared items per epoch — a network effect of the same character as the incentive feedback of S16; violation is graceful: the relative bounds and the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) are unaffected, and the general-form rate conditions of A13 price the growth explicitly. Validation: log per-user distinct shareable-item demand per epoch across deployment growth (N17). 

S6.2 The single-user first-authorization cap 

The cap’s core is a counting identity. Its pathwise clause, Theorem S6.2(i), rests on the harness rules and INV-3 alone: no ledger row, no behavioural parameter, no demand model, and no assumption of this appendix. Its expected clauses restate that identity against the reference demand model’s masses (§S6.1), still using no assumption; the single assumption-bearing statement in this subsection is the mass comparison of Corollary S6.3 (A12). The cap is therefore stated first. Its dependency chain is Lemma S6.1 → INV-3 →

Theorem S6.2 → Lemma S6.7, strictly in that order. 

103 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

Lemma S6.1 (Local scope persistence) . Fix a user u, a value v, and a tool t, and suppose u’s own accepting review of (v, t ) has occurred at any gate transaction — including a transaction another of whose reviews aborted the enclosing invocation (Rule R2: endorsement is durable). Then in every later configuration, unless u’s own agent has evicted the block (N13), u’s store contains a C-block with bytes v and t ∈ scope ,and every use of v at t by u expressed as a readable literal takes the fast path of Rule R4(a); a use expressed through a handle gates (Rule R4(c)) as a covered gate, protocol-inert and redundant (Rule R13(i), §S6.1). In particular no later gate event of u for (v, t ) is required . (Option O3 is requirements-level and absent from the formal semantics; a conforming instantiation would leave the block in place and read the authorization and coverage tests over currently authorizing witnesses, O3(a),(c)–(d).) For the pseudo-target: once u holds any 

local readable E-level copy of the bytes (via RECALL -E , Definition S2.6), a later confirm on those bytes at 

reasoning is a covered gate by the reasoning-coverage clause of Rule R13(i), protocol-inert and redundant; hence no later gate event of u for (v, reasoning ) is required. Proof. By Rule R2 and Definition S2.5, every accepting review of (v, t ) writes a fresh C-block with bytes 

v and t ∈ scope at the close of its atomic transaction, on the executing and the aborting branch alike (C ONF -A CC , T OOL -G ATE -A CC , T OOL -G ATE -R EJ ; Figures S2–S3, convention (vii)). By A1–A2 blocks are immutable once written, so neither this block’s label nor its scope ever changes; local stores mutate only through owner-invoked Σ-operations and owner confirmations, and no protocol event initiated by any other identity alters or removes any block in u’s local store (Lemma S5.8). The only removal path is first-party eviction (N13). Rule R4(a) then byte-matches v against the stored block with t ∈ scope . For the pseudo-target: any local E-level copy of the bytes is readable (R ECALL -E, Definition S2.6) and persists by A1–A2 and Lemma S5.8, the first-party eviction exception aside; the reasoning-coverage clause of Rule R13(i) then classifies every later reasoning-target gate on those bytes as covered. Its owner-side test reads exactly the readability fact Rule R5 establishes: readability is the entirety of what an accepting reasoning confirmation buys, so the clause covers precisely the gates whose acceptance would change nothing locally. 

Theorem S6.2 (Single-user first-authorization cap) . The cap counts required user approvals — first autho-rizations of reusable (value, tool) pairs — and nothing else: not rejecting decisions, abandoned or repeated dialogs, covered-gate (redundant) acceptances, batch-page attention, or per-task confirmations (classes: §S6.1; effort account: N18). The superscript forced below denotes this required-approval count. Suppose the owner’s agent does not evict the relevant local blocks (the disclosed first-party exception of INV-3); Option O3 is requirements-level and absent from the formal semantics (O3), so no lease expiry exists in the proved system. (i) (Pathwise lifetime invariant.) Pathwise and independent of every federated and demand-model assump-tion, subject to the stated first-party persistence exceptions: under any dispute, rejection, revocation, or availability campaign whatsoever, no user is required to confirm any given (value, tool) pair more than once over the deployment’s lifetime: Cforced  

> u,v,t

≤ 1 pathwise, counting required pair-approvals (Defini-tion S2.5, counting unit). This is INV-3, established by Lemma S6.1. Rejecting decisions, abandoned dialogs, repeated within-transaction reviews, redundant confirmations (§S6.1), and inspection time are a distinct resource, disclosed and partially bounded in N18, and are not bounded by this clause. (ii) (Expected cap, single epoch, reference model.) In the independent reference model (§S6.1), the two components are bounded on their own horizons: E[Cforced ,∞ 

> personal

] ≤ dpriv (lifetime) and E[ ¯Cforced ,(e)share ] ≤

dtool (per epoch e); the componentwise sum dpriv + dtool is a valid upper bound for their sum on any single epoch read jointly with the lifetime personal term, not a homogeneous single-horizon cost. The bound dtool is exactly the expected shareable cost a single user incurs in isolation, where every gate necessarily fires: no user is ever pushed above the single-user cost. The comparison to the item-level mass, dtool ≤ ctool d, is Corollary S6.3 and is this theorem’s only assumption-bearing companion.

; the single-epoch mass bounds the lifetime cost only when it is itself read as the lifetime mass (§S6.1). (iv) (Monotonicity.) At a fixed realised workload with no disputes, adding users never increases any individual’s required count: promotions only advance, and a promotion of (v, t ) removes the gate for every later demander of that pair (Rule R4). With disputes, an added identity can suspend amortisation, forcing later demanders to confirm personally; the pathwise invariant (i) still caps every user at once per pair. Proof. (i) By Lemma S6.1: u’s first accepting review of (v, t ) writes the covering local copy durably, whatever the enclosing transaction’s outcome, so no later gate event of u for (v, t ) is required (a literal-expressed use fast-paths; a handle-expressed use is a covered gate, redundant by definition); within that first transaction the pair is counted once (Definition S2.5); declining voluntary invitations costs nothing (Definition S4.6). (ii) By (i), each required confirmation of (v, t ) belongs to a distinct demander of the pair (realized, hence latent, §S8.1), so the population’s required total for (v, t ) is at most nv,t , with E[nv,t ] = M p v,t in the reference model; summing over pairs and dividing by M gives dtool ; personal pairs add dpriv , once each (INV-3). (iii) The pathwise clause (i) is lifetime; taking expectations over lifetime pair-demand indicators gives d∞

Remark S6.4 (Voluntary effort and the cap) . The cap is stated over required confirmations. Voluntary community effort (a repeat re-inspection after a dispute or re-entry notification) is declinable at zero personal cost, carries no promotion weight (INV-1), and is counted in the budget (Lemma S6.8), never in the cap. 

Corollary S6.5 (The cap under revocation) . Post-promotion suspensions and re-verification (N16) do not multiply required cost: a user who relied on ungated CC status at t and loses it on demotion or suspension owes at most their first-ever confirmation of (v, t ), already counted inside d∞

> tool

, after which their local C

copy covers all reuse at t for the deployment’s lifetime (Lemma S6.1). Hence expected total required cost is at most dpriv + d∞

> tool

, lifetime, independent of the number of epochs. A multiplicative factor Elease would arise only under a conforming instantiation of the requirements-level Option O3 — an extension-level claim, stated and to be proved with that extension (O3(e)), outside this corollary. Proof. Immediate from Theorem S6.2(i),(iii): required confirmations are once per (user, value, tool) over the lifetime; demotion, suspension, and re-verification never touch local C copies (Lemma S5.8). Option O3 is absent from the formal semantics (O3). 

Corollary S6.6 (Budget under Options O1–O2) . Option-induced cache confirmations (Options O1–O2) belong to no pipeline ledger row and to no residual (Lemma S6.7, exclusions): they are accept-inert for the shared tier (Definition S4.6(iii)) and are accounted in two classes. (Per-task.) O2’s confirmations of freshly composed data-tool arguments lie in the per-task stream, outside the reusable-memory accounting entirely, by the scope hypothesis of Proposition S3.28. (Floor-charged.) All remaining option-induced cache  

> 105 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

confirmations — O1’s first-use sensitive-sink confirmations of T-values and O2’s confirmations of endorsed-value reuse at data tools — are required pair-confirmations of the confirming user, charged to the floor component: each is at most one per (user, value, sensitive sink) resp. (user, value, data tool) (scope-caching, Lemma S6.1, INV-3), an O(1) enlargement of the per-item pair count under the sink-spread and data-tool-spread hypotheses of Proposition S3.28 — folded exactly as A12 folds tool pairs. The exhaustiveness of the charging convention (Lemma S6.7) is unaffected: option-induced confirmations are charged to the per-task stream or the floor, never to any pipeline row or residual. Proof. Immediate from Proposition S3.28(iii), Definition S4.6(iii), and INV-3: the fresh cache copy scope-caches (Lemma S6.1), so each (user, value, target) triple is required at most once; the spread hypotheses bound the targets per value by O(1) ; and the freshly-composed class is per-task by hypothesis. 

S6.3 The per-item confirmation ledger Lemma S6.7 (Per-item confirmation ledger, O-form) . Fix an item v and an epoch; adopt the default bars of Rule R13(iv), A9, A10, A12, and, for the bucket rows, that each honest inspection, fresh or repeat, accepts a benign item with conditional probability at least pb given all prior protocol events (for a repeat inspection of bytes the inspector previously accepted, the bound is if anything the more plausible). Adopt also the standing exception of Theorem S6.2 (no owner eviction of the relevant local blocks; Option O3 is requirements-level and absent from the formal semantics, O3). Option-induced cache confirmations (Options O1–O2) are excluded throughout — from Cpipe  

> v

, from the residual of A11, and from the pathwise count Cv of clause (iv): they are charged to the floor component, with the O(1) spread factors of Corollary S6.6. Let E⋆ := Emax + 1 .

Presence taxonomy. Within the epoch, the item’s shared-tier presence partitions into four classes of pieces: 

entry windows (at most Emax , by the per-item entry cap of Rule R14(i), counting promoted, evicted, and unpromoted entries alike; windows are epoch-contained, convention (C4)); freeze/suspension intervals 

(the current-epoch disputes and fast-track reviews, plus at most one carry-in instance — the byte-level freeze is exclusive, Rule R13(ii); a carried-in instance is counted toward Rule R14(i) in its opening epoch, convention (C4), but its current-epoch demanders are charged here, via the carry-in term of ¯Dv , and it issues no new prompts, Rule R13(vi)); candidate-dormancy intervals (no open window, no live promotion at any tool, unfrozen); and stable promoted intervals (a live, unsuspended community entry at some tool, no open window, unfrozen). Inter-window spans — each entry is followed by at most one, ending at the next entry, retirement, or the epoch boundary, so at most E⋆ begin in or carry into the epoch — have promotion status fixed at the preceding window’s close, so each span is candidate-dormant or stable-promoted throughout, with its freeze/suspension pieces carved out; A10’s dormant-interval clause is applied per candidate-dormancy span, freeze-time demanders within a span being charged to the freeze bucket. The first three classes are the 

ledger-charged pieces; stable promoted intervals are deliberately not bucketed: a matching literal fast-paths there at the promoted tool (Rule R4(a)) at zero cost, while the gates that do fire there — handle-expressed uses of the certified pair (Rule R4(c)) and uses at unpromoted tools — are charged to the ledger residual 

below, bounded by Theorem S6.9(iii) under A11. (A stable promoted interval is “window closed, not yet resubmitted or retired” in the sense of Definition S4.6(b): first decisions logged there bank toward the item’s next entry exactly as in candidate dormancy; the taxonomy separates the classes for cost attribution only and changes no protocol rule.) We use the common conservative bound E⋆ for the window- and dormancy-bucket piece counts. (The attempt structure of Definition S5.1 partitions the same presence; the ledger’s buckets and bounds are unchanged by it.) Write 

¯Dv := 2(1 − pb) E⋆ ctool

Pipeline component and ledger residual. The rows cover the pipeline component Cpipe  

> v

: every confirmation of a pair of v charged to a ledger row under the convention below — credited weight-bearing endorsements, bucketed required confirmations (window, candidate-dormancy, and freeze/suspension time, carry-in in-cluded), and all invited-repeat, prompt, and publish confirmations of v. Required confirmations charged to 

no row are the ledger residual : those of demanders whose epoch-first item-demand falls in a stable promoted interval (in particular handle-expressed uses of a live-CC pair, which gate under Rule R4(c) despite the certificate) or outside every shared-tier presence (before first publication, after retirement, or after demotion). The residual is bounded by Theorem S6.9(iii) under A11; any overlap between the two accountings only over-counts. 

Charging convention (retrospective). At the close of the logging epoch every confirmation of v — a durable accepting pair-confirmation, counted once per pair per transaction (Definition S2.5) — is assigned to exactly one row or to the ledger residual; the ledger is a hindsight accounting device, like A10, never protocol state. The ledger rows charge protocol-bearing decisions and invited and publish events (Definition S4.6); the 

residual is defined over all required confirmations of the bytes, protocol-bearing or not — in particular outside-presence confirmations, which are unlogged as candidate decisions — so that every required confirmation in this lemma’s accounting universe (option-induced cache confirmations excluded, above) is charged somewhere, to a row or to the residual, by construction. (Definition S4.4’s retirement clause governs demand counting , not the confirmation universe of this convention.) Non-covered reasoning-target confirmations satisfying Definition S4.6 are protocol-bearing (§S4.1) and are charged to the pair 

(v, reasoning ) exactly as tool pairs are; covered reasoning gates are inert (Rule R13(i)) and excluded like any redundant confirmation. Credited weight-bearing endorsements (in-entry, banked-and-credited within the epoch, or vindication-credited; required or invited alike) are assigned to the weight row — including a banked first decision taken in a stable promoted interval, so a residual-class demander’s credited acceptance is weight-row-charged, only shrinking the residual; uncredited invited confirmations to their invitation row (re-entry or prompt; invitations are epoch-scoped, so no invitation row carries over); publish 

confirmations to the publish row; every other required confirmation to the ledger-charged piece (window, candidate-dormancy, or freeze/suspension — current-epoch instances and the at most one carry-in instance) containing its demander’s first demand of the item in the epoch, at most ctool confirmations per demander (one per pair, INV-3, A12), when that first item-demand falls in a ledger-charged piece, and to the ledger residual otherwise (first item-demand in a stable promoted interval or outside every presence). Cross-epoch crediting of an already-logged event (Definition S4.6(b), Rule R13(iv)) relabels a prior epoch’s event and adds nothing to any epoch. The four presence classes partition the item’s shared-tier presence within the epoch, so every demander’s epoch-first item-demand falls in exactly one piece or outside every presence; the assignment classes are disjoint and exhaustive over required, voluntary, and publish confirmations (redundant confirmations excluded throughout, §S6.1) — in particular every required confirmation is charged somewhere, to a bucket row or to the residual, by construction — and each row bound is an upper bound for its assigned class. Then: (i) (Once, ever, per pair.) No user’s required count for any pair (v, t ) exceeds one over the deployment’s lifetime (INV-3; exceptions per Theorem S6.2), a fortiori one per epoch; required confirmations of (v, t )

belong to distinct demanders of the pair. (ii) (Row bounds.) Every pipeline-component confirmation is assigned to exactly one row of Table S2 under the retrospective charging convention above, with the stated per-epoch bounds; cross-epoch crediting of an already-logged event (Rule R13(iv), Definition S4.6(b)) is a relabel adding no event to any epoch.

Let Cv count all required, voluntary, and publish confirmations of v’s exact bytes in the epoch, wherever they fall — ledger-charged and residual alike; redundant confirmations and option-induced cache confirmations excluded (§S6.1; Corollary S6.6). In the single-epoch model, with K := Emax (ctool N + + cpk + 1) ,

Cv ≤ ctool nv + K 1{nv ≥ 1} :

every required confirmation of a pair of v belongs to a demander of the pair — its confirmer’s own attempted use fires the gate, hence realizes the latent indicator (required-approval clause, §S6.1), inside or outside any shared-tier presence — at most ctool pairs per demander (one per pair, INV-3, A12); voluntary confirmations exist only at invitation surfaces, by definition (§S6.1), and invitations per epoch are deterministically capped at Emax ctool N + re-entry notifications plus Emax cpk suspension prompts, independently of current-epoch demand — notified endorsers and prompted adopters need not be current-epoch demanders — with joint deduplication (Definition S4.6, Rule R13(vi)); the owner contributes at most Emax publish confirmations (one accepting publish per opened entry: a cap-exhausted submission is ineligible and records no accepting confirmation; Rules R12, R14(i)). Every term vanishes on {nv = 0 }, by the event-based vanishing clause of the owner-coupling convention and the required-approval clause (§S6.1): no latent demander means no attempted use, hence no required confirmation, and no entry, review, invitation, or publish confirmation occurs in the epoch, whatever presence is carried in. Proof. (i) After a user’s own accepting confirmation of (v, t ), the local in-scope C copy covers all their reuse at t for the deployment’s lifetime (Lemma S6.1) and no third party can disturb it (Lemma S5.8); this is INV-3. 

(ii) Weight-bearing: within an entry, credited endorsements per tool number at most N + by the credit caps (Definition S4.6(f)), banked credits filling slots the same caps govern; each credited endorsement is a distinct demander’s first protocol-bearing decision, so successive entries’ credited sets are disjoint (Lemma S5.3), entries per epoch at most Emax (the entry cap of Rule R14(i), republications and post-eviction republications included). Buckets: within the epoch, the item’s shared-tier presence partitions into the four classes of the presence taxonomy: at most Emax windows, at most E⋆ candidate-dormancy pieces (one possibly carried in), the freeze/suspension intervals of the current-epoch disputes and reviews plus at most one carry-in instance, and the stable promoted intervals; every demander of v has exactly one epoch-first item-demand, in exactly one piece or outside every presence. A demander whose first item-demand falls in a window or candidate-dormancy piece has a pair-first-demand there for at least one of the item’s ≤ ctool pairs, so A10’s demand clause (scoped to exactly these two piece types) bounds window/candidate-dormancy occupancy by ctool (¯ γ n v + s0) per piece; each such demander is charged at most ctool required pair-confirmations (once per pair per transaction, Definition S2.5; once ever required, INV-3), giving the two bucket rows with the factor E⋆. Lemma S5.6 bounds each freeze or review’s arriving demanders by κ in expectation while unresolved — for the carry-in instance, counting only its current-epoch arrivals, a sub-count of the same dominated arrival sequence — each charged at most ctool ; the freeze-bucket bound ctool ¯Dv κ follows by the tower property under the demand-conditional measure (§S8.1). Remark S6.11 (Reading of Theorem S6.9) . Four observations govern the interpretation of Theorem S6.9. First, the residual charges are the design operating as intended: each residual poisoned-demand confirmation is a personal gate inspection of an unpromoted or flagged item, and each residual handle-arrival confirmation is a personal gate inspection of bytes that reached this user through an untrusted channel — the certificate amortises trusted-channel reuse, never untrusted-channel arrival (Rule R4(c), S18); both are exactly the inspections the design intends (cf. A11). Second, the front-loading term is the price of certifying popular items rather than an overhead: each within-window confirmation is a fresh honest inspection before finalisation :exactly the pre-promotion scrutiny the exponent N − f of Theorem S5.13 counts. Third, the bound is informative when ¯γ ≲ 1/B fl ; for larger ¯γ it remains valid but loose, a bounded multiple of d, with Theorem S6.2 the binding guarantee throughout. Fourth, these are conditional accounting statements. Clauses (i)–(ii) consume the ledger, smoothness, competence, juror-supply, and demand-shape hypotheses; clause (iii) additionally consumes A11 — the assumption carrying publication, promotion, and arrival-representation coverage jointly — together with the product condition D0(M )V (M ) = o(M ) and the remaining vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii). The shrinking-front-loading conditions describe changing time geometry, never a consequence of population growth alone (Remark S6.12); no clause derives coverage or shrinking front-loading from the protocol. The assumption-free protocol guarantee is the pathwise cap of Theorem S6.2(i), with clause (ii) giving its reference-model standalone-mass ceiling. 

S6.5 Conditional co-scaling Status of the corridor. The corridor collects, in one place, every constraint the co-scaling result uses; each row exists elsewhere and none is new. Two of its rows are schedules of protocol constants ( gN , g k), which a deployment controls; the rest are empirical or behavioural properties ( β, ¯γ, ρseat , q + pb, d, the coverage law (D0, ε ) of A11, the Sybil bounds), which a deployment measures (N17) and cannot set. The co-scaling theorem is therefore a compatibility statement: if the measured environment satisfies its rows, 

then schedules exist under which security, resolution correctness, and budget improve together; and the floor of Theorem S6.2 holds outside the corridor unconditionally.

Remark S6.12 (Operational reading of the shrinking-front-loading rows) . For steady demand over an epoch of length L with fixed window Twin , ¯γ ≈ Twin /L independently of M (A10, commentary): population 

> 113

PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

growth alone does not shrink ¯γ. The rows ¯γ(M )(1 + κ(M )) = o(1) and ¯γ(M )k(M ) → 0 therefore describe deployments whose time geometry changes with scale — finalisation windows shrinking relative to the epoch, or demand growing more diffuse as the population grows — and are to be read as measured properties of that geometry, not consequences of M → ∞ . Where they fail, the fixed-¯γ reading of Theorem S6.9 is the operating point: pipeline cost falls to the front-loading multiple Bfl ¯γ d good of the standalone single-user shareable mass, with Theorem S6.2 binding throughout (Remark S6.11). Remark S6.14 (Role of the saturation exponent) . Smaller β is uniformly favourable: faster budget decay 

M −(1 −β) and a weaker Sybil requirement f (M ) = o(M 1−β ). There is no design trade-off along β: it is not a design parameter but an empirical property of the community’s demand stream, to be measured (N17); Theorem S5.11, which references neither β nor the demand model, remains available at any β under its own hypotheses (Remark S5.12). A worked Zipf instantiation, including the tail, is §S8.5.
