S7.2 Design rationale 

Four rationale remarks whose content adjoins specific formal results (the freshness asymmetry, the reading of 

q, the accountability calibration, and the roles of the protocol clocks) are set as numbered Remarks adjacent to their points of use (Remarks S4.7, S5.19, S5.5, S5.26) and are indexed here for completeness. The notes below record the remaining design rationale. 

116 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

Note N1 (Model lineage) . The target property is an integrity property: content from an untrusted source must not drive a sensitive action unless the user explicitly authorised the exact value involved. Integrity is the lattice dual of Denning’s confidentiality flow Denning (1976); Denning & Denning (1977), made precise by Biba Biba (1977); the behavioural definition is noninterference Goguen & Meseguer (1982), proved by the unwinding method Goguen & Meseguer (1984) transported to a small-step operational semantics Volpano et al. (1996); Sabelfeld & Myers (2003). Two refinements distinguish PerMIT. First, PerMIT does permit untrusted-derived values to reach sensitive actions, but only after a user confirmation; a confirmation is an endorsement Myers et al. (2006), and the danger that an attacker controls what is endorsed is ruled out by robustness Zdancewic & Myers (2005); Myers et al. (2006). Second, PerMIT lets verified crystallized knowledge inform the Protected LLM’s reasoning; the right noninterference statement is therefore one modulo the release history, in the delimited- and gradual-release tradition Sabelfeld & Sands (2009); Sabelfeld & Myers (2004); Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007), with the display transcript playing the role of the release-event history. The carve-out is the full transcript: candidate previews as well as decision-pane displays, rejections as well as acceptances (Definition S3.8). The substrate, including the provenance-tracking interpreter and the separation of a trusted orchestrating model from a quarantined reader, is CaMeL Debenedetti et al. (2025); we name these the Protected LLM ( P ) and the Quarantined Reader LLM ( Q), realizing Q as a Recursive Language Model Zhang et al. (2025). Related efforts: AgentDojo Debenedetti et al. (2024), information-flow control for agents Costa et al. (2025), memory-poisoning defenses Wei et al. (2025), collaborative memory Rezazadeh et al. (2025). CaMeL leaves formal verification as future work (Debenedetti et al., 2025, §10). PerMIT leaves planning and tool choice to the agent while bounding its observations and data flows through the finite interface of Figures S2–S3, including tool-output capture and argument mediation. The channel analysis (Lemmas S3.17–S3.18) checks this written specification clause by clause. 

Note N2 (The endorsement unit) . Endorsement is per (value, tool) and cached indefinitely while the relevant local copy is retained. A conforming instantiation of the requirements-level Option O3 would bound its scope-checked tool-authorization lifetime, but is outside the proved model. Combinations, orderings, and timings of separately endorsed values are ungated. The policy does not bind argument position, task, purpose, or the complete argument tuple. Reasoning Integrity excludes additional hidden influence when the complete display transcript is fixed; it does not certify the safety of later combinations (S10, S11). Options O1–O2 refine tool-scoped checks, not complete-transaction approval. 

Note N3 (The reader asymmetry) . Q may read the whole store because its output is always untrusted or handle-hidden; RE may read only the endorsed store because its output flows to P as readable endorsed bytes. Neither can be relaxed without breaking a theorem: a U-reading RE violates Theorem S3.25; an 

E-emitting Q violates Lemma S3.14. 

Note N4 (Failure indistinguishability, operationally) . Under Rule R7, the agent cannot distinguish “the untrusted store contained nothing useful” from “the reader failed while looking”: both worlds return the same operation-determined handle, so neither the contents nor the volume of untrusted memory can signal through the failure path. Likewise, whether an identifier is occupied by a U-block is a fact the attacker influences through the number of blocks captured per call, which is why occupancy is never observable (Rule R10(b)). The store-quota policy of convention (C7) exists because a volume-dependent error would reopen exactly this channel (S7). 

Note N5 (The human channel) . A6 idealizes the user’s typing as exogenous. In reality, displayed content can steer what the user subsequently types, which enters as trusted input. Influence of displayed content on typing is accounted for exactly insofar as that content appears in the display transcript, which, by Rule R8(e), records 

every attacker-influenceable rendering the user sees, on both surfaces. The design therefore discharges the idealization’s practical content by logging both surfaces: the release history covers every surface through which such influence can pass, so Corollary S3.26 and N7 remain faithful even where A6 frays. The residual is disclosed at S9 and §S7.4.  

> 117 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Note N6 (The batch gate is all-or-nothing) . The selected batch design is all-or-nothing: a per-page decision returned to P would leak the attacker-influenceable page count (Rule R9(c)), and the aggregate decision avoids that channel at the cost that one poisoned element forces rejection of the whole batch — an availability and attention cost, not a safety gap. Sufficiency of this design is what is established; no impossibility of alternative fixed-shape or padded designs is claimed. The user may reject at any page, aborting the remainder (Rule R9(d)), so attacker-imposed attention is bounded by the user’s own patience, never compelled. Both facts argue for sizing cbatch conservatively (S12). 

Note N7 (The transcript is recorded, not prevented) . The harness records every display event, selection renderings included, in a hash-chained audit log, a tamper-evident materialization of DT . Corollary S3.26 therefore has operational force: the transcript supplies a complete record of the modeled release surfaces relevant to the protected behaviours — ungated sinks, Protected-LLM operations, tool invocations (Def-inition S3.11) — missing no surface on which attacker-shaped content was ever rendered; it does not by itself reconstruct causation, timing, unreleased tool outputs, or attacker identity. This is not a proof that the user cannot be manipulated; it is a proof that any attacker influence on those behaviours must pass through the logged display transcript or an explicitly accepted endorsement. Logging makes influence visible and attributable after the fact — attributable to a recorded release or decision, not necessarily to an attacker identity; the prevention half of the design is Theorem S3.21’s gate, which stops any value that is neither user-typed nor endorsed (personally, or, under delegation, by a qualifying community quorum) for the exact sensitive tool from ever crossing that boundary (argument-value integrity: the decision to invoke a tool, and zero-argument sensitive calls, are scoped at §S7.4(X)). 

Note N8 (What previews can and cannot do) . An adversarial reader can decorate a malicious candidate with a benign-looking preview. Within the formal model, previews are inert: they never enter σP (Lemma S3.12), and endorsement binds to the true resolved bytes on the decision pane (A3–A4), so a preview can never forge an endorsement. Outside the formal model, a preview can influence the human: what the user chooses to examine, and, through A6’s idealization, what the user subsequently types. This is precisely why previews are included in the display transcript and the audit log (S9). 

Note N9 (What the gate does to the detection task) . The confirmation gate reshapes the detection task an honest inspector faces, in three architectural ways. First, stealth is eliminated by construction: Rule R8 renders the exact bytes that will flow, in full, on every gated invocation, so no gated content can reach a sensitive action unseen — there is no channel by which poison avoids display altogether. Second, hijacking-style content — bytes written to be read as instructions rather than data — is surfaced on the decision pane out of the narrative context it was designed to exploit, isolated against a fixed provenance zone (A3–A4); this plausibly raises qpre for that class of poison, but it is offered as motivation for the parameter, never as a proof of it: qpre remains an empirical, weakest-target quantity (theorem S5.19), estimated by logging, not derived from the display design. Third, because every gate decision is logged (Definition S3.8), q is made log-measurable rather than merely posited: the validation protocol of N17 is only possible because the architecture records the events it needs. None of this touches semantic poison — content that is simply false or misleading, with no injection mechanism for display to expose — which gains nothing from being shown out of context and remains exactly the disclosed residual of S14, consumed by no proof in this document. 

Note N10 (Federated lineage) . The federated tier is built on machinery network security has already made rigorous: threshold endorsement from the PGP web of trust Maurer (1996); transparency and revocation from Certificate Transparency Laurie et al. (2013; 2021); the identity-cost requirement of the Sybil literature Douceur (2002); Dwork & Naor (2007) , the precise reason the scaling results are conditional on an attested or cost-bounded setting. The panel’s competence condition is the classical jury condition Grofman et al. (1983); Allen et al. (2021); Martel et al. (2024). 

Note N11 (What pool diversity buys) . The requirement Dpool caps per-pool verdicts and mixes the fresh-inspector population, attacking correlated blind spots and demand-side selection bias among panelists. Diversity bounds correlation , not the minimum : since the adversary chooses whom to present the item to (§S8.1), q remains a uniform lower bound over the honest identities the adversary can target, and the  

> 118 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

pool caps of Definition S4.3 force those targets to span Dpool pools rather than one cluster, raising the effective bound from the most fallible cluster toward the most fallible identity per pool, never to a population average. The per-person correlation across logged re-inspections is discharged structurally by fresh-inspection counting (INV-1); the residual across unlogged personal inspections is a calibration matter (theorem S5.19, Definition S4.5, S13). 

Note N12 (Reputation farming) . f , Wadv , and fs are lifetime totals over every identity the adversary ever controls (§S4.1), capped by the attestation layer’s issuance; farming weight before spending it changes when 

the capacity exists, never its total, so no bound stated against the lifetime totals can be outrun. Publisher penalties (Rule R13(vii)) only shrink the adversary’s capacity below these totals, so every bound stated against them remains valid. The economic deterrent of burning accrued reputation is real but deliberately unused in any proof: the proofs assume the attacker spends everything (S3). 

Note N13 (The evict residual) . evict is owner-scoped but P -invocable and ungated, so an erring (not attacker-steered, which Lemmas S3.12–S3.18 exclude) Protected LLM can remove its owner’s endorsed blocks without a confirmation; the no-deletion guarantee of Lemma S5.8 is a third-party guarantee only. Deployments wanting first-party cover may gate evict on E-labelled blocks like any sensitive call, a further monotone hardening in the sense of Proposition S3.28. The uniform-ack totalisation of evict (Figure S3, convention (v)) changes only the reply surface, never this capability: the residual is unchanged, and the realised outcome remains visible to the owner in the audit log. The residual includes evictions P performs in reaction to legitimate federation events (notifications are E-level log data): a third party can occasion such a reaction but not steer it — Lemmas S3.12–S3.18 exclude any U-derived influence on the choice — so this is erring-agent behaviour, outside the threat model (§S7.4(V)), not an attacker channel. 

Note N14 (Availability degrades gracefully, never destructively) . All federated guarantees are safety guaran-tees; availability is explicitly not guaranteed, but its loss is bounded in harm (in required confirmations) per dispute: under A9, at most κ = ( k+1) /ρ seat affected demander arrivals in expectation, with exponential tail (Lemma S5.6); if A12 is additionally read over the entire unresolved-dispute demand horizon, at most 

ctool κ additional required confirmations in expectation, with the corresponding tail at thresholds scaled by 

ctool ; under the stated per-epoch reading of A12, only the corresponding per-epoch conversion is asserted, while the pathwise per-user cap remains valid for a freeze of any duration (Lemma S5.24(ii)); pathwise, independent of every federated and demand assumption (persistence exceptions per INV-3), no affected user exceeds the single-user cap, regardless of the freeze’s duration (Lemma S5.24(ii)); recoverable (low wrongful-eviction risk, Proposition S5.20); accountable on both sides (Rule R13(v),(vii)); and never destruc-tive (Lemma S5.8): in the worst case the shared tier contributes nothing and each user verifies personally, within the single-user cap of Theorem S6.2. The per-epoch free charge renews, so the adversary retains a per-epoch throughput bounded in the three currencies of Lemma S5.24(iv); what it buys is bounded required-confirmation harm and bounded per-epoch harassment throughput (Lemma S5.24(ii),(iv)), never deletion — the wall-clock duration of an unresolved freeze is deliberately unbounded, resolution being demand-paced with no deadline (Remark S5.21); its harm, not its duration, is what is capped. A promoted benign item can be harassed only through Suspended-state fast-track panels, at most Emax per item per epoch (Rule R13(vi)) and penalty-escalating. An unpromoted benign candidate cannot be retired by sequential vindicated disputes (Rule R14(i)(b) exempts them, N15): each adversarial rejection buys one freeze, resolved at the item’s own demand rate, while occupying a rejection slot and, beyond the first per identity per epoch, escalating the rejecter’s accountability; the adversary’s per-epoch freeze throughput against any item being bounded (Lem. S5.24(iv)), what it buys is bounded required-confirmation harm within the single-user cap — of unbounded wall-clock duration, the freeze being demand-paced — never retirement, and the single-initiation clause caps any identity, adversarial or honest, at one freeze per item per epoch, so per-item harassment throughput is at most f freezes per epoch deterministically. The entry cap of Rule R14(i) is likewise availability-only: an item whose per-epoch entries are exhausted (e.g. through repeated wrongful evictions, each already exponentially rare, Proposition S5.20) waits for the epoch boundary with its banked credits and any live promotion status intact (convention (C4)), each demander meanwhile paying at most the cap (Theorem S6.2(i)). Under this invalidating semantics, a suspension additionally converts its demanders’  

> 119 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

ungated reuse into at most one required confirmation each — the cap confirmation they would in any case owe on demotion (Corollary S6.5) — after which the pair is permanently covered by a local C copy; this is priced once per demander and never renewed. 

Note N15 (The vindication exemption) . Rule R14(i)(b) counts only non-vindicated disputes toward expiry. The exemption is what prevents both an adversary’s wrongful rejections (each vindicated, each feeding the accountability schedule of Rule R13(v)) and the honest wrongful-rejection noise, which arrives at rate 1 − pb

per benign fresh inspection and would otherwise, at the co-scaling thresholds N (M ) → ∞ of Theorem S6.13, retire every item before promotion became arithmetically reachable, from terminating a benign item. Under the default bars every resolved dispute ends in vindication or eviction and every unresolved one ends in expiry, so this trigger fires only through the optional neither-bar outcome of Rule R13(iv); it remains availability-only and conservative (Lemma S5.9). 

Note N16 (Revocation, re-verification, and grandfathering) . Post-promotion suspensions and re-verification add panel and re-inspection events but never multiply any user’s required cost (Corollary S6.5): local endorsements are permanent, so revocation campaigns tax the shared tier’s voluntary and verdict effort, bounded per suspension by Rule R13(vi) and the rate limits; a prior CC -only user additionally owes their first-ever required confirmation of the pair — a cost within the cap, already counted inside d∞ 

> tool

(Corollary S6.5), never beyond it. Re-verification draws its inspectors from identities with no prior logged decision on the bytes (log-fresh, Definition S4.5; a cohort that can include previously exposed but never-logged inspectors, theorem S5.19), so the probability of a uniformly convincing poison remaining uncaught across Erv re-verification rounds, each reaching a cohort of new inspectors, is at most (1 − qc)Erv → 0, with qc the correlated catch rate of S13; the mechanism’s strength scales with the supply of never-decided inspectors (population growth and new demand), not with epoch renewal. Grandfathering: items promoted at lower historical thresholds retain CC but enter the re-verification queue until their endorsement meets the current bar. Within the formal semantics, first-party eviction aside (N13), nothing re-forces a confirmed pair’s authorization; a conforming instantiation of the requirements-level Option O3 would re-force exactly the scope-checked tool authorization on lease expiry — never reasoning readability — an explicit trade stated and to be priced with that extension (O3). 

Note N17 (Validation without a user study) . Theorem S6.2 and the counting cores of Lemmas S6.7, S6.8 are identities. Saturation ( V (M ) = o(M )), fractional temporal smoothness (A10: log each item’s realised front-loading fraction γv, validated specifically on the most-demanded items, where population averages would hide spikes), coverage sufficiency (A11: log each item’s realized residual fraction Uv,t /n v,t and the fast-path-vs-gate split of live-CC demand — the handle-arrival fraction, S18 — stratified on the most-demanded items, with per-item time-to-promotion against accumulated demand), tool-spread (A12: log per-item distinct-tool counts), bounded demand mass (A13: log per-user distinct shareable-item demand per epoch across deployment growth), and the verdict rate (A9: log verdict-eligibility rates on disputed items) are modelling assumptions checked by logging in the prototype and by replaying the promotion and demand-paced panel mechanics in simulation while sweeping the growth exponent, a replay of an algorithm, not an experiment on people. The replay’s own fidelity is checked independently of any behavioural parameter by the closed form of §S8.2, whose support-level consequences are byte-identity properties of the implementation rather than statistical agreements. One caveat governs the behavioural parameters specifically: qpre , qpan , pb,and ρseat are uniform conditional lower bounds, each validated on its own stratum (gate inspections vs. eligible verdicts), on the prior-atom σ-algebras of §S8.1; aggregate log rates do not directly estimate such bounds. Their validation therefore reports stratified rates (by identity class, presentation context, and flag state) rather than population averages, uses lower confidence bounds rather than point estimates, corrects for adaptive selection of inspection targets, and monitors nonstationarity across epochs; the estimand is the infimum over strata, and the deployed constants ( N, k, m margins, θacc , cceil ) are set against that infimum with margin, never against a mean. These stratified, lower-confidence-bound estimates are falsification and calibration evidence: they can refute an assumed cap — a stratum whose observed rate falls below the claimed bound — and can inform recalibration, but they do not certify the uniform infimum-over-history bounds qpre , q pan , p b require. Sparse or unobserved strata (rare identity classes, rare presentation contexts)  

> 120 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

are not covered by any finite sample, and the uniform bounds remain modelling hypotheses, not measured quantities. One further caveat: the classification Bad is exogenous (Definition S4.10), so the log alone cannot label a trial as poisoned or benign; estimating qpre , qpan , and pb therefore requires an exogenously labeled corpus — red-team–injected known-poison items and expert-adjudicated benign samples — with the stratified lower-confidence-bound machinery applied to the labeled trials. Unlabeled log rates validate the protocol mechanics only ( ρseat , realised γv, Uv,t /n v,t , tool spread, demand mass). 

Note N18 (Total effort, including rejections) . The ledger of Lemma S6.7 covers every counted (required or voluntary) durable accepting pair-confirmation of shareable items, redundant covered-gate acceptances and the personal, per-task, and option-induced streams excluded or separately charged (§S6.1, Corollary S6.6); rejection and attention effort is a distinct resource with a two-sided account. Under the durable semantics of Rule R2 there are no accepted-but-uncommitted confirmations: every accepting review commits its copy. What remains uncounted is attention: rejected dialogs, abandonment, batch-page review, repeated reviews of an already-accepted pair within one transaction (counted once, Definition S2.5), and redundant covered-gate decisions. Protocol-effective rejections are tightly capped: one dispute-initiation per identity per byte string per epoch (Rule R13(i)), at most rmax open slots, and the accountability schedule of Rule R13(v). Rejection dialogs , by contrast, recur: a user who rejects an uncovered pair holds no local C copy, so each later attempted use of that pair fires the gate again, and the later rejections are logged as corroboration or advisory flags (Rule R13(i)) — the protocol does not bound a user’s total rejection dialogs on one value. What bounds them is the user’s own use stream and the exposure caps: per retrieval at most ccand candidates are surfaced (Rule R14(ii)), disputed items are flagged and ordered last, paraphrase spam consumes the submitter’s rate limit per byte-distinct variant (Rule R12, Corollary S5.27), and a P -emitted confirm or handle-expressed tool call on a covered pair fires a covered gate, protocol-inert on both branches (Rule R13(i)), reasoning-target repetition on locally readable bytes included (reasoning coverage, Rule R13(i)): the resulting redundant confirmations and rejections have no shared-tier effect and are excluded from every ledger count (§S6.1), so agent repetition on covered values costs the user attention only, disclosed here — and P can re-emit confirm 

on a repeatedly-rejected, uncovered handle every step: covered-gate inertness neutralises covered values only, by design. Repeated dialogs arising from the user’s own repeated attempts at an uncovered pair are a first-party cost of declining, of the same character as the evict residual (N13): attacker-steered repetition is excluded (Lemmas S3.12–S3.18), but the model places no constant on it. Accordingly, every “bounded-harm” statement in this document is a statement about required user approvals (Theorem S6.2), with inspection effort disclosed here. One deployment device reduces this attention cost without touching the semantics: the decision pane may visibly badge a covered gate — the resolved bytes byte-match a local in-scope copy, an owner-side deterministic fact (Rule R13(i)) — and offer a one-tap accept; the gate, its display, and its logged decision are unchanged. Interpreter-level rate-limiting of repeated uncovered gate requests is a candidate further hardening, monotone in the sense of Proposition S3.28; it is an operational-semantics change requiring its own transition, transcript, and two-run treatment, and is not specified in this document. 

S7.3 Security and robustness analysis 

Each entry states an attack vector or failure mode in its strongest form ( Vector ), the facts that govern it (Disposition ), and its Status per §S7.1. Entries are grouped by attack surface. 

S7.3.1 Attacks on the endorsement mechanism 

Analysis S1 (Paraphrase-variant families) . Vector. Freshness is byte-keyed (INV-1). An adversary submits paraphrase variants of the same poison as distinct byte strings; every inspector, including one already fooled by a sibling variant, is fresh again on each variant, so the per-person discharge of correlation is byte-scoped, not content-scoped. Disposition. The discharge of per-person correlation is indeed per byte identity (convention (C5)), and the arithmetic is unchanged: each variant is a distinct item consuming the submitter’s rate limit and its own union-bound term (Rule R12, Corollary S5.27), and per-retrieval exposure is capped at ccand (Rule R14(ii), N18). What survives is an interpretation obligation on the deployer: q inside  

> 121 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

each union-bound term must be calibrated as the catch rate against the variant family , not against a single presentation; an inspector fooled by one variant is plausibly likelier to be fooled by its siblings. Deployments may additionally surface an advisory-only similarity flag (“similar disputed or evicted items exist”) alongside disputed items, of the zero-authority advisory class of Rule R13(i): it informs inspectors without granting the model computing similarity any protocol effect. Status. Mechanism disposed; calibration duty disclosed. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S2 (Self-dispute) . Vector. Reject one’s own item; fill the panel with one’s own verdicts; sustain the dispute across epochs via one’s own demand events. Disposition. The case-(b) bound of Theorem S5.13 does not condition on the rejecter’s identity, so self-dispute gains only the panel term already counted, while consuming a rejection slot and accountability exposure; verdict capacity never renews (INV-5), capping the adversary’s tally at fs for the dispute’s life; self-initiations are capped at one per identity per item per epoch (Rule R13(i)). Status. Disposed (under stated assumptions; Theorem S5.13, INV-5). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S3 (Reputation farming) . Vector. Farm attestation weight and reputation over time; spend it in one campaign. Disposition. f , Wadv , fs are lifetime totals over every identity the adversary ever controls (§S4.1); every bound is stated against the totals, so farming shifts timing, never capacity (N12). The economic deterrent of burning reputation is real but deliberately unused: the proofs assume the attacker spends everything. Status. Disposed (under stated assumptions; lifetime-total definition). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S4 (Tool-targeted poison) . Vector. Disputes are byte-level but harm is tool-level: a poison dangerous at tool A and innocuous at tool B is sincerely vindicated by a panel drawn from B-demanders, contradicting honest A-rejecters; the freeze lifts and the interrupted entry resumes. Disposition. The two promotion paths behave differently. Ordinary path at A: disposed. Promotion at A still requires N credited fresh endorsements at A itself (Lemma S3.14, Definition S4.6), where inspectors face the harmful use, so the case-(a) term of Theorem S5.13 applies with qpre read at A. Panel path: residual. The panel term is not 

bounded by A-context competence: qpan is uniform over the jurors’ own tool contexts (Theorem S5.13(c)), and for A-targeted, B-benign poison it may be near zero — sincere B-juror vindication is then likely, the freeze lifts, and the entry resumes toward its A-threshold. What remains against A-promotion is the ordinary-path term, plus renewable re-freezes: each epoch, each honest identity’s first rejecting decision on the bytes — in particular each new A-context detection — re-freezes promotion at every tool (Rule R13(i)–(ii)). 

Accountability erosion. Honest A-rejecters contradicted by B-vindications feed Rule R13(v); mitigation is threefold: the free first charge per epoch, the per-fresh-inspection rate trigger calibrated above the honest baseline (theorem S5.5), and conversion to zero-authority advisory flags at the ceiling. We deliberately do 

not exempt rejecters whose vindicating jurors sat at a different tool: because a rejection at any tool freezes all tools (Rule R13(ii)), such an exemption would let an adversary file every rejection “at” an obscure tool no juror will ever demand, harvesting freezes with zero accountability exposure and breaking the throughput bounds of Lemma S5.24(iv). The per-pair scoping variant of Remark S4.9 removes this residual; it is a deployment choice, consumed by no theorem. Status. Ordinary-path promotion at the targeted tool disposed; panel-path vindication under cross-tool juries residual, disclosed; accountability erosion residual, mitigated, availability-only. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S5 (Panel-lapse exploitation and slot economics) . Vector. Reject a low-demand benign item; let the panel lapse; tax the owner’s rate limit on each forced re-entry. Disposition. The attack presupposes a deadline-based design; there is no panel lapse and no forced re-entry (Lemma S5.24(iii)): the only re-entry paths are owner-initiated, and slots free on resolution or expiry. Roles of the clocks: theorem S5.26. Status. 

Disposed (under stated assumptions; Lemma S5.24(iii)). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S6 (Juror exhaustion by wide pre-exposure) . Vector. Pre-expose the poison so widely that few verdict-eligible (fresh, seat-weighted) demanders remain; the panel never fills. Disposition. The item stalls frozen : promotion is blocked at every tool with no deadline to exploit (Rule R13(ii)–(iii)); the cost is availability, never safety, borne by the adversary’s own submission, and the benign-item variant is bounded at the cap (Remark S5.25, Lemma S5.24(ii)). Pre-exposure also feeds the defence: each additional honest  

> 122 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

rejection opportunity can freeze promotion (INV-2), and consumed honest identities are never re-usable for later entries of the same bytes (Corollary S5.27). Status. Safety disposed + availability residual (under Theorem S5.13’s hypotheses; deterministic failure mode of A9, disclosed at its commentary). Not used in any proof. 

S7.3.2 Side channels and covert channels 

Analysis S7 (Store-exhaustion side channel) . Vector. The store is idealized as unbounded; a real deployment has quotas, and quota errors visible to P leak the attacker-chosen volume of captured blocks. Disposition. 

Correct, which is why the quota policy is a stated convention rather than an afterthought: deployments must apply quotas to the untrusted tier by silent eviction, never by an error visible to P (convention (C7)); otherwise store exhaustion reopens exactly the channel Rule R7 closes (N4). Under the convention, no operation’s defined behaviour depends on aggregate store volume, and Lemma S3.18(f) stands. Status. 

Disposed (under stated assumptions), conditional on the stated deployment policy (C7). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S8 (Timing channels) . Vector. The bisimulation matches P -operations; wall-clock timing of the harness is unmodelled and could leak. Disposition. Conceded: timing and termination are out of scope (§S7.4). The interface returns no clock or step-count value, so no timing value exists in σP to branch on (Lemma S3.18(e)); a clock consulted through a tool is already inside the model: its reading is captured to 

MU like any tool output (Rule R3) and never reaches σP . The exclusion concerns only implicit timing: wall-clock latency of the harness observed by an outside party. Status. Residual, precisely scoped (§S7.4(IV)). Not used in any proof. 

S7.3.3 The human factor 

Analysis S9 (Preview manipulation) . Vector. An adversarial reader decorates a malicious candidate with a benign-looking preview and steers the user. Disposition. Within the formal model, previews are inert: they never enter σP (Lemma S3.12), and endorsement binds to the true resolved bytes on the decision pane (A3–A4), so a preview can never forge an endorsement: the wrong value is displayed truthfully and rejected under A4. Outside the formal model, a preview can influence what the user chooses to examine and, through A6’s idealization, what the user subsequently types. That residual is covered by design rather than assumed away: previews are part of the display transcript and the audit log (Definition S3.8, Rule R8(e)), so the release history covers every surface through which such influence can pass, and Corollary S3.26 remains faithful even where A6 frays (N5, N8). Status. Disposed (under stated assumptions) within the model; residual at the human channel, disclosed. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S10 (Careless endorsement and the post-endorsement channel) . Vector. After one careless narrative confirmation (a false acceptance in the sense of Definition S4.10; a failure of A5), the endorsed summary’s bytes are readable and flow, literal-expressed, to data tools ungated (handle-expressed use gates regardless, Rule R4(c)); a poisoned summary can both mislead reasoning and direct data-tool egress. Disposition. 

Correct, and no theorem is violated: the influence entered through a transcript event the user accepted. The compound shape is: one careless narrative endorsement (a failure of A5) simultaneously (i) admits attacker-shaped content into P ’s readable state and (ii) opens ungated literal-expressed data-tool egress of endorsed and typed bytes (Rule R4(b); handle-expressed use gates regardless, Rule R4(c)); the misled P

may then recombine separately endorsed values at sensitive tools without further gates (S11). Containment is threefold: the influence never crosses a sensitive boundary with bytes neither user-typed nor endorsed for that tool (Theorem S3.21); its triggering endorsement is a logged transcript event, and any divergence it induces in the protected behaviours is transcript-carried (Corollary S3.26); and the community mechanism reduces the frequency of the triggering endorsement for shareable content (Appendix S4). In the federated tier the residual carries a further, deliberate cost: an honest user who carelessly confirms and then publishes 

attacker-crafted content will, on that item’s panel eviction, incur the publisher penalty of Rule R13(vii), a corrective on the publish gate; the penalty never touches any local store (Lemma S5.8, Proposition S5.22).  

> 123 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

Option O2 removes the ungated data-tool egress of out-of-scope endorsed bytes (each such flow then gates once per pair, Corollary S3.23(b)); Option O3, if instantiated conformingly, would limit only scope-checked local tool authorization; data-tool enforcement requires Option O2, and readable reasoning state is unaffected (O3). Status. Residual, disclosed, contained, and priced. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S11 (Recombination of endorsed values) . Vector. Individually authorized values can form a harmful transaction without violating Action Integrity: approval of a recipient and a body separately for an email tool does not record approval of sending that body to that recipient. Order, timing, and task context can also change the meaning of reuse. Disposition. Recombination is ungated by design. Theorem S3.25 excludes additional hidden influence for matched runs with equal complete display transcripts. It does not exclude influence through changed displays, incorrect endorsements, or inappropriate reuse in a later context. Option O1 narrows typed-value authority by sensitive tool, and Option O2 extends exact-tool checks to data tools; neither binds the complete transaction. Human or agent memory managers could review combined content and seek fresh endorsements before it becomes trusted, as proposed in the main paper (Appendix D.2.1). This is untested future work, not a guarantee for all later combinations. Context-bound or transaction-level authorization would require extended semantics and revised confirmation-cost accounting. 

Status. Policy boundary and endorsement residual disclosed. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S12 (Batch-gate attention) . Vector. The batch gate displays every element’s bytes, but displayed-in-full is not reviewed-in-full; A4 is weakest here. Disposition. Conceded: Definition S3.10 holds literally at the batch gate (every element’s bytes are displayed, Rule R9(b)), but the human premise A4 is at its weakest, and deployments should size cbatch conservatively. The selected batch design is all-or-nothing: a per-page decision returned to P would leak the attacker-influenceable page count, and the aggregate decision avoids that channel at the cost that one poisoned element forces rejection of the whole batch: an availability and attention cost, not a safety gap (no impossibility of alternative fixed-shape or padded designs is claimed); and the user may reject at any page, aborting the remainder, so attacker-imposed attention is bounded by the user’s own patience, never compelled (Rule R9(c),(d), N6). Deployments may additionally cap total batch cardinality (Rule R9(b)), converting oversize collections into a fixed, decision-free reject; the cap lies outside the mechanized batch layer (§S9). Status. Mechanism disposed; residual at the human premise, disclosed. Not used in any proof. 

S7.3.4 Adverse parameter regimes 

Analysis S13 (Cross-person correlated carelessness) . Vector. A uniformly convincing poison lowers every fresh inspector’s catch rate together; the exponential bounds assume independently varying carelessness and collapse toward 1 − qc under engineered correlation. Disposition. The premise of the vector is corrected first: the bounds of Theorem S5.13 assume no independence across inspectors; they assume a uniform conditional lower bound q on each fresh inspection given the entire past (§S8.1). What engineered cross-person correlation attacks is that bound itself: a uniformly convincing poison is one for which the honest conditional catch rate, after conditioning on earlier misses , is some qc < q . Three facts limit the damage: the theorem remains valid with q read as qc, the bound degrading toward 1 − qc transparently, never silently; the diversity requirement Dpool mixes fresh-inspector pools, raising qc (N11); and periodic re-verification re-presents the bytes to inspectors with no prior decision, so the probability of remaining uncaught across Erv rounds is at most (1 − qc)Erv , scaling with the supply of never-decided inspectors, not with epoch renewal (N16). The worst-case fallback as qc → 0 is the local layer of Remark S5.12, not Theorem S5.11, whose q = 1 hypothesis engineered correlation directly attacks. The per-person half of the correlation concern (re-inspection of the same bytes is not re-randomisation) is discharged structurally for 

logged inspections: no logged repeat carries weight or a verdict (INV-1, INV-5); across unlogged personal inspections (Definition S4.5) it survives as a calibration obligation inside the log-fresh class (theorem S5.19). 

Status. Residual with a three-layer cap, disclosed. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S14 (Unrecognizable poison, q → 0). Vector. If no honest reviewer can recognize the poison, the probabilistic bounds are vacuous. Disposition. Conceded, and the guarantee is calibrated to what a human- 

> 124 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

verification design can coherently mean by “safe”: content is treated as safe when the qualifying community, inspecting the exact bytes at the exact tool, accepts it. Content that fools every honest inspector is, within this paradigm, indistinguishable from truth, and no design that routes trust through human judgment can promise otherwise. What the design does promise is one-sided and strong: it takes only one honest effective qualifying rejection — a rejecting decision at a protocol-bearing gate by an identity currently holding dispute-initiation authority (Rule R13(i),(v)) — to freeze promotion immediately; detections by authority-inactive identities are advisory flags, but the same identities’ acceptances carry no promotion weight (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18), so silencing states never trade a lost rejection for a harvestable acceptance; shared-presence freshness ensures the attacker can never harvest a second weight-bearing acceptance from any identity that has already logged a decision on the bytes (INV-1, Corollary S5.27); and the moment a refuting inspection arrives, however late, it takes protocol-level effect immediately. What survives this regime is the local layer of Remark S5.12, the one-sided freeze clause above, and re-verification (N16); Theorem S5.11 does not 

apply, its q = 1 hypothesis being the exact negation of this regime. Safety alone can additionally be forced for any q > 0 by raising m toward k, at the price of a weaker wrongful-eviction bound (theorem S5.19). 

Status. Residual; the paradigm’s boundary disclosed (§S7.4(VI)). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S15 (Accountability mis-calibration) . Vector. The rate trigger and ceiling require estimating the honest baseline 1 − pb; mis-calibration can suppress honest dispute-initiation and crediting, reducing availability — and, absent authority-gated crediting (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18), it would also invalidate the uniform qpre interpretation. Disposition. The exposure is availability-only, never safety, and is bounded as follows. (i) Panel verdicts are never penalised and never charged (Rule R13(v)(a)), and verdict-eligible rejections are cast regardless of accountability state (Rule R13(v)(e)), so the panel arithmetic of Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 is independent of calibration. (ii) A capped or suspended honest identity retains advisory flags and ordinary use — though its acceptances are authority-inert for the duration (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18) — loses nothing permanent, and is restored at the epoch boundary (Rule R13(v)(d), theorem S5.5). (iii) Its ordinary, non-verdict gate inspections while dispute-initiation and crediting authority are inactive are excluded trials , not silenced misses: neither branch enters the qpre trial class — the acceptance carries no promotion weight and the rejection no initiation (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18, theorem S5.19) — so these inspections do not enter 1 − qpre . Eligible panel verdicts are different: verdict capacity is never authority-gated, and every cast eligible verdict remains a qpan trial regardless of accountability state (Rule R13(v)(a),(e), Remark S5.18, Theorem S5.13(c)). Mis-calibration may change the composition of the authority-active ordinary class, so the deployment must continue to validate the weakest-target qpre bound over that class (N17); it does not convert excluded inspections into misses. The remaining cost is availability: throttled honest dispute-initiation and delayed crediting. (iv) The calibration enters only the budget constants, through the deterministic bound cacc (Lemma S5.4, Figure S1); no safety theorem depends on it. (v) Under authority-gated crediting (Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18), mis-calibration cannot be exploited to harvest promotion weight from silenced honest identities — a silenced identity’s acceptance is authority-inert — so the exposure remains throttled honest dispute-initiation, with correspondingly delayed crediting, availability-only. What mis-calibration buys an adversary is therefore throttled honest dispute-initiation: bounded required-confirmation harm and bounded per-epoch throughput (Lemma S5.24; wall-clock delay unbounded, resolution being demand-paced, Remark S5.21), no third-party alteration of any local store (Lemma S5.8), and required cost within the single-user cap (Theorem S6.2). 

Status. Safety disposed; availability exposure residual, disclosed. Enters budget constants only. 

S7.3.5 Modelling-boundary effects 

Analysis S16 (Incentive feedback on publication) . Vector. Publisher penalties, though provably rare for benign items, depress publication; a thinner shared tier raises the effective β and weakens A11 directly at its coverage clause (late or absent publication inflates Uv,t ). Disposition. Conceded and unmodelled: the incentive feedback between the accountability layer and the demand model of Appendix S6 is outside the formal development. Its impact is confined to the budget asymptotics : the cap of Theorem S6.2 is unaffected  

> 125 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

by it (it is an identity over required confirmations), the safety theorems are unaffected (they do not reference 

β), and Proposition S5.22(ii) bounds the penalty’s probability for benign publishers at e−2δ2  

> b(k−fs)

per panel, with expected counts per epoch bounded by the dispute ledger (Prop. S5.22(ii), linear tier). Status. Residual, confined to budget asymptotics. Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S17 (Release-day demand spikes) . Vector. A popular item’s demand arrives in one burst; A10 fails, and the amortisation claims fall with it. Disposition. A release-day spike is precisely a violation of A10 

for that item (γv ≈ 1): its cost is at most the item’s own demand, once: each affected demander pays the required confirmations (at most one per demanded pair, INV-3) that the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) already bounds (whose counting identities never use A10), and every other item’s accounting is untouched, since the ledger of Lemma S6.7 is per-item. Degradation under spikes is therefore item-local, bounded by Theorem S6.2, and transient; the window- and dormancy-bucket rows of the ledger are exactly where within-window demand, diversity wait, propagation delay, and dormant intervals are collected, and A10 caps them at the fraction ¯γ of each item’s own demand, so popularity never enters the per-user bound (Lemma S6.8). A pre-publication demand burst is likewise an A11 violation for that item (its early demanders are residual), priced identically at the cap. Status. Disposed (under stated assumptions): the failure mode is priced by the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)). Not used in any proof. 

Analysis S18 (Handle-mediated arrival of certified content) . Vector. Not an attack but a representation effect: a pair (v, t ) is live-CC , yet a user’s copy of v arrives through an untrusted channel (a message, a tool output) and is therefore held only as an opaque handle (Lemma S3.12); passing the handle to t gates (Rule R4(c)) despite the certificate, and, absent a local in-scope copy, the confirmation is required. A demand stream arriving predominantly handle-expressed can drive the shared required load toward the standalone mass, community certification notwithstanding. Disposition. Deliberate and structural: exempting certificate-matching handle resolutions from the gate would make the gate-vs-no-gate behaviour a function of hidden U-resolution, reopening the channel Rules R6/R7 close and breaking Theorem S3.25 (two runs whose handles resolve to different CC -matching byte strings would flow ungated under equal transcripts with different sink projections); the gate on untrusted-channel arrivals is the inspection the design intends (Remark S6.11). The cost is bounded on both sides: each such confirmation is once ever per (user, pair) (INV-3), the epoch’s shared required load never exceeds the standalone mass (Theorem S6.2(ii)), and the accepting user thereafter holds a local in-scope copy, fast-pathing all literal reuse. Amortisation applies to the fast-path-expressed share of certified demand — notably trusted retrieval, which returns live entries as readable E-bytes byte-matching the entry (Definition S4.1, Rules R14(ii), R4(a)); the handle-arrival fraction is priced inside ε(M ) of A11 and logged directly (N17). Status. Residual, disclosed, priced in A11; bounded pathwise by Theorem S6.2(i) (persistence exceptions per INV-3). Not used in any safety proof; enters budget assumptions only. 

S7.4 Scope and residuals 

The guarantees’ boundaries, collected in one place. (I) Per-execution, per-(value, tool). Action Integrity is per execution; endorsement is per (value, tool) and cached indefinitely (a conforming instantiation of the requirements-level Option O3 would bound the scope-checked tool-authorization lifetime; O3); recombination of separately endorsed values is ungated by design (N2, S11). (II) The human channel. A6 idealizes typing as exogenous; influence through displayed content is recorded, not prevented (N5, N7, S9); Reasoning Integrity is modulo the transcript, and the compound residual is S10. (III) The batch gate. Every element’s bytes are displayed, so Definition S3.10 holds literally; but displayed-in-full is not reviewed-in-full: A4 is weakest here, and cbatch should be sized accordingly (N6, S12).  

> 126 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(IV) Timing and termination are out of scope; the exclusion concerns only implicit wall-clock channels observed from outside (S8). (V) Model intent. Isolation restricts hidden untrusted influence; it does not certify P or RE as benign or guarantee that a later combination of typed or endorsed values is appropriate. Influence through changed displays and incorrect endorsements remains possible (S10, S11). The baseline’s extensional, tool-blind trust of typed literals is Remark S3.22; Option O1 narrows that authority by sensitive tool without approving a whole transaction. One first-party residual: evict is owner-scoped but P -invocable and ungated, so an erring Protected LLM can remove its owner’s endorsed blocks; the no-deletion guarantee is third-party only, and gating evict on E-blocks is a further monotone hardening (N13). (VI) Unrecognizable poison (q → 0): no community-truthfulness guarantee survives this regime within a human-judgment design (Remark S5.12); what survives is the local layer, the one-sided freeze on any single honest recognition, and re-verification against never-decided inspectors (S14); degradation under engineered correlation is to the rate qc, transparently (S13). (VII) Federated influence through legitimate log activity is held fixed by A8 in the noninterference statement and is bounded instead by Theorems S5.11 and S5.13. (VIII) Availability is not guaranteed; a dispute’s harm in required confirmations is bounded per Lemma S5.24 and N14 (including the suspension-induced, once-per-demander confirmation, Rule R13(vi)), inspection effort disclosed separately (N18); degradation is never destructive (Lemma S5.8). (IX) Confidentiality. Only the type-level exclusion is proved (Lemma S5.2); semantic confidentiality of published content rests on the publish gate (A7). (X) Tool choice and zero-argument actions. Action Integrity constrains the values crossing sensitive tool boundaries; it does not authorize the invocation itself, and a sensitive tool with no gated arguments satisfies the value-flow property vacuously. Deployments requiring authorization of the act itself gate the capability (a monotone hardening in the sense of Prop. S3.28). (XI) The reasoning pseudo-target. Shared-tier accounting treats reasoning as a canonical pseudo-target (§S4.1): knowledge items amortise and are defended at it, and CC -at-reasoning status buys community-wide readability only, never any tool flow. 

S7.5 Summary of composed guarantees (A) One user, deployment lifetime (trust axioms A1–A6 alone). Mechanization boundary, wherever machine-checking is advertised: kernel-checked are Personal Action Integrity from the empty configuration and from arbitrary mechanized well-formed configurations; the map-free Reasoning Integrity core from empty stores and from the canonical shared-endorsed seed class; and the standalone batch-confirmation layer (§S9). Paper-only are the run-level map integration, the batch cardinality cap, Option O3 (requirements-level), arbitrary matched mid-run pairs, the index-oracle reduction (Prop. S3.29), the federated product system, and every probabilistic and budget result. Gating where influence acts: Theorem S3.21, Corollary S3.23(a). Recording where it persuades: Theorem S3.25, Corollary S3.26. (Phrasing discipline, here and in any summary elsewhere: Reasoning Integrity is a fixed-horizon, termination-insensitive equivalence modulo the full display transcript , under coupled typed input — and, federated, a coupled log (A8) — never an unconditional noninterference or inertness claim; Definition S3.11, Remark S3.9.) Residuals: the user’s own false endorsement (S10); the post-endorsement boundary (Corollary S3.23(b), O2); recombination (S11, O1). Deployments whose threat model emphasises data-tool exfiltration should select Option O2 as the default profile: it closes the baseline post-endorsement channel (Cor. S3.23(b)), is machine-checked at the gate level (§S9), and costs O(1) per reusable endorsed-value pair, freshly composed arguments remaining in the per-task stream (Cor. S6.6). Lifetime required cost: one accepting confirmation per (value, tool) relied on (Theorem S6.2, Corollary S6.5); effort scope per N18.  

> 127 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(B) Federated, deployment lifetime. Three layers, each surviving the failure of the layer above. Determin-istic (q=1 , W >W adv , m>f s): Theorem S5.11, scope Remark S5.12. Probabilistic, per poisoned attempt (Definition S5.1) (Definition S4.10): Theorem S5.13, reach-free (INV-6); per-anchor-epoch and lifetime exposure Corollary S5.27 (attempts keyed to anchor epochs, Rem. S5.28), Remark S5.29; system-wide per-anchor-epoch form under the union-bound condition of Theorem S6.13(i). Scoping caveats: S1, S4; and one explicitly: the numeric system-wide bound covers adversary-published attempts, counted by the rate limit at their anchors — poison published by honest-but-deceived identities (S10) is bounded per attempt (Thm. S5.13) and disciplined by publisher penalties (Rule R13(vii), Prop. S5.22), but its attempt count 

E[Sanc ] is not bounded in terms of the adversary’s resources alone (Cor. S5.27). Per-attempt bounds are marginal: deployment-wide exposure is controlled by summing over anchored attempts and byte-distinct variants, through the union-, expectation-, and deterministic-cap forms of Corollary S5.27 and the lifetime ceiling of Remark S5.29. Pathwise cap: Lemma S5.8 (third-party transitions; first-party residual N13) and Theorem S6.2: degradation to M independent copies of (A), never below. Budget: Theorem S6.2 (pathwise once-per-pair cap and reference-model standalone-mass ceiling, the firmest cost guarantee); Theorem S6.9, Corollary S6.10 (conditional upper bounds, with the Bad residual and the arrival-representation caveat S18) — the vanishing clauses are conditional accounting, consuming A9–A13, the rate conditions of Thm. S6.9(iii), the competence bound of Lemma S6.7, and changing time geometry (Rem. S6.12), never consequences of population growth alone —, Theorem S6.13, Remark S6.12. Scope: the federated layer is a protocol over the log service of convention (C3), not integrated into the single-user small-step relation (§S4). 

S8 Probabilistic Tools and Supplementary Results 

S8.1 Probabilistic framework, coupling, and the no-dispute baseline The adaptive-adversary framework. Fix an epoch. All protocol events (submissions, presentations, gate decisions, verdicts, promotions) form a discrete sequence; let (Fs)s≥0 be the filtration generated by all logged protocol events and all display events up to step s. The adversary’s strategy (whom to present the item to next, when to submit, re-enter, self-dispute, or stop) is predictable with respect to (Fs): its action at step s is Fs−1-measurable (convention (C2)). Two structural facts constrain the adversary’s adaptivity. First, an entry’s bytes are immutable once submitted (the log is append-only and the gate displays exact bytes), so within an entry the adversary adapts presentation and stopping only, never the inspected content. Second, because the adversary chooses whom to present the item to, the inspection parameter q must be read as a 

uniform lower bound over all honest identities and presentation contexts, the catch rate of the most fallible honest user the adversary can target, with flagged scrutiny only raising it (theorem S5.19, N11). 

Atomic gate events; log order. Gate events are atomic federated-wide, exactly as at the interface (Rule R2): a presentation and its disposition (an accepting decision, a rejecting decision, or abandonment) occupy a 

single position of the linearizable log (convention (C3)); no dialog is ever pending, and dispositions never resolve out of log order. The analysis reads each such position as up to three consecutive atoms : the rendering atom (item, tool, presenting identity, dispute flag, both provenance zones — recorded in the display transcript whether or not a decision follows, Rule R8(e)); if and only if the user records a decision, the header atom 

(the fact that a decision by this identity on these bytes at this gate is being appended, together with its verdict eligibility, a deterministic predicate of the prior log and the identity, Rule R13(iii)(a)–(e)); and the content atom (the decision z ∈ { accept , reject }). Abandonment appends no decision record and contributes no decision trial. Cache gates (Options O1–O2) are the one asymmetrically recorded class: both branches occupy a decision-pane position of the display transcript — rendering, header, and content atoms exactly as above, all inside the filtration, which is generated by display events as well as logged protocol events — while only the rejecting branch additionally appends a federation-log candidate record (Definition S4.6(iii)). We say a decision is audit-recorded if its atoms lie on the display transcript (every recorded gate decision, cache acceptances included) and federation-logged if it is appended to the federation log as a candidate  

> 128 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

decision (protocol-bearing decisions only). Trials at cache gates are indexed by their audit-recorded atoms: their occurrence is pre-outcome, never selected by the rejecting branch’s federation-log append. No protocol event, and no adversary action, interleaves between the atoms of one position; the discrete step index totally orders all atoms, and every trial sequence in this document is indexed by these log-ordered atoms: inspection and arrival trials by rendering atoms, decision and verdict trials by header atoms. In particular, every earlier trial’s content atom precedes every later trial’s occurrence atom. The adversary’s strategy, predictable with respect to the record-level filtration, remains predictable with respect to this refinement: the refinement inserts atoms inside single records and reveals nothing between records on which a strategy could act. 

Conditioning convention. The conditioning σ-algebra depends on the trial type. For an arrival or inspection-occurrence trial, Gi is generated by all atoms strictly prior to the trial’s rendering atom. For a 

decision- or verdict-content trial, Gi is generated by all atoms strictly prior to the trial’s header atom together with that header atom itself — the fact that a decision by this identity on these bytes at this gate is being recorded, and its deterministic eligibility facts (Rule R13(iii)) — but not the trial’s content atom. Thus the occurrence event Oi (a decision is recorded) is Gi-measurable in every application. Every behavioural lower bound ( qpre , qpan , pb, ρseat ) is a conditional bound given the corresponding Gi, further enlarged by the exogenous demand factor below (uniformly over demand realisations). Under the atomic reading above, these σ-algebras already contain the disposition of every earlier presentation — there are no latent or pending dispositions — so the occurrence, measurability, and independence conditions of Remark S8.4 hold at them directly. Where a behavioural parameter is stated at the disposition level ( qpre , pb), Lemma S8.1 transports it to the header-inclusive σ-algebra. For a per-attempt bound (Theorem S5.13), the conditioning σ-algebra is generated by all atoms up to and including the attempt’s anchor atom (Definition S5.1), the anchor preceding every decision creditable to the attempt; the trial σ-algebras above refine it, and the behavioural bounds, asserted uniformly over histories, hold under the anchor-conditional measure. 

Dispositions. An honest inspection presented at a gate has three possible dispositions, realized at that gate’s log position: an accepting decision, a rejecting decision, or abandonment. Only decisions are logged; abandonment leaves no record and has no protocol effect (Rule R13(i),(iii)). The conditional decision probabilities therefore satisfy pacc + prej ≤ 1, abandonment carrying the remaining mass, and every behavioural parameter ( q, pb, ρseat ) is stated over dispositions: abandonment counts as a miss inside 1 − q

and as a non-verdict inside ρseat (theorem S5.19). 

Lemma S8.1 (Decision-conditioned content bounds) . Consider a gate presentation with rendering-atom 

σ-algebra G and conditional disposition probabilities pacc , p rej given G, with pacc + prej ≤ 1 (abandonment carrying the remaining mass). On the event that a decision is recorded, and given G together with the header atom: (i) if prej ≥ q, the conditional probability of accept is at most 1 − q; (ii) if pacc ≥ pb, the conditional probability of reject is at most 1 − pb; (iii) symmetrically, let a designated sub-branch of the rejecting disposition (e.g. the effective qualifying rejection of Theorem S5.13(c)) have conditional probability 

peff ≤ prej given G; if peff ≥ q for some q ∈ (0 , 1] , then, on the event that a decision is recorded, the conditional probability of that sub-branch at the header atom is at least q: peff /(pacc + prej ) ≥ peff ≥ q,since pacc + prej ≤ 1. The header atom adds no randomness beyond the fact that a decision is recorded: verdict eligibility is a deterministic function of the prior log and the identity (Rule R13(iii)). (Part (i), the accept-side special case of (iii), is retained for reference; part (iii) serves qpre ’s lower-bound direction as used in Theorem S5.13 case (a); part (ii) serves pb; the verdict parameter qpan is defined at the header atom directly, Theorem S5.13(c), and needs no bridge.) Proof. The decision-recorded event has conditional probability pacc + prej given G, so the conditional accept probability given a recorded decision is pacc /(pacc + prej ) = 1 − prej /(pacc + prej ) ≤ 1 − prej ≤ 1 − q,using pacc + prej ≤ 1; (ii) is symmetric. Eligibility being measurable in G and the prior log, conditioning on it changes nothing.  

> 129 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

> u

> u

Realized shared-tier demand is the log-defined event of Definition S4.4: the first interpreter event at which u’s use of the pair fires a gate or dereferences a shared entry, per Definition S4.4’s exclusions (covered and cache gates are not demand events on either branch), occurring only while v has a shared-tier presence; its count is denoted nlog  

> v,t

Realized shared-tier demand is dominated pathwise by latent demand: every realized demander is a latent demander — for third parties by Definition S4.4 (a realized demand presupposes attempted use), for the owner by the owner-coupling convention (§S6.1) — so nlog  

> v,t

≤ nv,t pointwise; and by the required-approval clause (§S6.1), a reference-model coupling, any epoch event at which a user’s attempted use fires a gate realizes the user’s latent indicator, so every required confirmation in the epoch belongs to a latent demander, wherever the event falls; hence every ledger bound stated against nv,t or E[nv,t ] = M p v,t is an upper bound for the realized counts the rows charge. Which class a latent demander lands in (realized vs. reclassified) is protocol-dependent; the total 

per-pair required cost across both classes is nonetheless at most one per (user, pair) (INV-3), which is why the floor never consults the split. The arrival times of realized demand are protocol-endogenous, measurable in (Fs), revealing the corresponding indicator at that event; no independence between arrival times and the protocol is asserted, and A10 — both its demand clause (used for bucket attribution, Lemma S6.7) and its first-pair-decision clause (used only for the ¯Dv derivation) — is a hindsight cap on the realised arrival and decision schedules respectively, per item, read pathwise on completed epochs. The behavioural lower bounds q, pb, ρseat are asserted conditionally per the conditioning convention of this section, further enlarged by the entire demand factor: uniformly over demand realisations. This licenses the ledger’s two-stage argument (Lemma S6.7): the freeze rows bound conditional expectations given the demand coordinates, by Lemma S8.2 under the demand-conditional measure, whose hypotheses hold there by the uniform reading; the expectation over demand is taken last, by the tower property, and the pathwise domination nlog

≤ nv,t 

carries every realized-count row under the latent masses.

Remark S8.3 (Alternatives to pathwise coupling) . Readers preferring to avoid the sequential quantile construction may replace parts (i)–(ii) by exponential supermartingale bounds, at the cost of the pointwise dominations; the pathwise form is kept because the truncation and joint-event arguments of Theorem S5.13, Proposition S5.22(ii), and Lemma S5.6 consume domination, not merely tail bounds. 

Remark S8.4 ( σ-algebra form) . Lemma S8.2 holds verbatim with the pre-stopping-time σ-algebras Fτ −

The proof is unchanged: the conditional-quantile construction and the pointwise dominations use only these three properties. The applications take 

Gi per the conditioning convention of §S8.1: for arrival and inspection trials, all atoms strictly prior to the rendering atom; for decision and verdict trials, all prior atoms plus the trial’s own header atom (content atom excluded), so that Oi ∈ G i; both enlarged by the demand factor. All trials being log-ordered with earlier contents preceding later occurrence atoms, the three conditions hold at these σ-algebras directly.

Theorem S8.9 (No-dispute baseline: reach-dependent) . Without Rule R13, with rejections protocol-inert and a poisoned item shown to at most R honest users each erroneously accepting with probability ≤ ε

Comparison with the no-dispute baseline. The comparison isolates exactly what the panel and the gate-rejection-as-report rule buy. In Theorem S8.9 rejections are inert, so honest false acceptances accumulate with reach: the sample size is the attacker-chosen reach R, the bound requires W to grow linearly with εR 

merely to stay non-trivial, dragging the budget of Appendix S6 with it, and spreading the poison farther helps  

> 132 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

the attacker: the bound is not reach-free in the sense of INV-6. In Theorem S5.13, because every honest inspection carries its own report (Rule R13(i)), the no-dispute event shrinks with every additional honest viewer, and the false-accept mass that matters after a dispute is confined to a bounded jury of k − fs honest verdicts, a protocol constant the attacker cannot multiply, and one the demand-paced formation rule fills without any deadline the attacker could exploit. Reach drops out of the bound entirely, and spreading the poison cannot loosen the bound, while any single honest gate rejection freezes it. The dispute log additionally makes wrongful rejection a costly, attributable act, and panel eviction makes poisoned submission a costly, attributable act (Rule R13(v),(vii)), neither of which the inert-rejection baseline can express. 

S8.2 The blast-schedule promotion law 

This subsection derives, in closed form, the promotion probability of one simulated presentation schedule under one simulated model of honest behaviour. It is an analysis tool for the empirical evaluation, not a protocol guarantee: no rule, assumption, definition, or theorem of Appendices S2–S6 cites it, and nothing in this document depends on it. Its purpose is to turn the simulator’s output into a falsifiable prediction, so that agreement is evidence the implementation realises the rules of Appendix S4, and disagreement localises a defect in one of the two. 

Definition S8.10 (Blast schedule; independent honest coin) . The blast schedule is the non-adaptive pre-sentation strategy that (i) submits a poisoned item (Definition S4.10), with all endorsement and all demand at a single tool, (ii) spends every adversarial identity as a pre-dispute accepting endorser at that tool, and (iii) then presents the item, once each, to R honest identities in a fixed order (the reach , as in Theorem S8.9), continuing through every one of them whatever any of them decides. Four stipulations fix the simulated setting. (a) One window. All R presentations, and any panel the schedule induces, fall within a single finalisation window of one pipeline entry, with the freeze excluded from the window clock (Rule R13(iv)) and no epoch boundary closing the window early (convention (C4)). In particular the entry is open throughout, so no decision is banked (Definition S4.6(b), which requires no open entry) and no re-entry occurs: every weight-bearing decision is credited to that one entry. (b) Independent honest coin. Each of the R inspections is, independently of the others, a rejecting decision with probability q and an accepting decision with probability 1 − q, with no abstention. (c) Coupled reviewer quality. The same q governs pre-freeze gate inspections, panel verdicts, and post-resolution confirmations ( qpre = qpan = q); the decoupled reading, under which those three classes may carry different parameters, is Remark S8.17. (d) Seat weight and eligibility. Every identity carries attestation weight at least wseat , so a fresh decision on the item is verdict-eligible whenever a dispute is open (Rule R13(iii)(a)); every decision, the f

adversarial pre-dispute endorsements included, is authority-active (Definition S4.6(a)) and taken at a non-covered, non-cache gate, so every accepting decision is creditable and every rejecting decision takes effect; and identities are assigned to the Dpool pools round-robin. Independence across inspections is a property of this simulation only: no result of Appendices S4–S6 assumes it, and Theorem S5.13 explicitly does not (§S8.1, Remark S8.14). 

Proposition S8.11 (Blast promotion law) . Adopt Rules R12–R13 with the default bars of Rule R13(iv) (mrej = k + 1 − m, hence m + mrej − 1 = k), and the blast schedule and independent honest coin of Definition S8.10. Suppose 

N − f ≤ m, N − f ≤ R ≤ 2m,  

> 133 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

and suppose the deployment’s constants and pool assignment leave the credit caps of Definition S4.6(f) non-binding for promotion at every reach in that range (Step 1). Define 

S(J) := 

> min( J−m, m rej −1)

m − 1 + rr

 q

(J ≥ m), S(J) := 0 (J < m ).

Then the item is promoted to CC with probability πblast (R) = πord (R)+ πpan (R), where the two components — promotion with no dispute ever opened, and promotion after a dispute — are disjoint and equal 

πord (R) = (1 − q)R, πpan (R) = q (1 − q)R−1

> R−1

X

> J=m

S(J).

Neither expression contains k, f , fs, or Dpool .Proof. Step 0 (no adversarial verdict). Blast spends its f identities as pre-dispute accepting endorsers before any honest presentation. By byte-permanence of freshness (INV-1) each identity’s first logged decision on the bytes is its only weight-bearing inspection, and verdict eligibility requires freshness (Rule R13(iii)(b)); so when the freeze lands no adversarial identity is verdict-eligible, and every verdict is cast by an honest identity. Hence f and fs do not appear. 

Step 1 (no capacity, verdict-pool, or credit cap binds). Under the default bars m + mrej − 1 = k, so at most 

k verdicts are cast before a bar is reached (Lemma S5.7(ii)) and the capacity cap of Rule R13(iii)(e) is never the binding constraint. Two further caps are properties of the simulated population rather than consequences of the rules, and are disclosed as such. With the round-robin assignment of Definition S8.10(d), no pool contributes more than ⌈k/D pool ⌉ verdicts among any prefix of k arrivals, so the per-pool verdict cap of Rule R13(iii)(d) does not bind. Likewise the per-pool and total credit caps of Definition S4.6(f) truncate crediting at ⌈N/D pool ⌉ per pool and N + in total, and those caps are promotion-neutral in the sense stated there — N + ≥ N , and a credited set meeting N under the per-pool cap necessarily spans at least Dpool 

pools — provided enough accepting identities are available in each pool at the reach in question. That availability is a property of the constants and the round-robin assignment, verified for the deployment studied here and assumed in the statement; where it fails, the law does not apply and Dpool re-enters. Hence, under the stated hypothesis, k and Dpool do not appear. 

Step 2 (the ordinary component). Suppose no honest inspection rejects. No dispute opens; the accepting decisions are all authority-active (Definition S8.10(d)), so credited accepting endorsements number min( f +

R, N +) ≥ N , using R ≥ N −f with Step 1; and the entry promotes at its window’s close (Definition S4.6(c)). Conversely, a rejecting decision at any point in the window is a qualifying rejection that freezes promotion at every tool immediately (Rule R13(i)–(ii)), while finalisation occurs only at the window’s close, which by Definition S8.10(a) lies after all R presentations — so a single rejection, however late, denies the ordinary path. The ordinary component is therefore exactly the all-accept event, of probability (1 − q)R.

Step 3 (the panel component; conditioning). Condition on the first rejection falling at honest position j, an event of probability q(1 − q)j−1. The item is unpromoted and unfrozen at that moment, so that rejection is dispute-initiating (Rule R13(i)–(ii)). The remaining J := R − j inspections are, by Steps 0–1 and Definition S8.10(d), honest, fresh and seat-weighted; each is an eligible verdict for as long as the dispute is unresolved, and an ordinary accepting or rejecting confirmation thereafter. Steps 4–5 treat the two segments separately. 

Step 4 (no rejection after the m-th acceptance). Vindication resumes the interrupted entry but never itself promotes: promotion occurs only at the window’s close (Definition S4.6(c), Rule R13(iv)). A rejecting decision logged after vindication is therefore a fresh qualifying rejection against an unpromoted entry, which re-imposes the byte-level freeze (Rule R13(ii)). Verdict capacity is scoped to the dispute instance (INV-5) and so is available afresh in the second instance; what is not available is freshness, which is byte-permanent  

> 134 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT

(INV-1, Rule R13(iii)(b)): the second instance draws eligible jurors only from identities with no prior logged decision on the bytes, of whom at most m − 1 remain (Step 7). It therefore cannot vindicate; its only other outcomes are eviction, which ends the presence outright, and an unresolved instance, under which the window clock does not run out at all because the freeze is excluded from it (Rule R13(iv)). In either case the entry never reaches an unfrozen window end, so it cannot finalise. Under blast every one of the J post-freeze identities decides, so the qualifying sequences are exactly those whose tail after the m-th acceptance is unanimously accepting. 

Step 5 (the count, and the credited threshold). Fix J and index the post-freeze inspections 1, . . . , J . By Step 4, promotion requires: the m-th accepting verdict at position m + r for some r, with exactly r rejecting verdicts before it and r ≤ mrej − 1 (otherwise the eviction bar is reached first) and m + r ≤ J; and every one of the remaining J − m − r decisions accepting. The first m + r − 1 positions hold m − 1 acceptances and r rejections in any order,  m−1+ rr

 ways, and every such sequence carries r rejections and J − r

acceptances. The credited count is sufficient on every such sequence : the accepting decisions credited to the entry are the f pre-dispute endorsements, the j − 1 pre-freeze honest acceptances, the m accepting verdicts (credited on vindication, Rule R13(iv)) and the J − m − r post-resolution acceptances, all authority-active by Definition S8.10(d), totalling f + R − 1 − r; since r ≤ J − m = R − j − m and j ≥ 1, this is at least 

f + m ≥ N by the hypothesis N − f ≤ m, and Step 1 keeps the caps from truncating below N . Hence
