Step 7 (validity, and exhaustiveness). The hypothesis R ≤ 2m is what makes Step 4’s tail requirement exactly right. At the m-th accepting verdict, j + m + r inspections have occurred, so at most R − j − m − r ≤

R − m − 1 ≤ m − 1 < m remain: too few for any second dispute to reach its own m-bar, whatever its verdicts. The two cases — no rejection anywhere, and a first rejection at some position — are exhaustive and disjoint, so πblast = πord + πpan .

Remark S8.12 (Where each hypothesis is consumed) . The proposition’s hypotheses and the stipulations of Definition S8.10 are not interchangeable, and each is used once. R ≥ N − f is Step 2’s threshold. 

N − f ≤ m is Step 5’s, and it is not implied by the others, since N − f ≤ R ≤ 2m permits N − f = 2 m

— at which reach a counted panel sequence can credit as few as f + m < N acceptances and the formula would over-count; it is also what makes the plateau identity of Corollary S8.13(iv) meaningful, that identity evaluating πord at R = m, which requires m ≥ N − f . R ≤ 2m is Step 7. The credit-cap non-bindingness is Steps 1, 2 and 5. The one-window stipulation of Definition S8.10(a) is Step 2’s converse and Step 4’s re-freeze; the seat-weight and authority stipulations of Definition S8.10(d) are Step 3’s eligibility and Steps 2 and 5’s crediting. At the geometry studied here ( N = 10 , f = 2 , m = 20 , mrej = 12 ) the first two hold with slack, N − f = 8 ≤ min( m, R ) = min(20 , R ) for every R ≥ 8, and the binding hypothesis is R ≤ 40 .

Corollary S8.13 (Five structural consequences) . Under the hypotheses of Proposition S8.11: (i) (Structural zero and switch-on.) πpan (R) = 0 for every R ≤ m, and its first nonzero value is at 

R = m + 1 , where it equals exactly q(1 − q)m (since S(m) = 1 ). This is a support property, invariant under any change of measure. (ii) (No k, f , fs, Dpool .) As stated, by Steps 0–1. 

> 135

PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

(iii) (mrej enters late.) The cap in S(J) first differs from J − m at J = m + mrej , and πpan (R) reaches 

J = R − 1; hence πpan is independent of mrej for all R ≤ m + mrej and first depends on it at 

R = m + mrej + 1 , where the difference between the capped and uncapped forms is exactly the single 

r = mrej term at J = m + mrej .(iv) (Plateau identity.) πblast (m) = πblast (m + 1) = (1 − q)m exactly, since (1 − q)m+1 + q(1 − q)m =(1 − q)m. The switch-on is therefore invisible in the total rate and visible only in the decomposition — which is why the figure that exhibits it plots the two components separately rather than their sum. (v) (Peak and fall.) S(J) is constant in J for J ≥ m + mrej − 1, so P 

> J

S(J) grows linearly in R while 

(1 − q)R−1 decays geometrically: πpan rises, attains an interior maximum, and falls. Remark S8.14 ( πpan is not the theorem’s panel term) . πpan (R) and the panel term of Batt (Remark S5.17) are different objects and must not be conflated. The theorem’s term is a reach-free upper bound over all adaptive presentation, stopping and re-entry strategies, holding under a conditional lower bound on honest behaviour alone and assuming no independence — the exact binomial tail arises there from the coupling of Lemma S8.2, not from an independence hypothesis; πpan (R) is the exact realised probability of one non-adaptive schedule under the independence of Definition S8.10(b). A large gap between them is expected and is not evidence that the bound is loose: tightness is a separate empirical question, addressed by strategies that reserve seat-eligible identities and stop at the first bar reached, which empirically attain the flat panel term — precisely because they can stop, while blast cannot, which is why blast’s realised rate peaks and falls where the theorem’s term is constant. Two schedules, one mechanism, opposite consequences, both predicted. 

Remark S8.15 (Validity, and the direction of failure beyond it) . The law holds in the single-dispute regime 

R ≤ 2m, and Step 7 is where that hypothesis is consumed. Beyond it a post-vindication rejection can open a second dispute among still-unpresented, still-fresh jurors, whose own m-bar is now reachable; the single-dispute form therefore under-counts , and the direction of failure is fixed in advance rather than discovered. Consequently no reading of the law beyond 2m is a prediction: past that reach the schedule’s rate is a measured quantity and the law is not a model of it. Nothing about the failure bears on any bound: 

Batt is reach-free (INV-6) and continues to hold at every reach, and INV-6 disclaims monotonicity of the 

realised rate precisely so that a plateau or a rise in a measured sweep is inside the theory rather than against it. 

Commentary (non-normative; the validity boundary, measured). At the deployed geometry ( q = 0 .6, N = 10 ,

f = 2 , k = 31 , m = 20 , mrej = 12 , so 2m = 40 ) the boundary of Remark S8.15 is measured, not merely asserted. At the last in-validity reach the law gives πpan (40) = 1 .049 × 10 −5 against 9 promotions in 10 6

trials. At R = 64 the same schedule yields 8 promotions in 10 6 trials ( 8.0 × 10 −6, Clopper–Pearson 95% [3 .45 , 15 .76] × 10 −6) against an extrapolation of 1.057 × 10 −14 — a factor 7.57 × 10 8, i.e. 8.88 orders of magnitude above it, in the disclosed under-counting direction. At equal trial count R = 40 and R = 64 are statistically indistinguishable ( z = 0 .243 ), so the realised rate plateaus rather than continuing its geometric decay. The plateau does not persist: at R = 100 the rate is bounded above by 3.69 × 10 −6, a bound that overlaps R = 64 ’s lower confidence limit, so the claim is no rise , not a fall . Every measured cell of this sweep lies below Batt = 8 .957 × 10 −3: the boundary cell R = 40 by 3.00 decades and every cell beyond it by at least 3.05 . The generating experiment gates on that conformance, at every cell of every sweep, before writing its artifact. Inside the validity window the margin is far smaller — about 0.58 decades at the panel-path peak 

R = 32 , where the measured rate is 2.34 × 10 −3 — which is where the ceiling is informative rather than slack. No mechanism is proposed for the plateau’s magnitude: beyond 2m the sweep is measured, not modelled .

Remark S8.16 (Testability of the mrej entry) . Corollary S8.13(iii) is sharply testable only for small mrej .The entering term is weighed against the partial sum PR−1 

> J=m

S(J) that πpan carries at the entry reach, and that sum grows with mrej far faster than the single entering term does, so the relative effect shrinks sharply as the eviction bar rises. The claim is therefore made where it is resolvable — at small mrej , for two values of m — while for large mrej the testable statement is agreement with the uncapped form below the entry 

136 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

reach. Two of the three confirmations available are support properties rather than hypothesis tests: below the entry reach the capped and uncapped forms are equal , so two panel widths sharing one coin stream must produce byte-identical counts, which cannot be passed by luck. 

Commentary (non-normative; the mrej entry, measured). At m = 20 and q = 0 .2: at mrej = 4 , entry reach 

R = 25 , the entering term is 34 .59 against a partial sum of 112 .5, a +30 .75% effect; at mrej = 12 , entry reach 

R = 33 , it is 8.411 against 2003 .05 , a +0 .42% effect. The corresponding rates are πpan (25) = 0 .10625 capped against 0.13892 uncapped, and πpan (33) = 0 .31740 against 0.31873 . Reading a five-sigma requirement as five standard errors of the difference between two arms of K trials each, with the variance evaluated at the capped rate — K = 25 · 2p(1 − p)/∆2 — those effects need 4.5 × 10 3 and 6.1 × 10 6 trials per arm respectively. The prediction is accordingly claimed at mrej = 4 , where it is confirmed three ways: byte-identical counts across two panel widths sharing one coin stream at every reach below the entry; a +13 .6σ divergence at the entry reach itself; and two πpan profiles whose maxima over the sampled reach grid fall where the law places them, 

R = 26 with 4,715 observed against 4,705 predicted and R = 30 with 14 ,030 against 14 ,001 . One scope note on the maxima: the mrej =4 profile’s exact maximum lies at R = 27 , a reach that grid does not sample, so 

R = 26 is a maximum over the sampled grid and not the argmax of πpan ; the two differ by 2.2% in value. 

Remark S8.17 (Decoupled reviewer quality) . Definition S8.10(c) couples three decision classes that the protocol distinguishes: pre-freeze gate inspections, panel verdicts, and post-resolution confirmations. Under a decoupled reading the first and third are ordinary gate inspections, governed by qpre , while only the second are verdicts, governed by qpan ; Steps 0–5 then go through with 

πord (R) = (1 − qpre )R,

(1 − qpan )m(1 − qpre )J−m−r,

the m + r verdicts charged at qpan and the unanimous tail of J − m − r post-resolution confirmations at qpre . But Step 6’s collapse fails — the two geometric factors no longer share a base — and the panel component remains the double sum PR−mj=1 qpre (1 − qpre )j−1P[promotion | R − j]. Setting qpre = qpan = q

recovers Proposition S8.11 exactly, which is why the closed form is specific to the coupled reading and why the figures that exhibit the law run coupled reviewer quality while the decoupled figures report ceilings only. A deployment or simulator adopting the decoupled reading must additionally fix, and disclose, which parameter governs the post-resolution tail: assigning it to qpan instead — a defensible modelling choice, since those inspections occur under the dispute flag — replaces (1 − qpre )J−m−r by (1 − qpan )J−m−r and yields qpre (1 − qpre )j−1(1 − qpan )J Sqpan (J) with Sqpan the sum S at ratio qpan /(1 − qpan ). The two forms differ except in the coupled case, and no result of this document distinguishes them. 

Commentary (non-normative; epistemic status of the blast law). Three things this law is not. It is not a safety claim: the safety statement is Theorem S5.13, which assumes no independence and quantifies over all adaptive schedules, and this law’s exponential decay would not survive an adversary that stops at the first rejection — such an adversary is confined to the ordinary path and is measured separately. It is not a tightness claim (Remark S8.14). And it is not an assumption of anything: no rule reads it, no proof cites it, and the protocol’s behaviour is identical whether or not it holds; it is accordingly absent from the assumptions ledger of §S8.6. What it is, is the instrument that makes the simulator falsifiable. Every one of its five consequences is a prediction the implementation could violate, three of them at the level of support rather than of a tail probability — the structural zero, the k-independence, and the mrej -independence below the entry reach are all byte-identity properties that cannot be passed by luck. Its own derivation is verified against two independent oracles: direct enumeration of every verdict sequence at small (m, m rej ), applying the decision rule of Step 4 without reference to the closed form; and an uncollapsed exact-rational sum over the rejecter’s position, so that an algebra error in Step 6 cannot cancel. These checks share an interpretive premise: Step 4 is a reading of Rule R13, and the enumeration oracle shares that reading with the claim it checks, so the two are not independent on that point. Unlike the single-user core 137 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

(§S9), none of this is machine-checked. An implementation that disagreed with the law would be either a simulator defect or a misreading of Appendix S4, and the law’s role is to make the difference cheap to find.

S8.4 Knowledge-based formulation of Reasoning Integrity 

Theorem S3.25 is trace-wise per coin sequence. It induces the following statement in the knowledge-based style of gradual release Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007), recorded to dispel the classical worry that conditioning on release events can itself be secret-dependent. 

Proposition S8.20 (Gradual release) . Fix the strategies P and RE , the slot skeleton, a coin sequence ω,and a typed stream (A6; in the federated setting also a federation log, A8). By Theorem S3.25, the entire behaviour of a run of horizon n from a sorted configuration (its Protected-LLM operations, tool invocations, reply stream, and ungated sink events) is a function F (c≈ 

> 0

, DT , ω, n ) of the initial matched-configuration class (Definition S3.6), the run’s display transcript (the run suffix of Definition S3.8), and the coins, with no residual dependence on MU. Consequently, for any observer who knows these fixed inputs and the initial matched class, and any prior over untrusted-store contents consistent with that class, the posterior after observing the run to horizon n equals the prior conditioned on consistency with DT alone: knowledge about untrusted memory increases only at display events, never through undisplayed behaviour. Proof. By Theorem S3.25, any two untrusted-store contents consistent with the same DT (within the fixed initial matched class, matched coins, coupled typed stream and slot skeleton, conventions (C1)/(C9)) yield identical behaviour, so the likelihood of the observed behaviour is constant across such contents; Bayes’ rule then collapses the posterior to the prior restricted to DT -consistency. The observer here is granted ω and the typed stream, strictly more than any real attacker knows. The statement is claimed for this strong observer only: for an observer ignorant of ω, conditioning on the transcript can correlate the hidden store with the coins, and behaviour may then reveal information about ω that, jointly with the transcript, reveals more about untrusted memory than the transcript alone; marginalising over ω therefore does not yield the weaker-observer statement in general. Deployments wanting the weaker-observer form may include the public coin outcomes in the release history, whereupon this proposition applies verbatim with ω read as released. 

S8.5 Zipf case study for the budget 

This subsection instantiates the saturation cost bound (Theorem S6.9) under a Zipf rank-popularity law, as a worked example; Theorem S6.9 needs only sublinear distinct-count growth (with A10). (2010); the clean rate below holds in the top-heavy regime α > 1. S9 Machine-Checked Verification in Lean 4 

We mechanize the single-user core of PerMIT in Lean 4 (toolchain leanprover/lean4:v4.31.0 ; Mathlib 

v4.31.0 , revision fabf563a ). The development defines an executable interpreter for the memory interface and proves Theorems S3.21 and S3.25 over it for arbitrary Protected-LLM strategies, retrievers, attackers, and horizons, within the deployment, input, and starting-state conditions stated below. Building the development is the verification: Lean’s kernel checks every theorem, and every executable scenario is a build-time assertion. 

Scope. The mechanized surface is the single-user interface without run-level batched iteration: tool invocation, write P , recall , fill , crystallize , confirm (tool and reasoning targets), retrieve , display , evict , and the typed and paste channels. The batch-confirmation surface of Rule R9 is mechanized as a standalone layer. Paper arguments alone cover the integration of map into the run-level interpreter, durable endorsement on aborted invocations (delta 6), arbitrary mid-run matched pairs, the index-oracle reduction (delta 2), Options O1–O2 beyond the gate level and Option O3, and the federated tier with every probabilistic and budget result. 

Model. The interpreter execOp is a total function with one arm per operation, so every invocation has defined behavior (Rule R10(b)). Its state IState represents the extended configuration of Definition S2.6, subject to the modeling deltas below; the display transcript, sensitive-sink log, and operation log are ghost state. Four modeling choices carry the proofs. • Replies. The inductive type Reply (acknowledgment, handle, failure, decision, bytes) is the reply grammar of Rule R10; no other reply can be constructed. Bytes are returned only by recall of a non-U

block and by trusted retrieval. • Two-sorted identifiers. Endorsed-sort identifiers ( BlockId.e ) are allocated only by coupled events: typed input, the agent’s writes, and accepted confirmations. Untrusted-sort identifiers ( BlockId.u )are allocated by attacker-dependent events: the initial untrusted store, paste, tool-output capture, and crystallization. Every endorsement writes a fresh copy at the next endorsed-sort identifier. • Views. The Protected-LLM view Obs is computed from the endorsed store alone ( memEOf ), with the decision history, typed input, and handle count; the retriever likewise receives only the endorsed store. • Actors. The Protected LLM is an arbitrary function of its emitted operations, reply stream, view, and coins ( PStrat ). The attacker supplies the initial untrusted store, every tool output (count and content), every paste event, and the Quarantined Reader’s candidates and summaries. The user supplies the typed stream and the decision and selection at each confirmation. 

Checked statements. Table S5 gives the correspondence. Action Integrity is a projection of an inductive invariant, Inv , established initially and preserved by every transition. It is proved from the empty endorsed store and from any state satisfying Inv . A precedence corollary places each endorsing confirmation in the transcript that exists when the sink event is recorded. Reasoning Integrity is a lockstep simulation over MatchW , which equates everything the Protected LLM can observe and everything the transcript records, while leaving untrusted stores and hidden handle resolutions free. It is proved from empty endorsed stores and from any shared endorsed seed with independently chosen untrusted stores. Its conclusions exceed what Theorem S3.25 requires: equal transcripts force equal raw sink logs, operation sequences, reply streams, and final views. Proposition S3.27 is witnessed by computation. A taint-gating baseline, with data taint carried inside the model, yields equal (empty) transcripts but different ungated sink projections. PerMIT’s interpreter under the analogous strategy is attack-invariant. Eight scenarios check, by computation:

Trusted base. Lean’s kernel checks every theorem, and the build fails on any failed proof or false #guard .An omitted proof ( sorry ) produces only a warning, so completeness rests on the #print axioms report, reproduced verbatim in the artifact’s README from a clean build. It shows that the top-level theorems, and hence every lemma they use, depend only on propext and Quot.sound , and none on sorryAx . The 

#guard scenarios additionally trust Lean’s evaluator. The correspondence between the mechanized model and the paper’s semantics is not machine-checked; Table S5 and the deltas below argue it. 

Modeling deltas. None is silent. Deltas 1 and 3 strengthen the checked statements. (1) Reader. The Quarantined Reader is folded into the attacker, which chooses the reader’s candidates and summaries directly. (2) Index oracles. User decisions and selections, and attacker outputs, are functions of event ordinals. Any run of a reactive user or attacker is the run of the oracle equal to its realized sequence. In Theorem S3.25, 

145 PerMIT: Securing Persistent Memory for LLM Agents PREPRINT 

both runs share the user oracle, and transcript equality pins every decision a display-reactive user makes (Supplement, Proposition S3.29; a paper argument). (3) Channels. Compared runs share only the slot skeleton and the typed stream; each attacker chooses paste presence and content independently. (4) Starting states. Theorem S3.21 is proved from the empty endorsed store and from any state satisfying 

Inv . Theorem S3.25 is proved from empty or shared endorsed seeds, the canonical matched class of Definition S3.5. Arbitrary mid-run matched pairs are covered by the paper proof. (5) Collection handles. Without run-level map , a handle to a multi-element capture is confirmed at a direct call by selecting one displayed element, so at most one displayed value flows per accepting decision. (6) Aborted invocations. The mechanized interpreter writes endorsed copies only when an invocation executes. Rule R2 also writes durable copies for the accepted positions of an aborted invocation; that case is covered by the paper proof only. (7) Decision history. The mechanized history also records the selection index and marks null-resolution aborts, so the mechanized view extends the paper’s h. The operation semantics do not inspect these extra fields; both are transcript-recorded and therefore agree in the matched executions used by the proof. (8) Selections. An out-of-range selection clamps to the first candidate; the flowed bytes remain the displayed bytes. (9) Representation. One typed event is one T-block; bytes are lists of naturals; provenance marks are subsumed by labels and ghost logs. Inv checks endorsement against the transcript, without the sanitized-history record of Definition S3.3(ii). (10) Outside the interpreter. Options O1–O2 are checked at the gate level only. The covered-gate and reasoning-coverage clauses of Rule R13(i) are federated accounting conventions, not transitions. 

Specification corrections. Mechanization corrected the specification in three places, each adopted in the paper. • Endorsement must write a fresh copy at an operation-determined identifier. Relabeling the untrusted source in place would expose an attacker-dependent identifier in the endorsed store and break the matched-pair invariant; this is why identifiers are two-sorted. • Channel events must be acknowledged to the channel, not to the Protected LLM. Otherwise an uncoupled paste would emit a visible acknowledgment, and Theorem S3.25 would fail. • The fast-path case analysis of Lemma S3.13 is disjunctive: typed and endorsed matches may co-occur, and processArgs_spec proves exhaustiveness without exclusivity. 

Artifact. The Lean artifact is available upon request and will be released with the code when the paper is published. The development pins the toolchain ( lean-toolchain ) and all dependencies (lake-manifest.json ). Running lake exe cache get && lake build reproduces the verification. The artifact includes CORRESPONDENCE.md (Table S5), a README with the verbatim axiom report, the log of a clean build, and a SHA-256 manifest of the sources.
